## intake-2116#record — DeepInfer: DeepSeek's production inference framework for DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash (DSpark confidence-scheduled verification, SWA bounded replay, async scheduler, EPLB, FP4 KV)

- **URL**: https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepInfer
- **Verification**: dive-verified

- **Disposition**: integrated

### Claims (as verified)

0. DSpark verify-length scheduling maximizes expected tokens per second: it greedily adds the next-highest cumulative acceptance value across all (request, draft position) pairs, tracks the best value of (num_seqs + sum of selected values) / (profiled forward ms at the graph-size bucket + slope_ms*graph_size + sampler median ms), and cuts the batch to that total query-token count; selection is automatically a per-request prefix (csrc/engine/mtp_varlen.hpp, MTPVarlen::schedule). Drafter cost is constant in the choice and is not modelled.
   - *unaffected*: Release-scope claim not examined by D1.
   - anchor `csrc/engine/mtp_varlen.hpp:L243-L244 @ 774ce24` @ 774ce244b87c: "const double time_ms = baseline_ms + slope_ms * graph_size + sampler_ms; const double cur_throughput = cur_expected / time_ms;"
1. Expected acceptance is the cumulative product of per-position confidence-head outputs: the head is sigmoid(linear([pre-norm hidden, Markov embedding]) / T_i) with a per-position temperature knob, treated as a conditional per-token acceptance probability; the CPU multiplies them in process_MTPInfo and the device in a TileLang kernel. The only calibration instrument in the repo is batch_confidence_error (sum of |realized accept_prob - confidence| over verified rows), which reaches only the stats logger and is not consumed by the scheduler.
   - *narrowed*: Stage-1 key_claims[1] (DSpark varlen verification) is confirmed in code but narrowed: the curve is a latency-vs-graph-size curve of the main forward profiled at startup plus online correction; objective is expected tokens per second; cumulative product of per-position confidences; draft depth is fixed at 5 and only verify length varies; feature is default-off (see D1 key_claims 0-3).

2. The "profiled hardware throughput curve" is a startup-profiled LATENCY curve (ms per CUDA-graph size) of the main-model decode forward: 1 warm-up and 5 timed replays per graph size, middle-3 trimmed mean, EP-group average, random MoE gating forced on during profiling; decode_perf_cache.py is only its 1 h disk cache. Two online estimators correct it: a median-of-medians of (forward_ms - baseline)/graph_size over the last 5000 steps (>=10 samples per graph size) added as slope*graph_size, and a 9-step median of sampler time keyed by num_seqs.
   - *unaffected*: SWA bounded replay not examined by D1 (only the spec-decode rollback ring slack, which is a different mechanism).

3. Split of work: CPU fixes the total query-token budget from the N-2 round cumulative rates; the per-request lengths come from the N-1 round rates (CPU in the synchronous path, a GPU rank-counting kernel with fused remap scatter in the zero-overhead async path, where fresh requests get cumulative rate 0 and only receive leftover budget). Verify length differs per request and step in [1, 1+nextn]; draft depth does not (the block drafter always drafts 5 positions and the unverified tail is discarded). MTP_VARLEN is default off; ZERO_OVERHEAD_SCHEDULING is ON by default for NORMAL and COMPLETION engines (EngineArgs.__post_init__ sets it from EngineType.default_zero_overhead_scheduling although its EnvVar default is false), so the async N-1 path is the default whenever varlen is enabled.
   - *narrowed*: Stage-1 key_claims[3] speculative half: CPU fixes the total budget from N-2 rates and the device recomputes the split from N-1 rates only in the opt-in zero-overhead path; fresh requests get cumulative rate 0 (D1 key_claim 3).

9. Decoder bounded replay (compact mode) drops all query rows older than the last KEEP_TOKENS from decoder layers 20-39 (their projections, MoE, SWA KV), while layer 20 still computes its shared compressed KV and index K for every token from the full hidden. It is approximate: SWA visibility is window INTERSECT the last-KEEP rectangle, and the Q&A says exactness would need the last 2,560 tokens (20 layers x 128). The result is used for decode only and is never put in the prefix cache. First-token logits also pass through this approximate path.

13. KV byte formats verified in code: SWA and (FP4-off) main KV = 512 e4m3 + 16 ue8m0 per-32 scales = 528 B/token; FP4 main KV = 512 e2m1 + 32 e4m3 per-16 scales = 288 B/token (no global scale); indexer KV = 128 e2m1 + 4 ue8m0 = 68 B/token. 17. Zero-overhead scheduling (ZOS, default on for NORMAL engines) is a one-step-lag pipeline in worker.execute_model_step: CPU schedules step N+1 and launches its forward, THEN post-processes step N (sample_end_evt.synchronize), THEN samples N+1. Prompt steps and ZOS-off post-process in place. Finished/stop tokens are seen one step late: the extra forward for a finished sequence is wasted and its outputs dropped (process_outputs skips is_finished and preempted-in-flight seqs). Remaining sync points per decode step: (a) sample_end_evt.synchronize of the previous step; (b) a D2H event sync (d2h_evt) only when MTP-varlen is on, to read corrected seqlens_q before sampling; (c) pre_sampler_sync_fn runs CPU work (deferred release, KV-save, copy_state_event.synchronize) between sample launch and end-event record; (d) flush_inflight_reqs on drain. Batch order/length correction for the lag is done on device (compute_seqlens_k_zos; compute_seqlens_q_by_confidence_rates).
   - anchor `deepinfer/worker/worker.py:L182 @ 774ce24` @ deepseek-ai/DeepInfer @ 774ce244b87cc0eb8d4b94ab9f5de2ab22049f01: "# 4. prompt 步或配置 non-ZOS 时当场 post process；ZOS decode 步留到下一步（与 forward 重叠）"
18. Chunked prefill and P/D mixing inside one engine: prefill and decode are SEPARATE steps (prompt_run flag), never mixed in one batch. A NORMAL engine decides per step by DP-wide all-reduce vote: every prefill_attempt_interval (default 70) decode steps if ALL ranks have waiting prefill (ALL point), every prefill_any_attempt_interval (default 100) if ANY rank does (ANY point), gated by KV usage <= 0.9 watermark; a prompt step zeroes decode_step. Prefill is FIFO, packs sequences until max_num_batched_tokens (cp_align-rounded), chunked prefill truncates the head sequence to the remaining token budget and breaks after one chunked seq in NORMAL engines; chunk boundaries also respect multimodal image spans and chunk_checkpoints. chunked_prefill defaults False in EngineArgs.

21. Admission/preemption (decode): admit prompts from waiting only while running+scheduled seqs < max_num_seqs (max_num_prompts), prefill_max_sum_seqlen_k sum-K cap (default unlimited), prompt_limit, and block_manager (+state_manager) can_allocate (LATER -> stop, NEVER -> ignore). Decode picks LCFS: running sorted by output length, running sorted by output length DESCENDING and scheduled from the shortest-output end (running[r-1]) first; when no free KV block, victims are taken from the other end (running[l]), i.e. the MOST-progressed (longest-output) sequences and are either stopped (FINISHED_PREEMPTED, default stop_preempted_request=True) or reset for recompute and pushed to the head of waiting; structured-output seqs cannot be recomputed. COMPLETION engines require stop_preempted_request. Under ZOS a seq preempted while in flight has its in-flight step output discarded.
   - *overturned*: Second reader (2026-10-20) answered no; main verified scheduler.hpp:L624-L633: running is sorted by output length descending, so running[l] victims are the MOST-progressed (longest-output) sequences, not the least. The victim clause of the dive claim was wrong and is corrected in place; admission rules unaffected.

24. HALF-LAYER-SHIFTED MIX CHAIN INCLUDING THE DSPARK/MTP BLOCKS (DS41-B4). Every sub-layer (attention and FFN) applies the pre-mix produced by the PREVIOUS sub-layer; the first sub-layer of any stack uses a one-hot-on-stream-0 mix. The 3 DSpark blocks are ordinary full HC blocks built by the same MoeDecoderBlockHC builder (each owns its hc_attn/hc_ffn fn/scale/base set), start from the embedding replicated over 4 streams with the identity pre-mix, pass pre_layer_mix block to block, and collapse at the end with the last block's FFN mix; an hc_head parameter set exists only for the final block and only when hc_shifted is false. DSpark target features are the MEAN over the 4 streams of the layer input (after engram, before attention-pre) at layers 37/38/39. Our build_hc_mean at deepseek41.cpp:1266 matches.

25. LAYER-20 'MISSING attn_compressor_gate' IS BY CONSTRUCTION (DS41-B12b). For compress ratio 1 a group holds a single token, so the learned softmax over the group is identically 1: DeepInfer allocates the Gate projection only when ratio != 1, and the ratio-1 compressor is norm(A.x) in bf16 with no pooling, no fp32 and no state cache. The official Compressor says the same ('one token per group: nothing to pool, so no gate'). Layers 21-39 do not compress at all; they read layer 20's per-token latent plane (kv_source_layers 2/8/14/20).

26. COMPRESSION GEOMETRY IS RATIO 1 / 2 ONLY, NON-OVERLAPPING, APE-FREE (DS41-B12c). DeepInfer's model metadata rejects any ratio outside {0,1,2} ('C4 and C128 have been removed'), rejects compress_overlap, and rejects APE ('use an APE-free checkpoint'). So the DSv4 ratio-4 overlapped pooling (2*ratio rows, doubled projection) and APE paths do not exist for V4.1; groups are exactly `ratio` consecutive tokens. Resulting cache geometry: three ratio-2 kv-source planes (layers 2, 8, 14) plus one ratio-1 plane (layer 20) shared by layers 20-39. DeepInfer places its prefill/YOCO boundary at the first ratio-1 layer (n_prefill_layer = 20).

29. ENGRAM HASH RECIPE (PARTIAL; DS41-B8). DeepInfer's hash INPUTS are bit-for-bit the official recipe: per-layer numpy default_rng(10007*layer_id) draws max_ngram_size int64 multipliers in [0, (int64max // compressed_vocab)//2) then *2+1; one prime per (n-gram order 2..4, head 0..7) found with nextprime from engram_vocab_size-1 with a global never-reuse set; offsets are the cumulative prime sizes; tokens go through the compressed-vocab map (pad id 2) with lookback cut at sequence start and at image sentinels. The hash kernel body itself (tile_kernels.engram.engram_hash: the XOR/modulo arithmetic) is not in the repo, so the XOR-rolling / mod-prime / +offset arithmetic stays sourced to the official engram.py, which is also what the lane's B8 text already states.

30. ENGRAM TABLE LAYOUT AND RESIDENCY (DS41-B3/B7c/B9). Per engram layer the table is rows = sum of the 24 head prime sizes (order-major, head-minor), padded to a multiple of lcm(256, engram_parallel_size), 256 columns per row; the fp8 variant is e4m3 [rows,256] plus ue8m0 scales per 32 columns = 8 scale bytes per row (asserted groups of 32, packed 4 per int32), i.e. the same 256+8 = 264 information bytes per row as our GGUF e4m3_e8m0_32_row264, stored as two arrays instead of interleaved. Default placement is HOST DRAM: parameters materialise on 'cpu' unless DEEPINFER_ENGRAM_FORCE_GPU_STORAGE (forced only on Ascend), the EngramBuffer is allocated with use_cpu_rdma_storage = not FORCE_GPU_STORAGE, the table is written into that registered host storage and the parameter copy is released, and rows are then fetched one-sided over RDMA from storage sharded across the engram-parallel group. So the vendor keeps the ~189 GiB of tables fully DRAM-resident with a row gather and never streams them from disk; on a single host the RDMA hop is moot and the analogue is a local DRAM gather. Page/pin details live in DeepEP (not vendored).

31. ENGRAM FUSION SEMANTICS (parity). One fused projection maps the 24 gathered rows to hc_mult keys plus one shared value ([hc+1, n_embd]); gate = sigmoid(copysign(sqrt(max(|dot|,1e-6)), dot)) with dot the per-(token,stream) RMS-normalised key.h product scaled by dim^-0.5 and weight = q_weight*k_weight pre-multiplied; the value is added to all 4 streams. It is injected as a trunk intervention AFTER the previous layer's hc_post and BEFORE this layer's attention pre-mix (layers 1 and 14), and the DSpark feature mean is taken after it. Identical to the official Engram.forward and to our build_engram (deepseek41.cpp:~440-520). Whole-forward fetch: hashes are computed once before the layer loop and one fetch is issued for ALL engram layers with a per-layer completion hook, so row reads for layer 14 can overlap layers 0-13.

32. SPARSE ATTENTION IS INDEX-GATHER AT EVERY STAGE IN THE VENDOR ENGINE (answers DS41-C123/T2 'does anything make it gather sparsely'). (a) Final attention: decode calls FlashMLA flash_mla_with_kvcache with two paged pools (SWA cache + compressed cache) and per-token physical-slot index lists (indices_in_kvcache / extra_indices_in_kvcache), prefill concatenates SWA and compressed index lists and calls flash_mla_sparse_fwd; no dense -inf mask and no concatenation of the compressed plane exists. The official inference/kernel.py sparse_attn is also a gather kernel over topk_idxs. (b) The candidate stage is a real compute saver: layer 20 scores all rows densely, reduces to block maxima (block 8, newest block pinned, top 2048 blocks), and the later index layers (24/28/32/36) compute indexer logits ONLY over the candidate tokens (deep_gemm.fp8_fp4_sparse_mqa_logits / fp8_fp4_paged_sparse_mqa_logits), then top-512. (c) In prefill the gathered KV buffer of a KV-source layer is reused by all consumers in its sharing segment. Our port masks densely (deepseek41.cpp:1091-1097 concat + top_k_mask) and scores indexer keys densely even after the candidate source (:854 dense lid_kq precedes the candidate mask add at :875), so both the memory traffic and the indexer FLOPs of the candidate stage are not realised on CPU.

33. EPLB PHASE 1 - REPLICA ALLOCATION (policy.py:L73-L123). Inputs are per-step, per-layer, per-logical-expert token counts over a window (not an average). Starting from one replica per expert, each of num_redundant rounds adds one replica to the expert j that minimises, summed over window steps, the maximum per-replica load max_e(load_e/count_e) after adding it (score_added = load/(count+1); ties broken by adding index*1e-4 to the counts). Layers are independent. The objective is therefore the sum over steps of the worst per-replica load, which targets stragglers, not mean balance. Initial layout before the first rebalance copies experts 0..k-1 (index % n_routed), not hot ones.

34. EPLB PHASE 2 - GREEDY PLACEMENT ONTO RANKS (policy.py:L12-L70, pinned by tests). Each physical replica has per-step load load_e,step/count_e. Replicas are ordered by total per-replica load descending (stable) and placed one at a time onto the not-full rank (capacity = physical experts per rank, equal for all ranks) that minimises sum_over_steps max(peak_step, rank_load_step + this replica's load_step); ties go to the smallest rank id. Because it scores the per-step max over ranks it prefers ranks whose existing load is complementary in time, i.e. it uses co-activation across the window; the hand-built unit cases (tests/models/test_eplb_policy.py:L97-L117) fix the tie-break and the complementarity behaviour. Complexity O(num_physical * ranks * window) per layer.

35. EPLB RUNTIME IS OPT-IN AND EP-ONLY. Counts come from the dispatch/mega-MoE kernels' cumulative per-local-expert receive stats, are kept in a ring of window steps (default window 100), all-reduced over the EP group into logical-expert counts, and fed to the policy every DEEPINFER_EXPERT_REARRANGEMENT_STEP_INTERVAL steps (default 0 = disabled; window must be <= interval; the first rearrangement fires after interval/4 steps to fix cold-start skew; in the combined 'normal' engine prefill steps are skipped unless rearrange-after-prefill is set). Total physical experts are rounded up so they divide ep_size (DEEPINFER_OVERRIDE_NUM_EXTRA_EXPERTS is a TOTAL, not per-GPU). Weights are moved in place per layer (NVLink symm_mem mirror intranode, batched P2P otherwise; only slots whose expert changed are overwritten, the NVLink path first mirrors each whole layer into symmetric memory). With ep_size <= 1 the balancer does nothing. The drafter's 128-expert MoE layers form their own layout group in the same balancer.

37. WEIGHT FORMATS (seed claim 6, expert part). Routed experts: FP4 E2M1 packed 2/byte as int8 [n_experts, n, k/2] with UE8M0 scales per 32 along K packed 4 per int32 (== MXFP4 block layout), fused gate_up + down, activations dynamically FP8 E4M3 per-32, grouped GEMM fp8xfp4 (bf16/fp8 fallbacks), swiglu clamp passed as activation_clamp. Everything dense stays 8-bit or wider: attention Wq/Wkv/Wv/out_proj and shared experts use FP8 GEMM, gate, compressor A/Gate, indexer weights_proj/k_proj and lm_head are bf16. The vendor does NOT run any dense/attention weight at 4 bits. All 40 main layers and the 3 DSpark layers are MoE (counts from the official config.json, which DeepInfer reads: main 384 routed, top-6, 1 shared; drafter 128 routed, top-3, its own EPLB layout group).

40. Deterministic sampling is a COUNTER-BASED RNG, not a stateful generator. The uniform for a sampled token is SplitMix64(seq_seed + f(3*sample_position + case)), case 0 = target sample, 1 = reject/residual sample, 2 = accept test (fused_sample.py:L239 verify_seq = pos*3+2); draft/MTP streams are separated by a seed offset (_MTP_SEED_OFFSET=1000000007) and draft rows use sample_position + local_step*DRAFT_POS_HASH_MUL (tilelang_ops/sampler.py:L65). The draw therefore depends only on (seq_seed, absolute position, row role), never on batch size, row order or how many rejected draws came before; random_sampling.py's own docstring and test_random_sampling_batch_invariant assert equality under row permutation and single-row vs batched. seq_seed defaults to random.getrandbits(64) (sampling_params.py:L74-L88), so determinism requires the caller to pass one.
   - *unaffected*: Adds the mechanism the Stage-1 text lacked: counter-based RNG keyed on (seq_seed, position, role).

41. Sampling arithmetic is reduction-order independent by construction: probabilities are converted to FIXED-POINT integers (default uint32, scale 2^31, exact f32-mantissa shift on the uint64 path) and summed/scanned as integers in a two-level scheme (BD=2048 lane sums, then the winning lane's strided slots), with a rejection-loop modular draw (rnd % total after discarding rnd>=limit) so the choice is unbiased. Integer addition is associative, so no warp/thread-order variance can move the CDF; zero-probability columns can never be selected. The pure-PyTorch reference (random_sampling_ref) uses an fp64 cumsum instead and is documented as to-be-replaced for Ascend, i.e. the reference and kernel are not bit-identical.
   - anchor `deepinfer/model_executor/layers/tilelang_ops/cuda/random_sampling.py:L13 @ 774ce24` @ deepseek-ai/DeepInfer @ 774ce244b87cc0eb8d4b94ab9f5de2ab22049f01: "fixed-point encoded into scale_dtype with scale 2**scale_bits.""""
42. Speculative sampling uses standard accept-test + residual resampling with a deliberate bias bound: accept iff exp(min(target_lp - draft_lp, 0)) > U (U from the hashed stream above), else resample from residual max(q - alpha*p, 0) with alpha = exp(-DRAFT_PROB_PENALTY), DRAFT_PROB_PENALTY = 5e-5 (so residual is never all-zero); common.py derives q[j]-eps/(1+eps) <= A[j] <= (1+eps)q[j], eps = 1-alpha ~5e-5. The fused kernel also emits accept_probs = max(1 - sum residual, 0), the per-row quantity the DSpark confidence/varlen path can be calibrated against. README states deterministic sampling additionally needs DEEPINFER_ENABLE_MTP_VARLEN=0; the code reason is NOT written down - the on-device recompute of verify lengths from confidence (worker.py:L218-L221) makes how many rows (hence which positions) are verified depend on batch-wide budget, which is the natural cause (inference, flagged as such).
   - *narrowed*: Deterministic sampling needs MTP_VARLEN=0 per README; the code reason is not stated (inference only).

KV formats (read from cache_common.py, cache_engine.py, model_metadata_utils.py; byte counts re-derived): main compressed KV, FP4 mode (cfg use_fp4_sparse_kv, default True in hugging_face/config.py:L303): 512 e2m1 values/token in groups of 16, one e4m3 (fp8) scale per group, NO global scale (NVFP4 sub-block without the tensor scale) = 256 B data + 32 B scale = 288 B/token; main compressed KV, MXFP8 mode and the SWA window KV: 512 e4m3 values, ue8m0 (power-of-two) scale per 32 = 512 + 16 = 528 B/token (Interleaved [data|scale] per token, 16-byte-aligned rows); indexer KV: 128 values/token, MXFP4 (e2m1, ue8m0 per 32 = 64+4 B) or MXFP8 (e4m3, ue8m0 per 32), CUDA also keeps an fp32-per-token-scale FP8 index format; index caches store block-wide data then scale sections. Scales: FP4 = amax/6 (FLOAT4_E2M1_MAX), FP8 = amax/448, amax floored by an epsilon margin (FP4 6*2^-126, FP8 1e-4); for the e4m3-scale FP4 path the scale is quantised to e4m3 FIRST and the quantiser divides by the DECODED scale, so the stored scale is exactly the one used; ue8m0 rounds the scale UP to a power of two. The Ascend FP4 converter uses sticky-bit truncation to avoid double rounding before the FP32->BF16->FP4 cast. Routed-expert MXFP4 (per-32 ue8m0) is stated in the Q&A only; the weight loader was not read.

The quantisation of the KV caches is PART OF THE MODEL DEFINITION, not a serving-time compression option: the official DS4.1 reference inference/model.py applies act_quant (FP8, post-RoPE, RoPE tail included) to the sliding-window K and main KV (L707, L1042, L1062) and fp4_act_quant(latent, 16, True, scale_dtype=float8_e4m3fn) to every compressed-KV latent before it is written to the cache (L760), with the indexer's q/k at fp4 group 32 (L546-L552). DeepInfer's cache formats are the byte-level realisation of exactly those reference quantisers, so a BF16/F16 KV is NOT numerically the reference. The EPYC port tree at dd3d9721c contains no fake-quant / act_quant / fp4-roundtrip op on the KV path (grep over src/ and ggml.h found only ggml_gather_rows_e4m3_e8m0 for the engram table), so the llama.cpp port stores un-roundtripped KV.

50. Structured-output pipeline (CPU matcher + GPU bitmask): xgrammar GrammarCompiler (json_schema strict_mode, regex, ebnf; 10 GiB compile cache by default, kill-switch DEEPINFER_XGRAMMAR_DISABLE_CACHE) runs in a ThreadPool (SOMatcherBuilder, optionally CPU-pinned; DEEPINFER_SO_BUILDER_CPUS=4); a request enters the scheduler only when its matcher future resolves (pump()), a 20 s timeout or compile error yields an ABORTED matcher that kills only that request. Per decode step (sampler._get_so_mask) each SO sequence's matcher accepts the previous step's accepted tokens, fills a pinned int32 bitmask row for position 0, then SPECULATIVELY accepts each draft token in order, filling a further bitmask row after each accepted prefix and stopping at the first draft token the grammar rejects, then rolls the matcher back by the number of speculative accepts (rollback depth capped at NextN+2). Masks are copied on a side stream (event-synchronised) and applied IN PLACE to the target logits of the verify rows BEFORE temperature/top-p/reject sampling, so a grammar-illegal draft token gets target probability 0 and is rejected by the ordinary accept test; rows after the first illegal draft are left unmasked because only accepted rows are read downstream. Extra: trigger_token_ids (KMP-matched free-run until a token sequence appears, then constrained) and bound_token_id (force the trigger sequence token-by-token once bound), which supports 'free reasoning, then JSON' without a second pass. Masks are computed only on CP rank 0. No claim here that the mask cost is hidden by async scheduling beyond the side-stream copy; the matcher work itself is synchronous CPU in _get_so_mask.

Eval harness (eval/): offline batch, generation and scoring separated; AIME 2026 (30 tasks x 32 samples), GPQA Diamond (198 x 8), HLE (2500 x 1), all T=1.0 top_p=1.0, reasoning_effort 100, per-sample seq_seed, DSpark varlen verify enabled in run_eval.py; Pass@1 = mean per-sample accuracy via the unbiased Codex pass@k estimator; summary.json carries coverage_complete (a Pass@1 is invalid unless true) and rule_vs_llm_agreement. Judge: LLM-judge service default (configured by EVAL_JUDGE_* env vars; README example model deepseek-v4-flash; deterministic-grader prompt, HLE uses the official HLE judge prompt with a drift-tolerant verdict parser) or a deterministic rule judge (\boxed{} integer / ANSWER: letter; HLE rejects --judge rule). Vendor-reported reference Pass@1: AIME 2026 99.9%, GPQA-D 93.4%, HLE-full 42.6%, labelled 'indicative rather than exact'. The harness does NOT itself require batch invariance (--batch-invariant is optional) and ships no datasets.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 (wave 1, D1): Stage 1 described the feature as "scheduler verifies the prefix subset that maximizes expected effective throughput against a profiled hardware throughput curve". Code shows: curve = main-forward latency per graph size (startup-profiled, trimmed mean, random gating) plus online slope and sampler medians; score = cumulative product of per-position sigmoid confidences; draft depth fixed at 5, verify length variable; CPU budget from N-2 rates, split from N-1 rates; MTP_VARLEN and ZOS default off. Vendor table is labelled "estimated", computed from EP64/DP64 benchmark data, with no data in the repo; add the +11.0% row and the 1.2-5.2% accepted-length loss. Added EPYC finding: our DS41 port computes the confidence head but reads it only under p_min>0 (default 0), as a raw per-token threshold; no DS41 p_min measurement exists in the lane; DFlash2 has no head and its draft branch ignores p_min. 2026-10-20 (wave 2, D2): Stage 1 said "recomputes only the last 128 tokens" for the decoder variant; code retains KEEP_TOKENS (default 140, floor 134) and the 128 is the SWA window. Stage 1 listed the layer split only implicitly; it is now pinned to n_prefill_layer=20 derived from compress_ratios. The shipped YoCo equivalence harness is mask-vs-compact bitwise, so it gives no quality evidence for the approximation (Stage 1 left this unexamined). The 128-grain store, replica-region copy and L-134 hit rule are Q&A-only and not implemented in the released code. 2026-10-20 (wave 1, D3): Stage-1 claim 4 (time-budget cost(q,c) chunking and request-count-vs-KV decode routing) overturned as 'present in release': both are Q&A-only descriptions of closed load-balancer components. Claim 3 (ZOS) confirmed and made precise (one-step lag, residual syncs, late stop detection). Added: prefill and decode are separate steps, not mixed; our llama-server already has an adaptive prefill budget (default OFF, in fold W / v11). 2026-10-20 (wave 1, D4): Stage 1 had read no code. This dive (a) narrows seed claim 5 (EPLB is opt-in, EP>1-only, windowed per-step-peak objective; replica routing not open) and claim 6 (dense stays 8-bit+); (b) answers DS41-B12 a/b/c and B11/B4 from a second implementation and the official reference - the lane graph already encodes all of them, so those boxes only need evidence attached; (c) corrects DS41-B8's 'no prefetch depth' to 'no cross-step prefetch, but intra-step overlap depth exists'; (d) corrects the working belief that the dense-mask attention is how the model is run: vendor and official reference gather by index, and the candidate stage also restricts indexer logits, which our port does not; (e) records that the one router detail B11 lists (top-k eps) is not settled by DeepInfer and differs in our generic path by a measure-zero margin. 2026-10-20 (wave 1, D5): Stage-1 claim 7 narrowed (flag confirmed, mechanism in unvendored libraries, NVIDIA-only = Ascend TODO); claim 6 narrowed (FP4 main KV conditional on use_fp4_sparse_kv; 288/528 B/token derived); added that KV quantisation is part of the reference model definition (act_quant/fp4_act_quant), which the EPYC port does not replicate; added the counter-RNG, integer-CDF, PIT/KS-test and structured-output-with-speculation mechanisms; overturned the idea that offline_decode_ppl.json is a usable DS41-T3 reference. 2026-10-20 (wave 1, main review of second reader): claim 3 ZOS-default corrected (default ON for NORMAL/COMPLETION engines); claim 21 preemption victim order OVERTURNED and corrected (victims are the longest-output sequences). Both verified by main against source; anchors added. 2026-10-20 second reader (independent agents, two batches): mechanical re-find 138/138 (134 dive anchors + 4 main-added anchors; hashes recomputed); support yes 23 / partial 28 / no 1. Disagreements: claim 21 answered no -> main verified source and OVERTURNED the victim clause (corrected in place); claim 3 partial (ZOS default) -> main verified and corrected in place; claim 1 partial (per-position temperature) -> main verified mtp/dflash.py:L62-L64 (temperature tuple indexed by step) and kept the claim (reader wrong); claim 9 partial (first-token logits through the approximate path) -> kept as an EPYC-side inference, carried to Stage 3; 22 partials on claims 26-51 are missing-anchor specifics (config values, lines outside the anchored window), no contradiction - carried to the Stage-3 plan as anchor gaps, not auto-corrected.

### Verdict

CITE: the mechanism (cumulative product of per-position confidences, greedy rank-and-cut maximizing expected tokens per second against a measured step-latency curve, fixed 5-token draft with variable verify length, lossless rejection sampling with positional rollback) as read from code at 774ce24. DO NOT CARRY: the +4-7% / +11% figures as a performance expectation for EPYC (they are vendor-derived estimates from a GPU cluster with a flat-then-rising verify cost; our DS41 CPU verify cost is linear at about 23 ms per row on 86 ms) or the phrase "profiled throughput curve" (it is a latency curve of the main forward). DO NOT propose a duplicate of CAFE-1/2/3: the per-request adaptive-width controller and the verify cost curve are already filed there; what DeepInfer adds, and what is new for the lane, is (a) the cumulative-product score, (b) a cross-request global budget ranked on that score, (c) a measured-latency objective, and (d) the staged N-2/N-1 split. Cite for: (1) the exact layer split (n_prefill_layer=20 derived from the first ratio-1 layer; layer-20 KV plane shared by all 20 decoder layers); (2) decoder tail replay is an APPROXIMATE, implemented, vendor-used prefill shortcut whose equivalence test is mask-vs-compact bitwise only; (3) the KV byte formats (528 / 288 / 68 B per token). Do NOT carry: "nearly halves prefill" as an EPYC speed number; the L-134 hit rule or the replica-region copy as verified behaviour (Q&A only, KV system unreleased); 140 as derived (floor is 134, the extra 6 is unexplained); any quality claim for the truncation (none exists in the source). The mechanism is NOT applicable to recurrent (GDN) state, which has no finite window. Cite only: (a) the open engine is a one-step-lag ZOS pipeline with separate prefill and decode steps, DP-voted prefill attempts every 70/100 decode steps, token-budget chunking and block-based LCFS preemption (code-verified @774ce24); (b) the Q&A's quadratic cost(q,c) time-budget chunk solve and the decode request-count-vs-KV-length heuristic are VENDOR-DESCRIBED ONLY with no code in the release - cite as 'vendor-described, unverified', never as a verified mechanism. Do NOT carry the 155T tokens/day or EP64 figures from this dive. FlopsEstimator is MFU accounting, not the cost model. CITE for: (1) independent corroboration - from a second implementation - of DS41-B12 a/b/c, B11 (interleaved RoPE, inverse rotation on the output, compressed-only YaRN, no mscale) and B4 (shifted HC chain, DSpark blocks, stream-mean features); anchors 0-4 and the official model.py lines are the evidence, the lane may tick B12 and close B11 except the top-k eps; (2) vendor-mechanism evidence that sparse attention, the candidate stage and the sparse indexer are index-gather end to end, so the dense -inf mask in our port is a port choice (DS41-C123/T2); (3) the EPLB algorithm as a portable, test-pinned policy (claims 10-11) to be used as an OFFLINE analysis instrument on our routing traces. DO NOT carry: any EPYC throughput expectation from EPLB (vendor moe_lb 1.045-1.090 are EP64-scale GPU token ratios; our batch-1 CPU decode has max/mean >= 1.87 under a null model and the in-tree CPU15 expert-parallel history is neutral-to-negative); the router epsilon, the engram XOR/mod arithmetic or the replica-selection rule as DeepInfer-sourced (all live in unvendored kernels - use official model.py/engram.py); and do not treat the engram 'no prefetch depth' statement as final - intra-step overlap exists. Cite for: (1) the sampling design - counter-based position-keyed RNG, integer fixed-point CDF, bounded-bias speculative residual, and the PIT/KS distribution test as a spec-sampling gate; (2) test SHAPES for exactness gates (golden-forced batched-vs-single bit-equality; decode-vs-prefill logprob equality at zero tolerance); (3) the KV byte formats (FP4 per-16 e4m3 = 288 B/token, MXFP8 per-32 ue8m0 = 528 B/token, NVFP4-without-global-scale) and the finding that the REFERENCE model.py already roundtrips KV through act_quant/fp4_act_quant, so a bf16/f16-KV port is not numerically the reference; (4) the structured-output verify protocol. Do NOT carry: offline_decode_ppl.json values as a parity reference (exact-float, one hardware config, post-temperature logprobs of self-sampled text); the claim that batch invariance = 'fixed reduction order / split-K off' (that policy sits in unvendored FlashMLA/DeepGEMM/tile_kernels and was not read); any Pass@1 figure as an EPYC target (vendor-reported, FP8-grid reference arm).

## intake-2117#record — DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash: Pushing the Limits of KV Cache Compression

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19969
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty high, relevance high; categories: kv_cache, inference_serving, moe_optimization, quantization, speculative_decoding, memory_augmented, context_extension)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2116#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Layer map (paper SS4.2.1, matches config.json): 40 layers = 20-layer causal encoder + 20-layer decoder. Layers 0-1 are SWA-only. Encoder layers 2-19 are CSA2 with compression m=2 in three 6-layer groups (1 Full + 5 Reuse), so Full layers sit at 2, 8, 14. Decoder layers 20-39 are CSA2 with m=1 (UNcompressed main KV) in five 4-layer groups: group 1 = Full at layer 20 + 3 Reuse; groups 2-5 = Reindex at 24, 28, 32, 36 + 3 Reuse. The layer INDICES are our derivation from the stated group sizes; they coincide exactly with config.json kv_source_layer_ids=[2,8,14,20] (Full layers), index_source_layer_ids=[2,8,14,20,24,28,32,36] (Full+Reindex) and candidate_source_layer_id=20.
   - *narrowed*: Stage-1 claim 0 ("552B backbone ... up to 1M ... CED activates fewer params in prefill than decode") - 552B, 1M, 8B/16B all CONFIRMED (Abstract, SS2.1, SS4.2.1, Table 1). Narrowed: 552B EXCLUDES the 196B Engram (and DSpark, ViT); the 763.2B in our handoff is a scope difference, not a conflict.

These layers are divided into five groups of four layers. In the first group, the first layer operates in Full Mode, and the remaining three layers operate in Reuse Mode. The remaining four groups share the same configuration: the ..."
1. CED global KV is exact, not approximate: decoder-layer global KV entries are projected from the encoder-boundary hidden state H_{L/2} with layer-dependent weights (Eq. 1); under CSA2 only the decoder Full-mode layer (layer 20) actually computes global KV (from H_{L/2}), and Reindex/Reuse decoder layers share it. Hence prefill needs only the 20 encoder layers to obtain ALL global KV. What prefill cannot skip is SWA: SWA KV is still derived from each layer's own hidden state, so an exact decoder SWA KV needs the decoder layers run over the last n_win x L/2 = 128 x 20 = 2,560 prompt tokens.
   - *narrowed*: Stage-1 claim 1 (CED mechanism) CONFIRMED and sharpened: only the decoder Full-mode layer (20) projects global KV from H_{L/2}; decoder layers sharing it are Reindex/Reuse. "prefill computes only the encoder half" holds for global KV (exact) but the decoder tail of 128 tokens is still computed (approximate SWA). Exact decoder SWA would need 2,560 tokens.

The Reindex and Reuse Modes are unchanged."

2. The "nearly half of prefill" figure is a structural/complexity statement, not a measured speedup: prefill cost O(N*L) -> O(N*L/2 + n_win*L/2), and 8B activated params in prefill vs 16B in decode. The report contains NO prefill FLOPs/latency table and NO CED-on/CED-off quality ablation; "comparable performance" rests on Table 1, which compares different base models (V4-Flash, V4-Pro, V4.1-Flash), not CED vs non-CED. By our arithmetic the per-prompt layer-compute fraction is 0.5 + 64/N (N prompt tokens, n_win=128): 0.625 at N=512, 0.531 at 2,048, 0.508 at 8,192.
   - *unaffected*: CSA2 Full/Reindex/Reuse static assignment CONFIRMED; now with the exact per-layer map (claim 0) matched to config kv_source/index_source/candidate ids.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv 2609.19969v1 (2026-09-17); text.txt extracted from arxiv.org/html; arXiv PDF sha256-12 0cfdda19c8e1; content cross-checked identical to local HF PDF sha256-12 ba68e2e40408: "the model activates 16B parameters per token during decode but only 8B parameters during prefill"
   - anchor `SS2.2 CED` @ arXiv 2609.19969v1 (2026-09-17); text.txt extracted from arxiv.org/html; arXiv PDF sha256-12 0cfdda19c8e1; content cross-checked identical to local HF PDF sha256-12 ba68e2e40408: "Consequently, CED successfully reduces nearly half of the prefill computation while maintaining performance comparable to the baseline."
3. SWA Bounded Replay: replay only the most recent n_win=128 tokens and truncate SWA to the replay segment (a query at i attends to [max(s, i-W+1), i] for replay start s) - an explicitly APPROXIMATE reconstruction. Encoder variant: on a persistent-cache SWA miss, replay the last 128 tokens of the cached prefix (regenerating only SWA KV, global KV untouched). Decoder variant: at EVERY prefill, push the last 128 tokens' encoder outputs through the decoder layers (same truncation); the result is used for decoding only, never cached. Degradation is reported ONLY qualitatively ("negligible", "barely compromises response quality"); no ablation table, no numbers, no conditions are given; the Conclusion concedes untested boundary cases. Decoder-variant justification: PowerAttention (Chen et al. 2025) said to show SWA effective receptive field << n_win x L/2 (not read), plus replay is simulated during post-training for train-aware adaptation.
   - *narrowed*: Stage-1 claim 3 CONFIRMED in mechanism, with the encoder (cache-miss fallback) vs decoder (every prefill) variants separated, and the measured-degradation question settled as NOT-FOUND: qualitative only.

4. CSA2 modes (SS2.3.1): each layer keeps its own Q and SWA KV. Full = own main KV + indexer Q, indexer K projected from main KV, fresh Top-K. Reindex = reuse a preceding Full layer's main KV and indexer K, own indexer Q, fresh Top-K. Reuse = reuse main KV AND latest Top-K, no indexer. Hierarchical Sparse Indexer (decoder only, introduced in post-training): the first decoder Full layer scans the whole visible range, then builds a candidate pool (up to 2,048 blocks x 8 positions = 16,384 positions) that later Reindex layers search; Top-K=512 per layer.
   - *narrowed*: Stage-1 claim 4 CONFIRMED (E2M1, one E4M3 per 16, no global scale, dequant before attention). Added: QAT in post-training; post-RoPE; SWA stays FP8; reference applies it as fake-quant; no with/without table.
   - anchor `SS2.3.1` @ arXiv 2609.19969v1 (2026-09-17); text.txt extracted from arxiv.org/html; arXiv PDF sha256-12 0cfdda19c8e1; content cross-checked identical to local HF PDF sha256-12 ba68e2e40408: "Reindex Mode. The layer reuses the most recent available main KV from a preceding layer together with its corresponding indexer K . The indexer computes its own query, rescores the reused keys, and produces fresh Top-K indices."
   - anchor `SS2.3.2` @ arXiv 2609.19969v1 (2026-09-17); text.txt extracted from arxiv.org/html; arXiv PDF sha256-12 0cfdda19c8e1; content cross-checked identical to local HF PDF sha256-12 ba68e2e40408: "For example, selecting 2,048 blocks with 8 positions each yields 16,384 candidate positions."
5. FP4 main KV: E2M1 values with one E4M3 scale per 16 channels (NVFP4-like, second-level global scale omitted); applied AFTER RoPE to non-RoPE and RoPE parts alike; QAT introduced in POST-training; dequantized before attention (storage saving, no FP4 matmul needed); SWA KV stays FP8. Quality is described only as "only marginal" / "no measurable decrease" - there is no with/without-FP4 table anywhere in the report. The official model.py confirms the numerics at inference as fake-quant (in-place dequantized write-back): compressed KV fp4_act_quant(latent, 16, True, E4M3) at model.py:760; indexer K/Q use FP4 with block 32 + E8M0 scales (model.py:546,552); window KV uses FP8 QDQ (model.py:707). The paper text is loose about naming (it says it "adopts MXFP4" in the same paragraph, which is the indexer format; main KV is the NVFP4-style E4M3/16 variant).
   - *unaffected*: Stage-1 claim 5 (DSpark structure) CONFIRMED verbatim (3 blocks, 128 window, 5 positions, Markov + confidence heads, throughput-curve scheduler). No numbers exist in this report.

6. Global KV 890 B/token (main KV + indexer K, excluding SWA) is vendor-stated and internally reproducible: per 512-ch main entry 256 B FP4 + 32 E4M3 scales = 288 B; per 128-ch indexer K entry 64 B + 4 E8M0 scales = 68 B; encoder 3 Full layers at m=2 -> 3 x (288+68)/2 = 534 B/token; decoder 1 Full layer at m=1 -> 288+68 = 356 B/token; total 890 B/token. (This reconstruction is OURS, not in the paper; exact match supports the layer map in claim 0 and the sharing semantics.) Reported as ~1/4 of V4-Flash and ~1/437 of V1 (Fig. 1b, image only - per-generation byte values are not in the text).

7. Persistent KV management (SS3.2.1): V4 kept global KV and SWA KV in separate LRU pools; SWA KV (cached only at end-of-prompt and end-of-output) was ~half of persistent capacity; target residency >72 h on SSD. V4.1 removes SWA KV from the persistent cache into a distributed host-DRAM pool sized at 10% of each machine's DRAM with minute-scale TTL; global KV keeps a >=72 h guaranteed lifetime; infrequent global-hit/SWA-miss requests recover via Encoder SWA Bounded Replay. Persistent footprint 1/8 of V4 = (SWA removed ~1/2) x (global KV 1/4). Also EPD (encoder-prefill-decode) disaggregation at deployment.
   - *narrowed*: Stage-1 reported_result "CED removes nearly half of prefill compute at comparable quality": compute half is structural; "at comparable quality" is NOT separately measured (no ablation). Table 1 does not isolate CED and trails V4-Pro on Simple-QA/LongBench-V2/MultiLoKo.
   - anchor `SS3.2.1 item 1` @ arXiv 2609.19969v1 (2026-09-17); text.txt extracted from arxiv.org/html; arXiv PDF sha256-12 0cfdda19c8e1; content cross-checked identical to local HF PDF sha256-12 ba68e2e40408: "SWA KV is no longer cached in the persistent KV cache and instead stored in a distributed memory pool provisioned from 10% of the host DRAM on each machine."
   - anchor `SS3.2.1` @ arXiv 2609.19969v1 (2026-09-17); text.txt extracted from arxiv.org/html; arXiv PDF sha256-12 0cfdda19c8e1; content cross-checked identical to local HF PDF sha256-12 ba68e2e40408: "Global KV remains in the persistent KV cache with a guaranteed lifetime of at least 72 hours."
8. DSpark (SS2.4.3): drafter = 3 Transformer blocks with 128-token sliding window; one forward pass yields base logits for 5 draft positions in parallel; a Markov head models inter-draft dependencies; a confidence head predicts per-position conditional acceptance probability -> prefix survival probability; a scheduler combines these with PROFILED engine throughput curves to pick a per-request verification length maximizing expected system-wide token throughput under current load. Trained in a dedicated stage after pre-training with the backbone frozen, then alongside the backbone in post-training with no gradient into the backbone; MTP omitted from backbone pre-training. This report gives NO acceptance-length, speedup or throughput numbers for DSpark (algorithm/numbers live in the separate DSpark paper, arXiv 2607.05147 = intake-738#record).
   - anchor `SS2.4.3 DSpark` @ arXiv 2609.19969v1 (2026-09-17); text.txt extracted from arxiv.org/html; arXiv PDF sha256-12 0cfdda19c8e1; content cross-checked identical to local HF PDF sha256-12 ba68e2e40408: "The scheduler combines these estimates with profiled engine throughput curves to dynamically select the verification length for each request, aiming to maximize expected system-wide token throughput under the current system load."
9. Parameter accounting: the report states 552B BACKBONE parameters (Table 1 "# Backbone Params") plus 196B Engram parameters (2 modules, layers 1 and 14, FP8) - 748B before DSpark and the vision stack. The 763.2B in our DS41 handoff is not in the report; the difference 763.2 - 552 - 196 = 15.2B is consistent with the DSpark drafter (3 blocks x 128 routed experts x 3 x 5120 x 2304 = 13.6B in experts alone) + DeepSeek-ViT (32 layers, d=1024, ~0.41B) + projector/aligner + embeddings, i.e. a scope difference, NOT a contradiction (this decomposition is our arithmetic, not in the paper). Backbone check: 385 experts x 3 x 5120 x 2304 x 40 layers = 545B + attention ~ 552B.
   - anchor `SS2.1 Overview` @ arXiv 2609.19969v1 (2026-09-17); text.txt extracted from arxiv.org/html; arXiv PDF sha256-12 0cfdda19c8e1; content cross-checked identical to local HF PDF sha256-12 ba68e2e40408: "Overall, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash has 552B backbone parameters and 196B Engram parameters, activating 8B parameters per token during prefill and 16B during decode."
10. Efficiency evidence in the report is figure-only: Fig. 1b (global KV bytes/token by generation: ~4x below V4-Flash, ~437x below V1) and Fig. 2 (single-token decode FLOPs vs context; 4K->1M, 256x longer, raises decode FLOPs by only 1/4; ops weighted BF16=1, FP8=0.5, FP4=0.25). Text-only claims: 8B/16B activated params (prefill/decode), CSA2 Reuse layers run 15 kernels in prefill / 11 in decode, Mega-mHC halves activation memory traffic. There is no table of prefill FLOPs, no KV-bytes-at-128K/1M table (by arithmetic 890 B x 131,072 = 117 MB; x 1,048,576 = 933 MB per sequence for global KV alone) and no throughput benchmark.
   - anchor `SS1 (Fig. 2 discussion)` @ arXiv 2609.19969v1 (2026-09-17); text.txt extracted from arxiv.org/html; arXiv PDF sha256-12 0cfdda19c8e1; content cross-checked identical to local HF PDF sha256-12 ba68e2e40408: "Figure 2 shows that extending the context length 256-fold, from 4K to 1M, increases its Decode FLOPs by only 1/4"
11. Table 1 (base models, internal framework, "gap <= 0.3 = same level") does NOT show V4.1-Flash-Base "fully on par" with V4-Pro-Base: Simple-QA-verified 42.3 vs 55.2, LongBench-V2 45.2 vs 51.5, MultiLoKo 45.5 vs 50.9 trail V4-Pro; MMLU-Pro 74.1 vs 73.5 and HumanEval 79.4 vs 76.8 lead. Base-model evals precede post-training, so they measure neither FP4 KV QAT nor the post-training replay adaptation. No long-context or KV-compression ablation is reported.
   - anchor `Table 1` @ arXiv 2609.19969v1 (2026-09-17); text.txt extracted from arxiv.org/html; arXiv PDF sha256-12 0cfdda19c8e1; content cross-checked identical to local HF PDF sha256-12 ba68e2e40408: "Simple-QA verified (EM) 25-shot 30.1 55.2 42.3"
   - anchor `Table 1` @ arXiv 2609.19969v1 (2026-09-17); text.txt extracted from arxiv.org/html; arXiv PDF sha256-12 0cfdda19c8e1; content cross-checked identical to local HF PDF sha256-12 ba68e2e40408: "Long Context LongBench-V2 (EM) 1-shot 44.7 51.5 45.2"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 (wave 1): Stage 1 left the persistent-KV, CSA2, evaluation and parameter sections unread. This dive (a) resolved the 552B vs 763.2B "conflict" as a scope difference (552B backbone + 196B Engram; DSpark/ViT make up the rest of 763.2B), (b) replaced "nearly half of prefill at comparable quality" with its actual basis (structural, no ablation) and a derived layer-FLOP fraction 0.5+64/N, (c) found NO numeric degradation evidence for SWA Bounded Replay or FP4 KV (qualitative only; Conclusion concedes untested cases), (d) pinned the exact per-layer Full/Reindex/Reuse map to config kv_source/index_source/candidate ids, (e) reproduced 890 B/token from the layer map (534 + 356), (f) showed Table 1 does not support "on par with V4-Pro" and excludes the post-training FP4/replay adaptations, (g) established the local PDF and arXiv v1 are content-identical, and (h) mapped to the port: global-KV semantics already match CED, but prefill runs all 40 layers and no KV QDQ is applied. 2026-10-20 second reader (independent agent): re-find 29/29; support yes 11 / partial 1 / no 0. Disagreement carried to Stage 3: claim 1 says decoder global KV is exact, reader notes §3.2.2 says the global KV of a replayed uncached suffix is not identical across cache-hit positions; corrections applied: none.

### Verdict

Cite from this report ONLY: the layer map and CSA2 mode assignment (SS4.2.1, matches config), the CED global-KV mechanism (exact; one decoder Full layer projects from the encoder boundary), the FP4 main-KV format, the 890 B/token global-KV figure (vendor-stated, reproducible from the layer map), the persistent-KV tiering design, and the DSpark mechanism description. All are vendor-reported (DeepSeek production design), never an EPYC number. Do NOT carry: (1) "nearly half of prefill at comparable quality" as a measured result - it is structural (0.5 + 64/N layer-FLOP fraction, 8B vs 16B active params) with no CED ablation; (2) "negligible degradation" for SWA Bounded Replay or FP4 KV - there are no numbers or ablations anywhere in the report and the Conclusion concedes untested boundary cases; (3) "fully on par with V4-Pro" - Table 1 trails V4-Pro on Simple-QA-verified, LongBench-V2 and MultiLoKo, and base-model evals exclude FP4 QAT and replay adaptation; (4) the 552B-vs-763.2B "discrepancy" - it is a scope difference (backbone vs +Engram +DSpark +ViT). Our port already matches CED global-KV semantics but runs all 40 layers over every prompt token (as does the official reference) and applies none of the reference KV QDQ; a CED-aware prefill is an unmeasured candidate whose quality gate must be ours.

## intake-2118#record — deepseek-recipe: protocol conversion, prompt rendering and tokenization for DeepSeek V4/V4.1

- **URL**: https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-recipe
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: inference_serving, tool_implementation, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2116#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. deepseek-recipe (MIT, 0.2.0 dated 2026-10-20) is Rust libraries plus Python bindings that normalise Messages / Chat Completions / Responses requests into one Conversation, render DeepSeek V4 and V4.1 prompts, encode them to token ids with an attached tokenizer, and parse (streamed) model output back; the caller supplies inference, tool execution and HTTP transport.
   - *unaffected*: Stage-1 claims 1-2 (format conversion, rendering, encoding, output parsing; caller supplies inference): confirmed from README plus source.
   - *unaffected*: Stage-1 claim 5 (README SSRF warning for the default image fetcher): README statement only, image crate not read; not carried into the final list.
1. Special-token injection prevention is structural, not an escape pass: the renderer records byte ranges of explicit special tokens (template control tokens, image placeholders, assistant grounding tokens); every other byte of the prompt - user, system and tool-result text, tool schemas, tool-call names and arguments, the response-format schema - is plain text, and PromptTokenizer encodes plain regions with a clone of the tokenizer whose added vocabulary is emptied, so a special-token spelling typed by a user becomes ordinary BPE pieces and cannot act as a control token.
   - *reattributed*: Stage-1 cross-reference note (DeepInfer Q&A mechanisms not visible in README): injection prevention and segmented tokenization are located in docs/tokenizer.md and deepseek-recipe-encoding/src/tokenizer.rs; segmentation is by special-token boundary, not by length (see claim 3).
   - anchor `docs/tokenizer.md:L74 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "A special-token spelling typed into a user message is plain text, so it cannot"
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe-encoding/src/tokenizer.rs:L56 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "plain_tokenizer.with_added_vocabulary(AddedVocabulary::new());"
2. The only special spellings promoted from message text back to control tokens are the eight grounding tokens (ref/box/point/polygon open and close), and only inside assistant content and assistant reasoning content (Aho-Corasick match); every other special spelling in assistant content stays plain text.
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe-encoding/src/v4/mod.rs:L22 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "/// explicit special token; every other special-token spelling in assistant"
3. Segmented parallel tokenization exists in code but the segments are the plain-text regions between explicit special tokens, encoded as one encode_batch_fast batch (parallel unless TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM disables it). It is a consequence of the special/plain split, not a length-based chunker: one long plain region (a single huge user message) is one segment, so no intra-message parallelism is claimed or found.
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe-encoding/src/tokenizer.rs:L102 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: ".encode_batch_fast(regions, false)"
   - anchor `docs/tokenizer.md:L72 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "in parallel, depending on its `TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM` setting."
4. V4.1 tool-call tags are space-prefixed (' calls', ' invoke', ' parameter') after the DSML token, matching the official encoding.py (tool_calls_block_name = ' calls'); the stream parser's block-close matcher accepts both '</DSML tool_calls>' and '</DSML calls>'.
   - *narrowed*: Stage-1 claim 3 (thinking mode, reasoning effort, tool calls...): thinking, effort and DSML rendering verified in code (final claims 4-6); deferred tool loading is the defer_loading filter in dsv41.rs render_tool_prompt; images and constraints not read.

   - anchor `deepseek-recipe/src/stream/output_parser.rs:L403 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "Segment::Plain(" calls>"),"
5. V4.1 thinking-mode rendering writes a system token plus 'Reasoning Effort: N (range 1-100 ...)' before message 0 only in thinking mode, with N = 50 (low), 75 (none/high/xhigh), 100 (max); the official encoding.py maps low/high/max to 50/75/100 with default high and also accepts an integer 1-100 (the recipe enum has no integer path, and 'xhigh' is a recipe-only synonym for 75).
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe-encoding/src/v4/dsv41.rs:L23 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "Some(ReasoningEffort::Low) => "50","
6. BOS: the V4.1 renderer writes the BOS spelling as the first explicit special token of every rendered prompt, and the TokenizerEncoder contract forbids the encoder adding any token (BOS included) beyond what the text encodes to, so the recipe yields exactly one BOS; the official tokenizer_config has add_bos_token false.
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe-encoding/src/v4/dsv41.rs:L288 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "let mut prompt = prompt!("{}", self.components.special(SpecialToken::Bos));"
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe-encoding/src/tokenizer.rs:L25 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "/// such as a BOS token, beyond those that the text encodes to."
7. Default max_tokens when the request sets none: 8192 outside thinking mode, 65536 in thinking mode, 131072 in thinking mode with max effort; accepted upper bound 393216; resolve_max_tokens(prompt_tokens, context_limit) rejects a request whose prompt plus requested max_tokens exceeds the context limit and otherwise clamps the default to the context left (minimum 1). These defaults are far below the model card's 'max_tokens >= 256K' recommendation.
   - *narrowed*: Stage-1 claim 4 (default max_tokens plus context check): confirmed with exact numbers 8192/65536/131072, upper bound 393216 and the rejection rule; adds that the defaults sit below the card recommendation.
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe/src/request/mod.rs:L162 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "pub const DEFAULT_THINKING_MAX_TOKENS: u32 = 64 * 1024;"
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe/src/request/mod.rs:L166 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "pub const DEFAULT_MAX_EFFORT_MAX_TOKENS: u32 = 128 * 1024;"
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe/src/request/options.rs:L96 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "pub const DEFAULT_MAX_TOKENS_UPPER_BOUND: u32 = 393_216;"
8. Sampling resolution: temperature is forced to 1.0 in thinking mode and is the requested value (default 1.0) otherwise; top_p for V4.1 is max(requested or 1.0, 0.95) in thinking mode and 1.0 outside it (requested value ignored). The resolved InferenceOptions carry only max_tokens, temperature, top_p, logprobs, top_logprobs, seed and the output constraint - no top_k, min_p or penalty fields (presence/frequency penalties are validated, not forwarded).
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe/src/request/options.rs:L87 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "(Self::DeepseekV41Flash, true) => top_p.unwrap_or(1.0).max(0.95),"
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe/src/protocol/validation.rs:L114 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "temperature: Some(if self.thinking_mode {"
9. Streaming decoder: token ids are detokenized incrementally and held while the decoded text ends in U+FFFD (an incomplete multi-token character); each token of the added vocabulary is decoded as its own marked piece so that '</think>' and the DSML tags are recognised only when they arrive as special tokens - the same text spelled with plain tokens is content (the output-side mirror of injection prevention). A token that itself decodes to U+FFFD is held until later ids complete it and is dropped at end of input.
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe/src/stream/decoder.rs:L218 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "if content.is_empty() || (!force && content.ends_with(REPLACEMENT_CHARACTER)) {"
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe/src/stream/decoder.rs:L59 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "fn is_special_token(&self, _id: u32) -> bool {"
10. Output parser matches markers with a byte-level KMP automaton over (byte, is_special) pairs, so a marker can straddle chunks and a text chunk matches by spelling while a token chunk matches only special/plain-consistently; the failure table is valid only because no marker has a border whose flags differ from the pattern start. Stop sequences share the same matcher.
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe/src/stream/output_parser.rs:L751 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "fn kmp_next("
11. Reasoning rendering matches the official encoding.py in the cases a tool-less or tool-bearing conversation exercises: earlier assistant reasoning is dropped unless the tool prompt is rendered or the message is a prefix continuation. Behavioural parity with the 1017 encoding.py (content_blocks, tool-result-only user messages not starting a turn) was NOT tested.
   - anchor `deepseek-recipe-encoding/src/v4/dsv41.rs:L206 @ 935817c` @ 935817c: "let renders_reasoning = thinking_mode && index > 0 && (!drop_thinking || *prefix);"
12. EPYC-side check (not a recipe claim): at the frozen-lineage tip dd3d9721c, llama-server renders chat templates to one flat std::string (jinja is_input flags are computed but dropped at chat.cpp:927) and tokenizes the result with parse_special = true (server-context.cpp:4882; mtmd path server-common.cpp:711), so a special-token spelling inside user content is parsed as a control token wherever the served vocabulary marks that spelling as a control token (static read; not exercised on a live server).
   - anchor `common/chat.cpp:L927 @ dd3d9721c` @ dd3d9721c: "std::string result = parts->as_string().str();"
   - anchor `tools/server/server-context.cpp:L4882 @ dd3d9721c` @ dd3d9721c: "inputs = tokenize_input_prompts(ctx_server.vocab, ctx_server.mctx, prompt, true, true);"
   - anchor `common/jinja/README.md:L84 @ dd3d9721c` @ dd3d9721c: "Downstream applications like `llama-server` can then make informed decisions about special token parsing based on the `is_input` flag."
   - anchor `tools/server/server-common.cpp:L711 @ dd3d9721c` @ dd3d9721c: "/* parse_special */ true,"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 (wave 1): Stage 1 could not see the mechanisms in the README; they are real and located in docs/tokenizer.md and deepseek-recipe-encoding/src/tokenizer.rs. 'Segmented parallel tokenization' narrowed to special-token-delimited plain regions. Added EPYC-side finding: llama-server at dd3d9721c tokenizes rendered chat prompts with parse_special=true and drops jinja is_input provenance (chat.cpp:927), the opposite of this recipe's design. Added: recipe max_tokens defaults < card recommendation; recipe top_p floor 0.95 vs 1017 card top_p 1.0. 2026-10-20 second reader (independent agent): re-find 23/23; support yes 11 / partial 1 / no 0. Disagreement carried to Stage 3: claim 11's parity with the official encoding.py is unanchored and the reader judges it likely FALSE for tool-less conversations ending in a non-prefix assistant turn (encoding.py:692/737 keep reasoning after the last user turn; recipe dsv41.rs:206 renders it only for prefix continuations under drop_thinking) - treat as an open divergence for DS41-T3b, not a settled parity.

### Verdict

Cite claims 1-3 for HOW special-token injection is prevented (structural provenance, not escaping) and claim 12 for the EPYC-side finding that llama-server at dd3d9721c does not do it (static read; no live probe yet - run A2 step 1 before asserting exploitability on a given role). Cite claims 4-8 for the V4.1 prompt contract (cross-checked against official encoding.py, not against 1017's changed encoding.py). Do NOT carry: the recipe's max_tokens defaults as recommendations (card says >=256K), the 'xhigh' effort alias (not in the official mapping), or any parallel-tokenization speedup for long single messages (segments are special-token-delimited). DeepInfer's Q&A attribution (intake-2116#record) of both mechanisms to this repo is correct for injection prevention and narrowed for 'segmented parallel tokenization'.
