## intake-2137#record — XGrammar-2: Dynamic and Efficient Structured Generation Engine for Agentic LLMs

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04426
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: inference_serving, tool_implementation)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2132#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. TAGDISPATCH (intra-request dynamism): a first-class EBNF-compatible construct parameterised by tag->sub-grammar pairs plus stop strings. Decoding alternates between a 'dispatching' mode (free-form text accepted while an Aho-Corasick DFA matches all registered tags at once) and a 'dispatched' mode (the matched tag's sub-grammar constrains output, then control returns to dispatching). This is the free-then-constrained / tag-triggered pattern for tool calls and Harmony channels. Encoding the same thing in plain EBNF blows up: 100 tags -> 952 AC states / 1902 transitions / EBNF size 6612, compile 15483.5 ms vs 2002.1 ms for TagDispatch (Table 2; 5 tags 1008.6 vs 191.4 ms).
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims verified against saved v4 text.
   - anchor `§3.2 ¶3` @ arXiv 2601.04426v4 (html fetched 2026-10-20; sha256 90fffdb5bc06219e...): "Decoding starts in the dispatching mode, where the engine accepts ordinary text while continuously matching registered tags."
   - anchor `§3.2 ¶4` @ arXiv 2601.04426v4 (html fetched 2026-10-20; sha256 90fffdb5bc06219e...): "we use an Aho–Corasick automaton (AC automaton) (Aho and Corasick, 1975) to match multiple tags simultaneously"
1. CROSS-GRAMMAR CACHE + JIT (inter-request dynamism): hierarchical structural hashing of FSMs (bottom-up over the rule-reference graph, cycles handled by provisional hash plus refinement) lets token-mask caches be reused across requests whose tool sets differ. Table 1 (1908-tool BFCL pool, 100 requests): whole-structure reuse 99.0% static vs 0% dynamic; substructure reuse 99.1% static vs 25.2% / 79.9% / 95.6% dynamic for 10 / 100 / 500 tools. JIT builds mask-cache entries lazily on first visit (compile overlapped with prefill, per-state mask build overlapped with decode); Table 4 on JSONSchemaBench (units not recoverable from the extracted text): baseline 4960.04 preprocessing / 45.50 mask-gen -> +JIT 612.07 / 722.47 (8.1x less preprocessing, 15.9x MORE mask time) -> +cross-grammar 534.80 / 333.75 -> +repetition compression 5.37 / 126.49.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims verified against saved v4 text.
   - anchor `Table 1 caption` @ arXiv 2601.04426v4 (html fetched 2026-10-20; sha256 90fffdb5bc06219e...): "Dynamic workloads fail to reuse the full structure, but can effectively reuse substructures."
   - anchor `§3.5 ¶3` @ arXiv 2601.04426v4 (html fetched 2026-10-20; sha256 90fffdb5bc06219e...): "compilation is overlapped with prefilling and influences the time to the first token"
2. MASK COST AND COMPILE COST (vendor-reported, authors' benchmarks, single-thread mask engines, AMD EPYC 9654 for the mask-only experiments): on CONFETTI-derived Llama tool-calling and Harmony formats, XGrammar-2 compiles in ~10 ms vs >1000 ms for XGrammar; llguidance costs ~250 us/token (Harmony) and >1000 us/token (Llama format); XGrammar-2 cache hit rates 71.43% (Llama format) / 47.21% (Harmony). Absolute XGrammar-2 per-token values are only in Figures 8/9 (images, not readable from the saved text). Abstract headline: over 6x faster compilation.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims verified against saved v4 text.
   - anchor `§4.2 ¶4` @ arXiv 2601.04426v4 (html fetched 2026-10-20; sha256 90fffdb5bc06219e...): "XGrammar-2 has a compilation time of about 10 ms, while XGrammar needs more than 1000 ms to compile"
   - anchor `§4.2 ¶4` @ arXiv 2601.04426v4 (html fetched 2026-10-20; sha256 90fffdb5bc06219e...): "llguidance has about 250 us per-token overhead with OpenAI Harmony Response Format and a more than 1000 us per-token overhead with Llama’s Tool Calling Format"
3. END-TO-END OVERHEAD (vendor-reported; SGLang 0.5.3.post3, RTX 5090 + Xeon 8470Q, BFCL-v3): XGrammar-2 end-to-end function-calling latency ~7x lower than XGrammar and within 6% of unconstrained. Output tokens/s (Table 3), XGrammar vs XGrammar-2: Llama-3.1-8B 83/96 (batch 1), 525/920 (16), 738/1938 (128); Qwen3-0.6B 462/599, 1712/4287, 3021/9475. Table 3 has no unconstrained row, so 'near-zero overhead' rests on the Figure 10 / 6% sentence. llguidance was excluded from throughput because in their SGLang integration it gave empty outputs on Qwen3-0.6B and language drift on Llama-3.1-8B.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims verified against saved v4 text.
   - anchor `§4.3 ¶3` @ arXiv 2601.04426v4 (html fetched 2026-10-20; sha256 90fffdb5bc06219e...): "the gap between the result of XGrammar-2 and the result without constraints is no more than 6%"
   - anchor `§4.3 ¶1` @ arXiv 2601.04426v4 (html fetched 2026-10-20; sha256 90fffdb5bc06219e...): "it results in empty outputs for Qwen3-0.6B and induces language drift from pure English to other languages in Llama3.1-8B"
4. QUALITY (vendor-reported, BFCL-v3, App. I Table 6): correct-call rate / schema-valid rate without vs with XGrammar-2: Llama-3.2-1B 6.07/22.07 -> 32.84/100; 3B 33.12/40.70 -> 77.75/100; 8B 59.48/66.95 -> 80.93/100; 70B 45.60/51.94 -> 86.41/100. Schema validity is guaranteed by construction; the accuracy gain comes from removing malformed calls, and the paper claims Llama-3.2-3B with grammar beats unconstrained 70B on BFCL. Llama family only and the unconstrained baselines are weak, so do not transfer the gain to tool-trained Qwen3.x models.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims verified against saved v4 text.
   - anchor `App. I ¶3` @ arXiv 2601.04426v4 (html fetched 2026-10-20; sha256 90fffdb5bc06219e...): "XGrammar-2 enables Llama-3.2-3B to outperform an unconstrained Llama-3.1-70B baseline on BFCL"
5. SPECULATIVE DECODING: the paper contains NO speculative-decoding, rollback or jump-forward content (zero hits for those terms in the full text). The contribution is orthogonal to the speculative-verify interaction analysed for intake-2132#record; trigger-switching state (AC state + sub-grammar position) would need the same per-accepted-token advance that llama.cpp already performs.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims verified against saved v4 text.
6. ENGINE LINEAGE: parsing backend moves from XGrammar's pushdown automaton to an Earley parser, with the adaptive accepted/rejected/context-dependent token-mask cache built only over 'scannable' Earley states; integrated into SGLang and vLLM per the authors. Baselines are XGrammar, llguidance and Outlines only; llama.cpp is not benchmarked, so no number in this paper describes llama.cpp.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims verified against saved v4 text.
   - anchor `§3.4 ¶1` @ arXiv 2601.04426v4 (html fetched 2026-10-20; sha256 90fffdb5bc06219e...): "we build a new adaptive cache mechanism on top of the Earley parser"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 (wave 3, Stage-2b): ingested and dived in one pass; settled that XGrammar-2 has no spec-decoding content, does not benchmark llama.cpp, that llama.cpp already has lazy trigger grammars, and that JIT raises mask-gen time 15.9x while cutting preprocessing 8.1x. 2026-10-20 second reader (independent agent): re-find 10/10; support yes 5 / partial 1 / no 0. Partial carried to Stage 3 as an anchor gap: claim 1's structural-hashing mechanism and Table 4 figures are not in its quotes. Main dropped a malformed bare-list depends_on [intake-2132#record]: counterfactual test fails (XGrammar-2's claims rest on its own paper).

### Verdict

Cite for: TagDispatch free-then-constrained semantics, the finding that dynamic tool sets defeat whole-grammar caches (0% reuse) while substructure reuse rises with tool count, and the JIT trade (cheaper compile, costlier mask). Do NOT cite as evidence about llama.cpp speed (not benchmarked), spec-decoding compatibility (absent) or CPU-forward overhead (GPU-overlap only). llama.cpp already implements the trigger pattern (lazy grammar) and no grammar-cost problem is measured in our lane, so the only action is to fold eager-vs-lazy mode into the intake-2132#record measurement.

### Linked handoffs and actions

## intake-2138#record — UltraEP: Unleash MoE Training and Inference on Rack-Scale Nodes with Near-Optimal Load Balancing

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04101
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: moe_optimization, inference_serving, hardware_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2134#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Scope is exact-load, per-microbatch, per-layer balancing for TRAINING and SERVING PREFILL on rack-scale nodes (64-GPU scale-up domains, EP64/EP40). Decode is explicitly NOT a target: the authors state compute-side imbalance in memory-bound decode is largely diluted by memory access latency, and raising decode batch conflicts with TPOT SLOs. Evaluation covers prefill TTFT/RPS only; no decode number exists in the paper.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims written from the saved paper text.
   - anchor `§3 Serving Prefill ¶1` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "For memory-bound decode, we discover that the impact of compute-side imbalance is largely diluted by memory access latency."
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "UltraEP, the first exact-load, real-time balancer for large-EP MoE training and serving prefill on rack-scale nodes (RSNs)."
1. Planning input is the CURRENT post-gating load, not a history window. Each rank reuses the notify-dispatch routing-metadata exchange to get the global load matrix, then every rank deterministically computes the identical replication+reroute plan with no extra sync; plan solving and weight replication sit on the critical path between gating and token dispatch. Contrast: EPLB-style deployments reuse recent routing history and rebalance periodically (the paper's baseline interval: every 50 prefill steps / every 3 global batches).
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims written from the saved paper text.
   - anchor `§4.2 Forward ¶1` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "Given this exact load, every rank deterministically computes an identical replication and reroute plan with no extra synchronization."
   - anchor `§4.2 Forward ¶2` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "As a result, planning and weight replication both stay on the critical path."
   - anchor `§8.1 Baselines` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "We use 50 prefill steps and 3 global batches as the rebalancing frequency for serving and training, respectively."
2. Algorithm (quota-driven planner, replication-only; main experts are never moved): binary-search the smallest per-rank load threshold tau between the target rank load (balancing coefficient 1.01) and the initial max rank load; for each tau run a greedy feasibility oracle that visits overloaded ranks by descending excess and their experts by descending load, moving as much load as fits to the admissible rank with the largest slack, subject to a per-rank redundant-slot budget, no duplicate expert on one rank, and a minimum useful quota per new replica (1024 tokens). Accepted transfers create a replica AND its quota; a replica exists only if it carries >= 1024 tokens. Reroute then fills quotas locally first and splits the residual proportionally; tokens go to the first instance whose cumulative quota covers them.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims written from the saved paper text.
   - anchor `§5.1 Quota Construction` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "UltraEP binary-searches between the target rank load and the initial maximum rank load, and runs a greedy feasibility oracle for each probe."
   - anchor `§4.1 Replication Only` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "UltraEP adopts replication-only balancing."
   - anchor `Table 1 notation` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "Minimum useful quota of a newly created replica (set to 1024 in this work)."
   - anchor `Table 1 notation` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "Target balancing coefficient (set to 1.01 in this work)."
3. Cost of planning: solved ON DEVICE in a single cooperative thread block of one SM (warp-parallel probes, shared-memory state). In the paper's simulations averaged over MoE/EP/redundancy settings solving time is 0.111 ms (UltraEP) vs 0.153 ms (EPLB) per layer-plan, i.e. -27.4%; replica count 45 vs 107 (-57.9% redundant slots); result imbalance 1.03 vs 1.19. Weight replication adds a per-layer transfer on the critical path (relay-tree fan-out holds ~0.28 ms for hot experts; 3.1-5.5x faster than torch.distributed/DeepEP-based transfer). Redundant slots reuse one buffer across layers (Qwen3-235B: 3.3 GB -> 36 MB weight per slot per rank).
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims written from the saved paper text.
   - anchor `§5.3 GPU-Native Solving` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "With one cooperative thread block on a single streaming multiprocessor (SM), it stages the load matrix and placement state in shared memory"
   - anchor `§8.5 Balancing Quality` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "At the same time, UltraEP saves solving latency by 27.4 %, consumes 57.9 % fewer redundant slots, and reduces token traffic by 3.9 % with locality."
   - anchor `§4.1 Cross-Layer Buffer Reuse` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "this reduces a single redundant slot from 3.3 GB weights and 6.6 GB gradients to 36 MB and 72 MB per rank"
4. Headline numbers (vendor/authors, 106B-671B bf16 on 64-GPU racks, 128-256 GPUs): 94.3% of force-balanced ideal averaged over training+prefill (94.6% training, 93.9% prefill), 1.49x over no balancing, residual imbalance 1.30-4.01 -> 1.01-1.04; prefill 1.56x vs SGLang and 1.29x vs history-based EPLB; training +42% vs Megatron-LM against +20% for history-EPLB, +12% LPLB, +29% EPLB with exact load; UltraEP beats exact-load EPLB by 5-24% in prefill.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims written from the saved paper text.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "reducing the final inter-rank imbalance from 1.30–4.01 to 1.01–1.04"
   - anchor `§8.2 Training` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "EPLB, LPLB, EPLB, and UltraEP improve throughput by 20 %, 12 %, 29 %, and 42 % over Megatron-LM, respectively."
5. The authors measure that history-based balancing is stale on frontier MoEs: hot experts drift between consecutive batches even within one domain, and on recorded loads (EP=64) EPLB with a 50-batch prefill interval 'can even worsen imbalance', creating new stragglers. Their EPLB-with-exact-load ablation (EPLB placement + round-robin reroute, same transport) lands between history-EPLB and UltraEP in training (+29% vs +20% vs +42%), separating a prediction-lag loss from a planning-objective loss.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims written from the saved paper text.
   - anchor `§3 Limitations of History-Based Balancing` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "When the realized load deviates from the statistics used to derive the expert layout, EPLB can even worsen imbalance, creating spikes and new stragglers."
   - anchor `§3 Serving Prefill` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "Even within a single domain, the hot experts drift from one batch to the next"
   - anchor `§8.1 Baselines` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "to isolate the benefit of our exact-load quota solving, we replace UltraEP’s planning with standard EPLB and round-robin reroute while keeping the communication mechanism unchanged."
6. Quota floor and replication-only design are scale-dependent: the argument that reordering main experts is not worth it rests on large-EP leaving only 2-4 main experts per rank; the 1024-token quota floor presumes thousands of tokens per layer-step. Neither premise holds for single-socket CPU decode at 1-4 tokens per step (6 selected experts of 384 for DS41, one token each), so the planner cannot be carried over to decode as-is; its prefill-like regime (chunked prefill, T in the thousands) is the only one it speaks to.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims written from the saved paper text.
   - *narrowed*: Wave 4 (intake-2141#record repo dive): the released planner overrides the paper 1024-token quota floor with a hard-coded dynamic floor (placement.cu:L434/L821 @ 1474022); decode untested in paper and repo.
   - anchor `§4.1 Replication Only` @ arXiv 2606.04101v3 (cs.DC, 18 Jun 2026; v1 2 Jun 2026): "This reordering-free design is effective because large-EP reduces the number of local main experts per rank (often two or four)."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 (wave 3, Stage-2b): ingested and dived in one pass; settled that UltraEP plans from current post-gating load (not a history window), is replication-only with a threshold-search quota planner at ~0.11 ms per layer on one GPU SM, targets prefill/training and explicitly excludes decode, and that its own ablation separates prediction-lag from planning-objective loss. 2026-10-20 second reader (independent agent): re-find 18/18; support yes 5 / partial 2 / no 0. Partials carried to Stage 3 as anchor gaps (claims 3 and 4: planner timing/replica counts and the §8.2 prefill serving ratios are in the paper but not in the quotes); no contradiction. 2026-10-20 (wave 4, from the intake-2141#record repo dive, UER-A3): claim 6's 1024-token replica quota floor is NARROWED - the released planner overrides the paper's floor with a hard-coded dynamic floor (placement.cu:L434/L821 @ 1474022); decode behaviour remains untested in both paper and repo.

### Verdict

Cite for: (a) exact post-gating planning is the alternative to history-window planning, and the authors measured the history window failing on non-stationary load in PREFILL and TRAINING; (b) the quota-driven threshold-search algorithm and its admission floor; (c) a solve time of 0.1 ms per layer on one GPU SM. Do NOT cite for any decode claim (the authors exclude decode), for any CPU/AMD/MXFP4 number, or as evidence that per-step planning helps at 1-4 tokens per step: the quota floor of 1024 tokens and replication-only argument assume large token batches. Do not carry 1.56x/1.29x/94.3% as EPYC expectations. It does change RT-6: add an oracle exact-load arm.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `moe-routing-tap-and-locality-measurement.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): RT-6 amend: add an ORACLE exact-load arm (zero-lag per-step quota planning, replication budget priced in copy bytes) beside the history-window policies, so the replay separates prediction-lag loss from placement loss; decode-only T in {1,3} is expected to show no headroom and that is the point
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): RT-6 amend: add a prefill-chunk regime row (T in the thousands) because that is the only regime the paper speaks to
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Knowledge-only: cite for the claim that history-window EPLB is stale on non-stationary load, not for any decode or CPU number

## intake-2139#record — MoonshotAI/MoonEP - perfectly balanced expert-parallel communication via dynamic redundant experts (online planning kernel + weight prefetch)

- **URL**: https://github.com/MoonshotAI/MoonEP
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: moe_optimization, inference_serving, hardware_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2135#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. MoonEP is an expert-parallel communication library that makes every rank receive exactly S x K tokens (fixed capacity NvS_capacity = S*K) by planning a small number of redundant experts ONLINE from the CURRENT router outputs on every layer call and prefetching their weights before expert compute. There is no history, moving average or predictor anywhere in the code (grep for history/predict/moving average/stale finds only an unrelated scratch comment).
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims written from the saved source.
   - anchor `README.md:L7 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "A small number of redundant experts is planned online from the current router outputs and prefetched before expert computation; their gradients are reduced back to their home ranks in the backward pass."
   - anchor `moonep/api.py:L265 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "NvS_capacity = S * K"
1. How the 'hot' set is found: there is no hot-expert detector or threshold. Each rank contributes its per-expert token counts (tokens_per_expert, [E]); the planning kernel gathers all ranks' counts, computes per-home-group (rank) token totals, and runs a greedy rebalancer: repeatedly take the group with the largest surplus over the capacity S*K and the group with the largest deficit, and move min(deficit) tokens from surplus to deficit (one pass fills the receiver to capacity). Then, per home group, tokens to be shifted are allocated to the owner's experts largest-remaining first. Which experts get replicated is therefore whatever carries the shifted token mass this step.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims written from the saved source.
   - anchor `moonep/planning.py:L655 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "if k < R: balance[j] = group_tokens[k] - CAP"
   - anchor `moonep/planning.py:L663 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "if surplus <= 0 or deficit >= 0:"
   - anchor `moonep/planning.py:L667 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "# shortfall; refill deficit_rank back to CAP in one shot."
2. How weights are moved: after planning, every destination rank holds epn = E/R prefetch slots (a process-global pool shared by all layers); each rank pushes its own local expert weights into the prefetch slots of peers that were assigned tokens for them (experts_to_copy table: all experts with a positive count that are not local, compacted by global expert id). The copy is a persistent warp-specialised TMA kernel (one producer warp, six store warps, 128x128 or 128x256 tiles, up to 6 stages) over NVLink with a trailing cross-rank barrier; packed MXFP4 (uint8 weights plus scale tensors) is supported alongside bf16. Constructive planner invariant: a destination rank receives tokens from at most one remote home group, so epn slots always suffice.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims written from the saved source.
   - anchor `moonep/planning.py:L818-L819 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "# With epn slots every remote expert fits. Compact all positive"
   - anchor `moonep/planning.py:L830 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "selected = (cnt > 0) & ("
   - anchor `README.md:L58 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "The planner moves experts from at most one remote home group to each destination rank, so these slots cover every remote expert segment."
   - anchor `moonep/api.py:L879 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "bf16 for unquantized experts, uint8 for packed MXFP4."
   - anchor `moonep/prefetch.py:L40-L45 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "Persistent multi-tensor 2D TMA local-expert prefetch push."
3. Overheads: the README states planning is 'negligible' and that the plotted comparison already includes MoonEP's planning and prefetch kernels, but gives no numbers in text; the only quantitative results are two bar charts (comm time vs DeepEP v2 across router imbalance maxvio, and end-to-end training iteration time) on 8 H20 GPUs at EP=8, which were NOT viewed. Planning runs as ONE cooperative-grid kernel with software grid barriers and a cross-rank barrier; the rebalancing loops in the Phase B/C section sit under an 'if rank == 0' branch (planning.py L584; how the result reaches the other ranks was not read). Memory overhead stated in the README is epn expert weights per projection in total, not per layer; training adds fp32 gradient reduce buffers.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims written from the saved source.
   - anchor `README.md:L56 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "Each rank's prefetch pool is process-global and shared by all layers, so the extra physical cost is `epn` expert weights per projection in total, not per layer."
   - anchor `README.md:L13 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "Both benchmarks run on H20 with EP=8, sweeping the router imbalance:"
   - anchor `moonep/planning.py:L584 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "if rank == 0:"
4. Hardware and scope: NVIDIA only (NVLink peer-to-peer writes, TMA, multimem, CuTe DSL); a Zhenwu PPU port is listed as coming. Built for training (gradient reduce back to home ranks) and forward dispatch; S is tokens per rank per microbatch, assumed large enough that S*K capacity is meaningful. Prompt/decode small-batch behaviour is not discussed.
   - *unaffected*: New entry; claims written from the saved source.
   - anchor `README.md:L33 @ d86fda4` @ MoonshotAI/MoonEP @ d86fda40329de4002e0cade6817df7b4b5512ee5: "- NVIDIA GPU"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 (wave 3, Stage-2b): ingested and dived in one pass; settled that MoonEP has no history or hot-expert detector (exact current counts, rank-level greedy rebalancing to capacity S*K), moves weights by owner-push TMA copies into epn layer-shared prefetch slots (MXFP4 supported), and publishes no overhead numbers in text. 2026-10-20 second reader (independent agent): re-find 14/14; support yes 5 / partial 0 / no 0; the reader re-checked the two negative claims (no history/predictor) by grep; corrections applied: none required.

### Verdict

Cite for the design: exact-load per-layer planning with a strict per-rank token capacity, owner-push prefetch of redundant experts into a layer-shared slot pool, MXFP4-capable. Do NOT cite for any speed, overhead or balance number (none in text; charts unviewed), for hot-expert detection (there is none: it is a rank-level greedy over current counts), or as a decode design. For EPYC the transferable pieces are the slot-pool layout and the "replicate only what the shifted token mass needs" rule; the per-step PCIe fetch itself loses at T=1-4 (0.65 ms to fetch an 18.8 MB expert at 28.9 GB/s vs ~0.11 ms to read it from DRAM on the CPU).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `moe-routing-tap-and-locality-measurement.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Knowledge-only for RT-7: the per-step dynamic prefetch design does not fit a PCIe-attached accelerator at decode (see UE/MO ledger arithmetic); the slow-adaptive cache is the RT-7 hit-rate curve
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Port-map note for CAFE-7: MXFP4 uint8 weight+scale prefetch layout is a ready reference for a gfx90a expert-slot pool
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Operator-review candidate: a prefill-only dynamic residency experiment on the MI210 once DS41-T7 prices tokens-per-expert break-even

## intake-2140#record — ROCm/mori - MORI: AMD GPU+RDMA communication framework (MORI-EP dispatch/combine, MORI-IO KV transfer, SHMEM, UMBP tiered KV pool)

- **URL**: https://github.com/ROCm/mori
- **Verification**: dive-verified

- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-2135#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Scope: MORI (Modular RDMA Interface, ROCm/mori, MIT) is an RDMA+GPU communication framework with four libraries: MORI-EP (MoE dispatch/combine, intra- and inter-node), MORI-IO (p2p transfer for KV cache, backends RDMA / XGMI / TCP), MORI-CCL (collectives, plus an SDMA all-gather) and MORI-UMBP (tiered DRAM/HBM/SSD KV pool with a distributed master), all on an OpenSHMEM-style symmetric-memory layer (MORI-SHMEM). It is the AMD counterpart of DeepEP (the DeepEP README names it as the ROCm backend).
   - *unaffected*: first-read in Stage-2b; no Stage-1 claim to correct
   - anchor `README.md:L23 @ 37cdd8e` @ 37cdd8e9f8a3b55d0e133ac8e0e15ebeae3ca62b: "MORI-EP** (MoE dispatch & combine kernels), **MORI-IO** (p2p communication for KVCache transfer), and **MORI-CCL** (collective communication)"
1. Supported hardware as stated: GPUs MI308X, MI300X, MI325X, MI355X are supported for EP/IO/SHMEM and MI450X is under development; NICs Pollara (AMD AINIC), ConnectX-7 and Thor2 are supported. gfx90a / MI210 / MI250 is NOT supported and not mentioned anywhere in the tree (0 hits for MI210, MI250 or gfx90a). The architecture allow-list is hard-coded in three places: setup.py _supported_arch_list = [gfx942, gfx950, gfx1250], cmake MORI_SUPPORTED_ARCHS = gfx942;gfx950, and the JIT toolchain; an unsupported local GPU is simply skipped by the detector and the build falls back to a fat binary for the supported list, so on MI210 the package would build but carry no gfx90a code. README prerequisite is ROCm >= 6.4 (our host is ROCm 6.2). Verdict on the platform question: the shipped binary is unusable on MI210 as-is; this is a port, not a decline (see ledger).

2. Hot paths are RDMA-NIC- or XGMI-peer-centric: EP dispatch/combine kernels run on symmetric memory across >= 2 GPUs (tests are EP8/EP16/EP32; tuned configs exist from ep2 upward and are tuned for gfx942/gfx950), inter-node uses GPU-initiated RDMA (IBGDA) through a vendor direct-verbs library (libionic / libmlx5 / libbnxt_re). Source comments tie behavior to gfx942 (hipMemSetAccess failure after several separate VMM allocations) and the FP4 paths require gfx950 OCP FP4 instructions. Low-latency (128 tokens): MI300X EP8 dispatch 35 us (134 GB/s), combine 47 us (204 GB/s); EP16-V1-LL 76 / 122 us. EP32 rows on MI300X are flagged by the README itself as stale (previous kernel version). These are 8-GPU XGMI-mesh numbers, not a single-GPU or PCIe number.

4. MORI-IO vendor-reported number: GPU Direct RDMA READ, pairwise, 128 consecutive transfers, 1 GPU, MI300X + Thor2, reaches 48.4 GB/s peak at 1 MiB messages and 36 us floor latency for 8-byte messages; labelled by the README as a preview benchmark. IO API has MemoryLocationType = {Unknown, CPU, GPU} so host memory is a first-class registered buffer, and the XGMI backend moves data with hipMemcpyAsync / hipMemcpyPeerAsync over per-transfer streams and events (it is a DMA-engine copy, not a GPU-initiated read).

5. The usable intra-node host-memory component is in MORI-UMBP's local tier, not in EP/IO/SHMEM: DRAMTier registers the host tier with hipHostRegister(Mapped|Portable) in pieces (default 1 GiB, async registration above 2 GiB), obtains a per-device alias with hipHostGetDevicePointer, and a HIP gather kernel (GatherFragmentsKernel: flat grid, up to 8 blocks per fragment, 16-byte uint4 loads) copies KV fragments from the host-mapped tier into device buffers GPU-initiated; pageable regions fall back to hipMemcpy. The kernel and registration code use only portable HIP runtime calls (no gfx942 intrinsics, no RDMA), so they are the one piece that ports to gfx90a with no hardware change. Host tier can be split per NUMA node (UMBP_DRAM_NUMA_NODE, MPOL_BIND/PREFERRED, parallel prefault up to 16 workers).

UMBP NUMA-tier measurement (vendor-reported, one MI355X two-socket 8-GPU node, DeepSeek-V4-Pro TP8 in SGLang): splitting a 512 GiB-740 GB DRAM KV tier across both NUMA nodes changed serving throughput by <= 1% (+0.2% at concurrency 128 with hugetlb, STRICT, 3600 s) and TTFT p50 by -0.4%; exposed tier load wait was about 1.3% of forward time either way; startup of a 740 GB tier fell from 36-274 s to 10-90 s. 7. ## intake-2141#record — Dots-Infra/UltraEP - exact-load expert replication and token-reroute library (reference implementation of arXiv 2606.04101)

- **URL**: https://github.com/Dots-Infra/UltraEP
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: moe_optimization, inference_serving, hardware_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2138#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Planner kernel: the paper's quota solver ships as one CUDA kernel, quota_placement_solve_kernel (csrc/kernels/placement.cu), launched with ONE thread block of 128 threads per NVLink domain (grid = number of domains), per-domain loads and replica-occupancy bitmaps in dynamic shared memory. Inside it, thread 0 sets the binary-search bounds (lo = ceil(mean rank load x balance threshold), hi = current max rank load) and warps run the greedy feasibility oracle; default tolerance ULTRA_EP_QUOTA_ORACLE_EPS=0.01 gives the 1.01 final imbalance. This matches the paper's description (paper claim 2 of NEW-ultraep) and is the only quota-producing planner in the repo.
   - *unaffected*: Confirms intake-2138#record claim 3 (quota-driven planner) in code and adds the launch geometry (one 128-thread block per NVLink domain).
   - anchor `csrc/kernels/placement.cu:L1625 @ 1474022` @ Dots-Infra/UltraEP @ 1474022 (2026-10-15, v1.0.0 line): "const dim3 grid(num_nvl_domains);"
   - anchor `csrc/kernels/placement.cu:L19 @ 1474022` @ Dots-Infra/UltraEP @ 1474022 (2026-10-15, v1.0.0 line): "static constexpr int QUOTA_SOLVER_THREADS = 128;"
   - anchor `csrc/kernels/placement.cu:L963 @ 1474022` @ Dots-Infra/UltraEP @ 1474022 (2026-10-15, v1.0.0 line): "smem_bs_hi = max(max_rank_load, smem_bs_lo);"
1. No host/CPU fallback for the quota planner. The only CPU code is csrc/kernels/legacy/placement_cpu.cpp, an EPLB-style greedy (repeatedly add a replica to the expert with the highest load/replica-count, then place replicas heaviest-first on the least-loaded rank without a duplicate), reached only via the legacy_placement flag (default false). It copies the loads device-to-host, solves, copies back, ignores min_tokens_per_replica/oracle_eps/locality/kernel_stage, and writes ZERO quotas. It is a different (history-style) algorithm, not a host build of the paper's planner. Everything else (reroute, weight_sync, grad_reduce) is CUDA; install requires NVIDIA SM90 or SM100 and NVLink/P2P.
   - *unaffected*: New fact: no CPU/host quota planner exists; legacy host path is a different EPLB-like algorithm with zero quotas.
   - anchor `csrc/kernels/legacy/placement_cpu.cpp:L238 @ 1474022` @ Dots-Infra/UltraEP @ 1474022 (2026-10-15, v1.0.0 line): "CUDA_RUNTIME_CHECK(cudaMemsetAsync(logical_instance_quota, 0, quota_bytes, stream));"
   - anchor `csrc/kernels/legacy/placement_cpu.cpp:L198 @ 1474022` @ Dots-Infra/UltraEP @ 1474022 (2026-10-15, v1.0.0 line): "(void)min_tokens_per_replica;"
   - anchor `README.md:L35 @ 1474022` @ Dots-Infra/UltraEP @ 1474022 (2026-10-15, v1.0.0 line): "NVIDIA GPUs with SM90 or SM100"
2. Small-token / decode behaviour is NOT documented and NOT tested: README and blog scope the system to training and serving PREFILL, every shipped test uses synthetic Zipf loads at 8k tokens per rank, and the SGLang serving integration is stated to be experimental and unreleased. One refinement of the paper's quota-floor premise: the 1024-token ULTRA_EP_QUOTA_MIN_TOKENS_PER_REPLICA default exists, but the shipped kernel hard-codes use_dynamic_q_floor = true and in the main oracle loops replaces the floor by max(1, ceil(need / feasible_slots)), so the floor is not a fixed 1024 in the code path. What the code does at 1-3 tokens per step is therefore unknown; it would be a planner over loads of 0/1/2 tokens per expert with GPU weight-copy replicas, which is not evidence for a CPU decode policy.
   - *narrowed*: Narrows intake-2138#record claim 6 / paper claim: the 1024-token floor is overridden by a hard-coded dynamic floor in the shipped kernel (placement.cu:L821, L434); the decode-regime exclusion still stands on the authors' own scope statement and on weight-copy cost, but no small-token behaviour is documented.
   - anchor `csrc/kernels/placement.cu:L821 @ 1474022` @ Dots-Infra/UltraEP @ 1474022 (2026-10-15, v1.0.0 line): "const bool use_dynamic_q_floor = true;"

3. License is MIT (Copyright 2026 PKU-DASYS and Dots-Infra), so the algorithm may be re-implemented or ported with notice. The repo states v1.0.0 (2026-07-15) is deployed in the authors' production MoE training; the HEAD commit (2026-10-15) swaps NVSHMEM for PyTorch symmetric memory, i.e. the code is still moving.
   - *unaffected*: License fact for the port.
   - anchor `LICENSE:L3 @ 1474022` @ Dots-Infra/UltraEP @ 1474022 (2026-10-15, v1.0.0 line): "Copyright (c) 2026 PKU-DASYS and Dots-Infra"
4. Usable as a standalone offline replay reference for RT-6? Not as shipped: tests/test_solving.py imports the compiled CUDA extension ultra_ep._C and calls torch.cuda, so running it needs an NVIDIA SM90/SM100 GPU and a build (not available here, and we do not execute source code in any case). As a READING reference it is good: the algorithm is fully in placement.cu plus paper section 5, and a numpy port of the threshold search with a greedy oracle is small enough to be an ORACLE arm beside the history-window EPLB policies. The legacy host solver (about 150 lines of pure C++, solve_on_host) is extractable but is the EPLB-like arm we already plan to port from deepseek-ai/EPLB policy.py, so it adds nothing.
   - *unaffected*: Answers the RT-6 reference question: readable, not runnable here.
   - anchor `README.md:L160 @ 1474022` @ Dots-Infra/UltraEP @ 1474022 (2026-10-15, v1.0.0 line): "`test_solving.py` runs a single-GPU placement/reroute solving with synthesized Zipf-distributed expert loads at target rank-level imbalance ratios (max/mean)."
5. Repo-reported planner cost (vendor-reported, EP64, 256 experts, 4 master + 2 redundant per rank, topk 8, 8k tokens per rank, bf16, NVIDIA SM90): update_placement 0.067 / 0.072 / 0.078 ms for initial imbalance 1.51 / 2.01 / 3.03, final imbalance 1.01 in all three, 38 / 46 / 51 replicas; weight_sync 0.19-0.32 ms at about 706-715 GB/s critical-path bandwidth; README states exposed overhead under 300 us at large EP. Planner time grows only mildly with imbalance but the weight copy is the larger and bandwidth-priced term, which is the part that has no cheap CPU analogue except a memcpy between node-local expert buffers.
   - *unaffected*: Repo-side planner timings (0.067-0.078 ms) differ from the paper's simulation figure (0.111 ms) because settings differ; neither is an EPYC number.
   - anchor `README.md:L13 @ 1474022` @ Dots-Infra/UltraEP @ 1474022 (2026-10-15, v1.0.0 line): "Exposed overhead is under 300 µs at large-scale EP, typically 1%–2% of end-to-end step time."
   - anchor `README.md:L178 @ 1474022` @ Dots-Infra/UltraEP @ 1474022 (2026-10-15, v1.0.0 line): "| `update_placement` | 0.067 ms | 0.072 ms | 0.078 ms |"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 (wave 4, Stage-2b): ingested and dived in one pass; settled that the quota planner is GPU-only with no host build, that the 1024 quota floor is overridden by a dynamic floor in code (narrowing intake-2138#record claim 6), license MIT, and that it is a reading reference (not a runnable replay) for RT-6. 2026-10-20 second reader (independent agent): re-find 14/14; support yes 6 / partial 0 / no 0; corrections applied: none required. Main dropped a malformed bare-list depends_on [intake-2138#record]: the repo claims rest on the repo, not on the paper entry. STAGE-2 CLOSE-OUT (wave budget of 4 exhausted): dive-surfaced github.com/Dots-Infra/Megatron-LM-UltraEP DECLINED (training integration, EPYC does not train; planner already read here) and github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepEP/tree/hybrid-ep DECLINED (branch of indexed intake-2135#record; NVLink/RDMA hybrid dispatch has no single-host consumer; monitor only if RT-6a shows a dispatch-side gap) - both carried to the Stage-3 plan for operator confirmation.

### Verdict

Cite for: (a) the planner is a single-block GPU kernel with no CPU build of the quota algorithm, and the only host code is an EPLB-style legacy greedy; (b) MIT license, readable algorithm, so RT-6a can port the oracle to numpy; (c) the 1024-token floor is a default overridden by a dynamic floor in the shipped kernel. Do NOT cite for any decode, CPU, AMD or small-batch behaviour (none exists in the repo), and do not treat the repo as runnable here (SM90/SM100 only).

## intake-2142#record — deepseek-ai/LPLB - Linear-Programming-Based Load Balancer for MoE expert parallelism

- **URL**: https://github.com/deepseek-ai/LPLB
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: moe_optimization, inference_serving, hardware_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2138#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. LP formulation (read from the NVRTC-compiled solver template minilp.cu): one small LP per redundancy group of ranks. Variables per (rank, redundant slot): the share x of that expert's current workload kept on its original rank and the share y sent to its replica, with x + y = 1; plus per-rank slack and one scalar t. Constraints: for every rank, fixed (non-replicated) load + kept shares + received replica shares + slack = t, so t bounds every rank's load. Objective: minimise t (cost 1 on t), i.e. min-max rank load over total token count. Each redundant expert is shared between exactly TWO ranks (planner.py asserts at most one replica per expert, max_logcnt == 2), so the flow graph is the redundancy topology (cube / hypercube / torus).
   - *unaffected*: Confirms the min-max LP and the at-most-one-replica constraint, read from the solver template.
   - anchor `lplb/resources/csrc-tmpl/minilp.cu:L258-L259 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "// Slack variable representing difference between max load in group and // current rank's load (coefficient 1)"
   - anchor `lplb/resources/csrc-tmpl/minilp.cu:L262 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "// Max value variable (coefficient -1)"
   - anchor `lplb/planner.py:L173 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "assert max_logcnt == 2"
   - anchor `lplb/planner.py:L55 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "This ensures that each redundant expert is shared between exactly two ranks."
1. Solver and cost: a hand-written primal interior-point style method on a fixed sized dense system, run inside ONE CUDA block per redundancy group with cuSolverDx (Cholesky posv) and cuBLASDx matmuls; exactly 5 iterations are executed (no convergence loop), with a Big-M column (cost 1000). A group is accepted only if d_max < 0.1 and residual < 0.05 and the Big-M variable is near zero; otherwise the group falls back to a fixed 0.5 / 0.5 split, and a counter of accepted groups is returned (the test asserts it equals the group count). README states about 100 us for intra-node solving, longer inter-node, and calls this possibly non-negligible for small batches.
   - *unaffected*: Adds solver internals (5 fixed IPM iterations, Big-M, fallback 0.5) that the README does not state.
   - anchor `lplb/resources/csrc-tmpl/minilp.cu:L335 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "for (int step = 0; step < 5; step++) {"
   - anchor `lplb/resources/csrc-tmpl/minilp.cu:L291 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "c[iv] = 1000;"
   - anchor `lplb/resources/csrc-tmpl/minilp.cu:L397 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "result[i] = 0.5;"
   - anchor `README.md:L70 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "The solver takes ~100 µs for intra-node optimization (longer for inter-node), which may be non-negligible for small batches."
2. Per-microbatch mechanics: (1) count selected logical experts per SM from the current batch's index tensor (count_idx), (2) sum workloads across the EP group by all-reduce or by DeepEP/NVSHMEM internal communicators, (3) solve the LP for the replica split ratios, (4) map_idx assigns each token's expert to the original or replica physical expert so that cumulative per-SM counts follow the ratio. Which experts get replicas is decided separately by EPLB reordering on a history (the heaviest, per topology); replicas are static between reorderings, only the SPLIT is per batch.
   - *unaffected*: Replicas are chosen by EPLB-style reordering on history; only the split is per batch.
   - anchor `README.md:L65 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "Experts to be replicated are selected via EPLB (reordering only, no replication)."
   - anchor `lplb/planner.py:L235 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "return self.solver.map_idx("
3. Authors' own limitations (README, no benchmark numbers given anywhere in the repo): 'early research stage, performance improvements still under evaluation'; balances only total token count and ignores the non-linearity of grouped-GEMM time; solver time may matter for small batches; under extreme global imbalance LPLB may do worse than EPLB because it never assigns multiple replicas of one expert. Topologies require at least 2 experts per GPU (Cube/Torus) or 16 GPUs (Hypercube).
   - *unaffected*: Author-stated limitations.
   - anchor `README.md:L5 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "LPLB is currently in the early research stage, and performance improvements are still under evaluation."
   - anchor `README.md:L71 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "Under extreme global load imbalance, LPLB may perform worse than EPLB due to differences in assigning redundant experts (LPLB avoids assigning multiple replicas to the same original expert)."
   - anchor `README.md:L69 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "The current planner balances only total token count, not accounting for non-linearity in grouped matrix multiplication time costs, which may lead to suboptimal performance."
4. Evidence quality: the repo is a single initial commit (2025-11-19), MIT-licensed (DeepSeek), NVIDIA-only (CUDA >= 12.6.3 with cuSolverDx/cuBLASDx via download-mathdx.sh, SM version baked into the template), and its tests assert only synthetic balance coefficients (tolerances 1.01-1.1 on normal-distributed loads), not throughput. The only throughput number about LPLB anywhere in this intake is a competitor's: UltraEP's paper reports +12% over Megatron-LM in training versus +42% for UltraEP (vendor-reported, cited from the UltraEP patch, not from this repo).
   - *unaffected*: No numbers; the only throughput figure is a competitor's.
   - anchor `README.md:L11 @ 0490f79` @ deepseek-ai/LPLB @ 0490f79 (2025-11-19, single Initial commit): "CUDA Toolkit >= 12.6.3 (with cuSolverDx dependencies)."
5. EPYC mapping verdict: an LP reroute is not meaningful for single-socket CPU decode as built. (a) Under the canonical interleave recipe every thread computes every selected expert, so there are no ranks to balance; the NUMA-placement alternative (CPU15 style) has 4-12 bins, tokens per step T = 1-3, and per-expert loads in {0,1,2}, where a fractional split is meaningless and the one-replica-per-expert choice reduces to picking the less loaded of two copies (power-of-two-choices) per token. (b) For a CPU + MI210 split the reroute is one scalar: given a GPU-resident subset with hit share h, the split that equalises CPU and GPU time has a closed form (the A7 ceiling h* = 1.6 TB/s / (1.6 TB/s + 165 GB/s) = 0.91 is unreachable at 39-63 resident experts per layer), so the LP degenerates to 'send resident hits to the GPU'. The meaningful content is the formulation (min-max over a replica-edge graph), not the solver.
   - *unaffected*: EPYC mapping verdict is derived, not from source.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 (wave 4, Stage-2b): ingested and dived in one pass; settled the LP formulation, the fixed-iteration IPM and its feasibility fallback, that replicas are history-selected with only the split per batch, and that the repo contains no performance numbers. 2026-10-20 second reader (independent agent): re-find 14/14; support yes 4 / partial 1 / no 0. The partial (claim 4's LPLB '+12% over Megatron-LM') was checked by main: the figure is in the UltraEP paper (intake-2138#record, saved paper text: 'EPLB, LPLB, EPLB++, and UltraEP improve throughput by 20%, 12%, 29%, and 42% over Megatron-LM'), and the claim already attributes it as competitor-reported; claim stands. Main dropped a malformed bare-list depends_on [intake-2138#record] (counterfactual test fails). STAGE-2 CLOSE-OUT: surfaced github.com/deepseek-ai/EPLB is already intake-2134#record (not a new source).

### Verdict

Cite for: the min-max replica-edge LP formulation, the at-most-one-replica-per-expert constraint, the authors' stated limitations, and a fixed-5-iteration on-device IPM costing about 100 us. Do NOT cite for any throughput claim (none exists), and do not carry the LP to CPU decode: with 4-12 bins and 1-3 tokens per step the decision is integral and trivial. It does change RT-6: report the fractional LP floor beside the integral policy as a bound.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `moe-routing-tap-and-locality-measurement.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): RT-6b: fractional LP floor arm in the RT-6 replay (bound only)
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Knowledge-only otherwise; do not port the solver

## intake-2143#record — Do Agent Optimizers Compound? A Continual-Learning Evaluation on Terminal-Bench 2.0

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14004
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: harness_optimization, benchmark_methodology, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1995#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Two-phase protocol on 22 hard Terminal-Bench 2.0 tasks (12 then 10 new): all three optimizers beat the baseline on the tasks they were optimized on, but once new tasks arrive GEPA transfers below the unoptimized baseline and Meta Harness fails to improve in a second round.
   - *unaffected*: Stated as the paper's own result; the held-out label is the reader's: only Transfer is held-out.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2607.14004v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 c3180f215947: "GEPA’s optimized agent transfers below the unoptimized baseline, Meta Harness transfers well but fails to improve further once given a second optimization budget"
1. Lifelong average pass rate (mean of Phase-1, Transfer, Final): RELAI-VCL 76.4%, GEPA 66.0%, Meta Harness 64.6%, baseline 58.7%.
   - *narrowed*: Average mixes one in-sample stage, one held-out-plus-in-sample stage and one in-sample stage; it rewards fit to the optimized tasks.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2607.14004v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 c3180f215947: "(76.4% vs. 66.0% for GEPA, 64.6% for Meta Harness, and 58.7% for the baseline)"
2. Authors conclude that gains compounded only where regression control (reject any candidate that regresses previously solved tasks) was built into the optimization loop; the paper itself hedges this as an interpretation, not a general claim.
   - *narrowed*: The paper itself calls the generalization-filter reading speculative and not a general claim; n=22, one run.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2607.14004v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 c3180f215947: "Our key observation was that optimization gains compounded only when regression control was built into the optimization loop"
   - anchor `§6.6 Interpretation` @ arXiv:2607.14004v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 c3180f215947: "We state this as an interpretation consistent with the evidence in this evaluation, not as a general claim about regression-aware optimization"
3. GEPA's prompt overfit mechanism: it grew from 5 to 103 to 195 lines, organized as named per-task lessons carrying task ids and verifier-specific literals.
   - anchor `§4.2 GEPA` @ arXiv:2607.14004v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 c3180f215947: "GEPA’s prompt grows from 5 lines before optimization to 103 lines after Phase 1 and 195 lines after Phase 2, with the added text organized as named, per-task lessons"
4. Protocol scale: 12 Phase-1 plus 10 Phase-2 tasks, 2 trials per task, 200 rollouts per phase per method, one run per method, GPT-5.5 as baseline LLM and proposer.
   - anchor `§3.3 Metrics` @ arXiv:2607.14004v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 c3180f215947: "Every task is evaluated twice per agent"
   - anchor `§5.1 Task split` @ arXiv:2607.14004v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 c3180f215947: "Phase 2 introduces 10 additional tasks with an agent timeout of at most 1800 seconds."
   - anchor `§5.2 Budget` @ arXiv:2607.14004v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 c3180f215947: "In Phase 1, each optimizer receives 200 rollouts to optimize the agent harness against the 12 Phase 1 tasks."
5. RELAI-VCL reaches 65.0% on the unseen Phase-2 tasks alone, about 15 points above baseline.
   - *narrowed*: Correct as stated for RELAI-VCL; on the same tasks the derived Meta Harness figure (70%) is higher, so 'strongest transfer' is a union-level statement.
   - anchor `§6.2 Transfer` @ arXiv:2607.14004v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 c3180f215947: "its pass rate on the unseen Phase 2 tasks alone is 65.0%, about 15 points above the baseline despite never having been optimized against those tasks"
6. The method and protocol assume repeatable tasks and reliable verifiers; the authors list this as a limitation.
   - anchor `§7.2 Repeatability` @ arXiv:2607.14004v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 c3180f215947: "assume that tasks can be executed repeatedly and that reliable evaluators or verifiers are already available to score each rollout"
7. Meta Harness in Phase 2: every candidate performed worse than the existing agent and its final score fell to 59.1%.
   - *unaffected*: Quote re-found; text does not say whether the 59.1 drop is real or measurement variance.
   - anchor `§6.3 Phase 2` @ arXiv:2607.14004v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 c3180f215947: "Every candidate generated during the second optimization round performed worse than the existing agent, and its score fell to 59.1%."
   - anchor `App. C Meta Harness` @ arXiv:2607.14004v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 c3180f215947: "is the candidate that was ultimately kept; the released artifacts do not include the rejected candidates from that round."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b read the full paper from disk. (1) Corrects the description in intake-886#record contradicting_evidence: that entry says a SECOND optimization pass gave GEPA negative transfer; the paper shows GEPA's negative transfer (54.5 vs 56.8 baseline) follows the FIRST pass (Phase 1) when evaluated on the union, and after the second pass GEPA is at 72.7 (in-sample). Its label 'independent' narrows to independent of the GEPA and Meta-Harness authors only; the authors develop RELAI-VCL. (2) intake-1995#record notes (c) say GEPA and Meta-Harness gains 'fail to compound': narrowed to GEPA overfits then recovers in-sample, Meta Harness stalls. (3) Index-owner action, not applied here. 2026-10-20 SEA-Eval (arXiv:2604.08988, intake-2156#record) dived: no general cross-episode metric needed in HIL-2; conditional memory-feature leg only. Declined (wave-1 dive): github.com/relai-ai/Continual-Learning-Terminal-Bench (kept candidates only, rejected ones not released; decline for now, operator may override), arXiv 2604.04247 Combee, arXiv 2603.18718 MemMA, arXiv 2601.18226 Yunjue agent, arXiv 2509.04642 Maestro (no EPYC consumer). 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-24: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: arXiv 2604.04247 Combee; 2603.18718 MemMA; 2601.18226 Yunjue; 2509.04642 Maestro -> decline: no EPYC consumer; HC-FLG-06: Operator-flag source: relai-ai/Continual-Learning-Terminal-Bench repository (recommend monitor on a continual-stream pilot suite) -> M16. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Cite for: a worked two-phase protocol shape (tune on T1, test on arriving T2 without re-tuning, then re-tune), and one vendor-authored data point that a regression-rejecting selector held gains where prompt-only evolution overfit to task-specific lessons. Do not carry: that RELAI-VCL or in-loop regression control is shown superior (n=22 tasks, one run, in-sample stages, author-developed method), that GEPA 'fails to compound' (it recovered to 72.7 in-sample after Phase 2; its failure was negative transfer after the FIRST pass), or that the study is independent of a compared method's developers.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive] Proposal for operator review: add a held-out arriving-task requirement and a literal-leak guard to the HIL design; see DIVE.md rows A1, A2, A4.

## intake-2144#record — Harness Updating Is Not Harness Benefit: Disentangling Evolution Capabilities in Self-Evolving LLM Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30621
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: harness_optimization, agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1995#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Harness-updating is flat in base capability: across seven evolvers the best-to-worst gap in mean gain is at most 3.1 pp on any benchmark, and the 9B evolver tops SkillsBench (3.8 pp vs Opus 4.6 2.3 pp).
   - *narrowed*: Gap numbers re-found; they are single-run pass-rate differences averaged over three anchor agents, close to one binomial SE on SWE.
   - anchor `§4.2 Obs. 1` @ arXiv:2605.30621v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 66bdf06e2598: "The gap between the best and worst evolver is at most 3.1 percentage points (pp) on any benchmark, and no model wins across benchmarks."
   - anchor `§4.2 Obs. 1` @ arXiv:2605.30621v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 66bdf06e2598: "The smallest evolver, Qwen3.5-9B, posts the highest gain on SB (3.8 pp), exceeding both Opus 4.6 (2.3 pp) and Qwen3-235B (1.5 pp)."
1. Post-evolution score is dominated by the task-solving agent's base capability, not by evolver identity (within-agent spread at most 5.1 pp vs a 36.0 pp between-agent gap on MCP-Atlas).
   - *unaffected*: Between-agent gaps are tens of pp, far larger than any noise argument.
   - anchor `§4.2 Obs. 2` @ arXiv:2605.30621v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 66bdf06e2598: "The within-agent spread across seven evolvers is at most 5.1 pp (Qwen3-235B), small against the 36.0 pp gap between the Opus and Qwen3-235B base capabilities."
2. Harness-benefit is non-monotonic in base capability: on SWE-bench Verified the gain peaks at Qwen3-235B (19.3 pp) while Qwen3-32B gains 4.4 and Opus 4.6 gains 2.6.
   - *narrowed*: Clear on SWE and MCP; the authors call SkillsBench noisy in the low-base regime.
   - anchor `§4.3 Obs. 1` @ arXiv:2605.30621v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 66bdf06e2598: "On SWE, the gain peaks at Qwen3-235B (19.3 pp), while the weaker Qwen3-32B gains only 4.4 pp and the stronger Opus 4.6 gains only 2.6 pp."
3. Weak-tier models fail to activate harness artifacts (SkillsBench skill-load rate 0.251 for Qwen3-32B and 0.446 for GPT-OSS-120B vs about 0.96 for strong models), and loading is not sufficient (Qwen3-235B loads at 0.961 but follows at 0.350).
   - *unaffected*: Rates re-found in Table 2; SkillsBench only.
   - anchor `§4.3 Obs. 2` @ arXiv:2605.30621v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 66bdf06e2598: "Tab. 2 shows that the skill-load rate is near ceiling for Opus 4.6, Sonnet 4.6, and Qwen3-235B (0.957–0.961), but drops to 0.446 for GPT-OSS-120B and 0.251 for Qwen3-32B."
   - anchor `§4.3 Obs. 2` @ arXiv:2605.30621v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 66bdf06e2598: "Qwen3-235B provides the cleanest separation between activation and adherence: its skill-load rate is 0.961, nearly identical to Opus 4.6, yet its HFR is only 0.350."
4. Adherence to a loaded harness decays over the trajectory for weak models (Qwen3-32B 0.52 to 0.13; Opus 4.6 0.89 to 0.80).
   - anchor `§4.3 Diagnosis` @ arXiv:2605.30621v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 66bdf06e2598: "Qwen3-32B drops sharply from 0.52 after harness loading to 0.13 at final validation"
5. Authors recommend investing capability budget in the task-solving agent rather than the evolver, and training for harness invocation and long-horizon instruction following.
   - *narrowed*: A recommendation inferred by the authors from skills/prompts/memory edits, not a measured result.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2605.30621v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 66bdf06e2598: "These findings suggest investing capability budget in the task-solving agent rather than the evolver"
6. Evaluation is in-situ: the same task stream drives evolution and evaluation; each task is scored under the harness from before its own evidence is used.
   - anchor `App. B.3 In-situ` @ arXiv:2605.30621v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 66bdf06e2598: "The pass rate of any individual task is thus not influenced by harness updates derived from that task itself."
7. Authors concede the SkillsBench low-base-rate trend is noisier.
   - anchor `App. D.2` @ arXiv:2605.30621v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 66bdf06e2598: "The SB trend is noisier in the low-base regime."
8. Harness-benefit is defined as the maximum pairwise gain over three anchor evolvers.
   - *unaffected*: Definition re-found; consequence for estimator bias is the reader's point.
   - anchor `§3.3 Metrics` @ arXiv:2605.30621v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 66bdf06e2598: "In practice, we estimate this as the maximum pairwise gain across a fixed anchor evolver set"
9. Evolvable surfaces are skills only (SWE-bench, SkillsBench) and skills plus prompts plus memory (MCP-Atlas); tools and evaluation files are read-only.
   - anchor `§4.1 Implementation` @ arXiv:2605.30621v1 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 66bdf06e2598: "The evolvable components are skills for SWE-bench Verified and SkillsBench, and skills, prompts, and memories for MCP-Atlas."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b read the paper from disk. Stage-1 knowledge came only from a search summary and a sibling paper's citation (intake-1995#record notes). Confirms the title, authors and two headline findings; narrows the non-monotonicity claim to SWE and MCP (authors' own concession for SkillsBench) and records that 'flat' is measured by gaps near one SE. 2026-10-20 SEA-Eval (arXiv:2604.08988, intake-2156#record) dived: no general cross-episode metric needed in HIL-2; conditional memory-feature leg only. Declined (wave-1 dive): github.com/relai-ai/Continual-Learning-Terminal-Bench (decline for now, operator may override), arXiv 2604.04247 Combee, arXiv 2603.18718 MemMA, arXiv 2601.18226 Yunjue agent, arXiv 2509.04642 Maestro (no EPYC consumer).

### Verdict

Cite for: (a) a factorial design that separates who writes harness updates from who benefits, (b) skill-load rate and adherence as diagnostics that explain a null harness gain, (c) one multi-benchmark data point that a small evolver wrote skills yielding gains of similar size to a frontier evolver. Do not carry: that evolver size never matters (edits were skills/prompts/memory, three benchmarks, single runs, differences near one standard error), that the weak-tier result transfers to EPYC's lineup (none tested), or that harness-benefit is established as non-monotonic on SkillsBench.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/episodic-memory-integrity.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive] Proposal for operator review: per-model feature-invocation diagnostics for HIL-2 and a local-editor arm in the HIL pilot; see DIVE.md rows B1, B3.

## intake-2145#record — TTHE: Test-Time Harness Evolution

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08124
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1995#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. TTHE adapts an agent's executable harness at test time with no labels and no weight updates: on each unlabeled batch, G branches are evolved for R rounds by coding-agent proposers that read execution traces, a judge commits one harness using traces plus execution-derived proxy signals, and the committed harness initializes the next batch. Solver, proposers and judge are the same frozen LLM in different harnesses.
   - *unaffected*: Label-free loop confirmed against sec. 2 and sec. 3.4; the judge may additionally probe and re-execute, which the Stage-1-free summary of the idea (judge reads proxies only) would omit.
   - anchor `§2 Problem Setting, Information available to adaptation` @ arXiv:2607.08124v2: "Final-answer labels, reference solutions, hidden-test outcomes, and final evaluation scores remain inaccessible to all adaptation components and their traces; evaluation feedback is not passed to subsequent batches."
   - anchor `§3.4 Evolution and Selection (Judge)` @ arXiv:2607.08124v2: "During selection, it may acquire additional evidence through probes or candidate re-execution"
   - anchor `§3.4 Evolution and Selection` @ arXiv:2607.08124v2: "The solver, proposers, and judge share the same frozen LLM."
1. The label-free evidence is deliberately NOT collapsed into a scalar: execution health, round-trip consistency (output re-described and compared to the input) and public-test pass rate are shown to proposers and judge together with the candidate code and traces. The judge may also probe or re-execute candidates during selection.
   - *unaffected*: Three proxy signals and the no-scalar design confirmed in sec. 3.3.
   - anchor `§3.3 Execution Evidence` @ arXiv:2607.08124v2: "TTHE therefore presents the signals together with candidate code and the underlying execution traces, rather than aggregating them into a single scalar objective."
   - anchor `§6 Limitations` @ arXiv:2607.08124v2: "Successful execution or agreement among candidates can coexist with semantic errors, making it difficult to distinguish effective program improvements from changes that merely satisfy available proxies."
2. On six execution-grounded benchmarks TTHE beats a fixed ReAct-style baseline harness in every one of three runs with deepseek-v4-flash: BIRD hard slice 12.7 -> 48.7, LiveCodeBench 29.4 -> 37.8, SWE-bench Verified 21.7 -> 34.2, DS-1000 34.7 -> 42.7, Terminal-Bench 2.1 55.1 -> 67.8, claw-eval 48.9 -> 69.4 (graded). A ten-run execution-result vote with the fixed harness reaches only 16.0 on BIRD.
   - *narrowed*: Gains are real in all 18 run pairs, but each set is 30-89 tasks, BIRD/SWE/DS-1000/LCB are hard subsets selected before adaptation (BIRD from baseline failures), and the 16.0 vote control matches only nominal harness executions, not proposer/judge tokens.
   - anchor `§5.2 Main Results` @ arXiv:2607.08124v2: "TTHE outperforms the fixed baseline on all six benchmarks in each of the three reported runs"
   - anchor `App. A Task Construction, BIRD` @ arXiv:2607.08124v2: "Selection is conditioned on baseline failures; the scores describe this recovery-oriented subset, not full-benchmark performance or a controlled ranking of different backbones."
   - anchor `§5.3 Repeated sampling without evolution` @ arXiv:2607.08124v2: "Total computation also depends on harness-specific token and tool use, as well as TTHE’s proposal and judging overhead."
3. Most of the lost accuracy is selection, not generation: on the BIRD reference run the judge commits 50.0 while the final-pool oracle reaches 64.0 (a 14-point, net seven-task selection regret) and the all-candidate oracle 70.0.
   - *unaffected*: Selection regret 14 points (7 net tasks) confirmed; it is a single-run diagnostic.
   - anchor `§5.4 Candidate availability and selection` @ arXiv:2607.08124v2: "The fourteen-point gap between the judge and the final-pool oracle exceeds the six-point gain from admitting earlier candidates, highlighting selection among already generated programs as a major source of lost performance."
4. Conditional theory for trace-triggered edits: net gain = repairs minus false-trigger regressions minus off-trigger harm (Proposition 1); if the trigger is sound-for-rejection and the edit exactly local, correctness cannot fall (Theorem 1), but clearing a failure flag alone cannot certify a positive gain.
   - *narrowed*: Theory is conditional on binary correctness, an exact-locality coupling and a sound-for-rejection trigger; the paper says the quantities are analysis-only and not accessible to the loop, and not claimed for graded claw-eval.
   - anchor `§4.3 Theorem 1` @ arXiv:2607.08124v2: "Thus failure-flag clearance alone cannot certify a strictly positive correctness gain."
5. Evolved harnesses transfer and accumulate: the DeepSeek-evolved BIRD harness raises MiMo V2.5 32 -> 56 and GPT-5.5 36 -> 68 without further evolution, and carrying the harness across batches (50.0) beats resetting each batch (44.0).
   - *narrowed*: Transfer evidence is the BIRD slice only; accumulate-vs-reset is a 3-task difference in one run; v1 conceded prequential scoring was not done and v2 removed that sentence.
   - anchor `§5.4 Cross-model harness transfer` @ arXiv:2607.08124v2: "The gains show that a batch-specific harness can benefit execution models other than the one that evolved it."
   - anchor `§5.3 Cross-batch accumulation` @ arXiv:2607.08124v2: "These results indicate that current-batch adaptation yields substantial gains, while retaining evolved program structure provides a useful initialization for subsequent adaptation."
   - anchor `§6 Limitations (v1 text, dropped in v2)` @ arXiv:2607.08124v1: "This directly measures within-batch adaptation, but does not by itself establish forward generalization"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b (expanded from intake-1995#record): promoted to dive-verified from a fresh Stage-1+2 read. v2 vs v1: v2 adds three-run means, Terminal-Bench 2.1, the ten-run vote control, cross-model transfer, Theorem 1 and App. E; v1 single-run numbers equal the v2 reference run (BIRD 12.0 -> 50.0). v2 dropped the v1 limitation that evaluation is transductive and that prequential scoring is untested; the transductive protocol statement remains in v2 sec. 2. No Stage-1 entry existed to overturn. 2026-10-20 correction from the wave-2 repo dive (junnie00/TTHE entry): dive row T8 is closed. The judge is prompt-level, the round-trip check is trace text, and the execution-health composite is not in the live loop.

### Verdict

adopt_patterns, pattern-level only. The mechanism (trace-reading proposers + a probing judge over unaggregated proxy signals, carried forward batch to batch) is a precise precedent for the HIL label-free arm and settles the novelty question for Harness^2; the evidence for its size of gain is thin (n=30-89 per set, hard slices selected on baseline failures, one API backbone, compute not matched) and the paper itself locates the dominant loss in label-free selection, which is the part EPYC cannot yet do.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Cite TTHE as the antecedent that label-free, trace-driven, per-batch harness evolution with execution-derived proxy signals and a probing judge predates Harness^2 (2610.14224) by three months; owner of the HIL-2 item-4 evidence note: handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Adopt the pool-oracle selection-regret diagnostic (committed score vs oracle over the candidate pool, gold used only post hoc) as a HIL reporting column; keep/revert stays on a protocol-cited paired measurement.

## intake-2146#record — Recursive Harness Self-Improvement

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15524
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1995#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. RHI represents the harness as a prompt-level text specification of a multi-agent coding workflow (agent roles, instructions, output-to-orchestrator "contracts", orchestrator-subagent "hops", auxiliary rules) and refines it per task from pairwise LLM-judge feedback against its immediately preceding output, using the accumulated comparison history as a momentum signal.
   - *unaffected*: Method description confirmed (sec. 3.2, Algorithm 1).
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2607.15524v1: "which represents the harness as a prompt-level specification of the agent loop and iteratively refines it using pairwise feedback over its own revision history"
1. Cost framing: the trajectory-local objective needs exactly one new execution and one pairwise judgment per iteration (Theta(1)), versus Theta(m^2) for finite-population harness search and Theta(M^2) for the ideal population objective.
   - *unaffected*: Theta(1) per-iteration cost confirmed from Table 1/sec. 3.1; it is an accounting identity of the objective, not a measured total.
   - anchor `§3.1 RHI is computationally lightweight` @ arXiv:2607.15524v1: "In contrast, RHI compares the current harness only with the cached output of its immediate predecessor, requiring exactly one new execution trace and one pairwise evaluation per iteration."
   - anchor `§2.2 Existing methods` @ arXiv:2607.15524v1: "Harness optimization is therefore worthwhile only when its search overhead is far smaller than that of population-based methods."
2. On 30 synthetic ML-research tasks (quant finance, robotics, pharmacy), a few iterations lift a high-effort agent above same-family xhigh/max/ultracode settings under pairwise LLM-judge evaluation: sonnet-4.6 after 2 iterations (20 of 30 wins vs max), opus-4.7 after 1, opus-4.8 after 2.
   - *narrowed*: Win-rate claims hold under the authors' protocol only: same 30 tasks iterated and evaluated, in-loop judge family equals evaluation judge family, mean of two or six judge configurations, bars read from figures (not extracted).
   - anchor `§5.3 Claim 1` @ arXiv:2607.15524v1: "These results indicate that a few RHI iterations can raise performance beyond the ceiling at which same-family test-time scaling saturates under our benchmark and evaluation protocol."
   - anchor `App. C pairwise history summary` @ arXiv:2607.15524v1: "Judge model: gpt-5.5"
   - anchor `§4.2 LLM-as-a-judge evaluation` @ arXiv:2607.15524v1: "gpt-5.5 with maximum reasoning effort and opus-4.7 (or opus-4.8) with xhigh reasoning effort"
3. Reported cost: opus-4.8-high with the iteration-2 harness costs 60% less than opus-4.8-ultracode (normalized 1.69 vs 4.15) and 23% less than max, with cache read/write down 64% and 32%; output tokens stay roughly flat (1.42-1.81 for opus-4.8).
   - *narrowed*: The "up to 60%" is normalized per-run cost of the final-iteration run vs ultracode; cumulative cost of the 2 iterations, judge and optimizer is not reported (NOT-FOUND-IN-SOURCE).
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2607.15524v1: "exceeding the corresponding maximum-reasoning-effort setting while reducing inference cost by up to 60%"
   - anchor `§5.2 Normalized cost, output token count, and cache read/write usage` @ arXiv:2607.15524v1: "For each task we record these three quantities, normalize each by the corresponding base-agent value, and average across tasks."
4. Mechanism hypothesis: gains come from task-specific communication contracts that cut redundant context propagation (a learned sparsity over inter-agent information flow), not from longer reasoning; supported by embedding/mutual-information diagnostics the authors call correlational, plus an information-theoretic hypothesis for the implicit objective.
   - *narrowed*: The authors state the embedding analysis is correlational and that contract/hop emphasis follows from the optimizer prompt; opus-4.7 output tokens rise with performance (inconclusive).
   - anchor `§5.4 Claim 2` @ arXiv:2607.15524v1: "For the Opus-4.7 baseline, the evidence is inconclusive."
   - anchor `§6.2.2 Harness-component evolution` @ arXiv:2607.15524v1: "while emphasizing that the embedding analysis is correlational rather than causal."
   - anchor `App. A Q2 Observation 1` @ arXiv:2607.15524v1: "multi(default) achieves lower Elo than single(default)despite incurring substantially higher normalized cost."
5. The harness optimizer is an LLM that receives the current harness, repository evidence and a summary of the pairwise history including the judge's verbatim rationale and a recurring-issues checklist, and must return a structured change record (changes, rationale, evidence used, expected impact, verification checks); it is not shown the evaluation prompt.
   - *unaffected*: Optimizer inputs and output schema confirmed in App. C; the evaluation prompt is withheld but judge rationales are provided verbatim.
   - anchor `App. C Harness optimizer prompt` @ arXiv:2607.15524v1: "You must ground changes in concrete evidence from submission artifacts and comparison history."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b (expanded from intake-1995#record): fresh Stage-1+2 read; no earlier entry existed. Recorded limits not stated by the authors: judge-in-loop equals judge-at-eval; cumulative improvement cost absent; no limitations section.

### Verdict

adopt_patterns, pattern-level only and low relevance. The cost framing and the change-record schema are reusable; the performance and cost headline is not decision-grade for EPYC: pairwise LLM-judge outcomes are observations under the measurement policy, the judge in the loop equals the judge at evaluation, there is no held-out task set, and the cumulative improvement cost is not reported. Its one directly portable hypothesis (explicit output contracts between orchestrator and subagents reduce prefill and context propagation) is flagged for the delegation lane as a monitor item.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/delegation-context-preassembly.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Pattern-level: use the per-iteration cost accounting (N_trace + N_pair) and the structured change record as reference when specifying HIL step records; do not adopt a pairwise LLM-judge as a keep/revert gate.

## intake-2147#record — Adaptive Auto-Harness: Sustained Self-Improvement for Agentic System Deployment on Open-Ended Task Streams

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01770
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-1995#record
