## intake-2155#record — Arbiter: Detecting Interference in LLM Agent System Prompts

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.08993
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: llm_prompting, harness_optimization, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2008#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. In the hand-labeled Claude Code v2.1.50 system prompt (56 blocks), 21 interference patterns were found (4 critical mandate-vs-prohibition contradictions, 13 scope overlaps, 2 priority ambiguities, 2 implicit dependencies) and 20 of 21 are statically detectable without an LLM.
   - *narrowed*: 20/21 static is over one prompt, hand-labeled by the author with no independent labeler or precision/recall; 13 of 21 are minor scope overlaps.
   - anchor `§3.2 body (Results on Claude Code paragraph)` @ arXiv:2603.08993v2: "Of these 21 patterns, 20 (95%) were statically detectable"
   - anchor `§5.6 Limitations (directed analysis)` @ arXiv:2603.08993v2: "no precision or recall of the evaluator against an independent labeling is reported"
1. Prompt architecture (monolith / flat / modular) correlates with failure class - stated by the author in v2 as a hypothesis from one prompt per class, not an established relationship.
   - *narrowed*: Downgraded by the author to a hypothesis in v2.
   - anchor `§5.6 Limitations (one prompt per architecture class)` @ arXiv:2603.08993v2: "The correlation is a hypothesis to be tested on further prompts per class, not an established relationship."
2. The claim that different models find categorically different findings is confounded by the map-passing instruction not to repeat prior findings; the same-model control was not run (v2 correction).
   - *overturned*: The v1 complementarity claim is retracted by the author in v2 (confounded by non-repetition instruction); only the restated hypothesis survives.
   - anchor `§4.5 Multi-Model Complementarity` @ arXiv:2603.08993v2: "This observation is confounded with the protocol."
3. The 152 scourer findings have no recorded per-finding human verdict, so no precision can be reported (v2 correction; v1 had claimed human review and cross-model confirmation).
   - *overturned*: The v1 mitigations (human review, cross-model confirmation) are retracted by the author; the 152 are unverified.
   - anchor `§5.6 Limitations (Scourer findings are LLM-generated)` @ arXiv:2603.08993v2: "no per-finding verdict was written down, so no precision figure can be reported for the 152"
4. Structural rules (priority markers, verbatim duplication) are Python predicates with no LLM call and no cost; LLM rules run only on pre-filtered block pairs (about 100-200 of roughly 15,680).
   - *unaffected*: Verified in the paper text and in the repo docstring and rules (structural vs LLM detection column).
   - anchor `§3.2 Rule application` @ arXiv:2603.08993v2: "Structural rules (priority markers, verbatim duplication) run as Python predicates—no LLM call, no cost."
5. Total API cost for the cross-vendor campaign was $0.27.
   - *unaffected*: Billing-verified per paper; lower bound (subscription pass excluded).
   - anchor `§4.1 Quantitative Summary` @ arXiv:2603.08993v2: "Total API cost across all three analyses: $0.27"
6. Repo experiment (author-run, one judge model): the structural oracle fires on 2 of 15 frame-collision pairs and 1 of 15 hard negatives, while an LLM neutral reader (Claude Haiku 4.5) fires on 15 of 15 collisions and 3 of 15 hard negatives; easy controls 0 of 10 for both.
   - *narrowed*: Counts computed from the results json; one judge model, 40 hand-built items; the author flagged the earlier 0.00 FP as an easy-control artifact.
   - anchor `experiments/hard_negatives.py:L3-L5 @ a4fa30f (docstring); counts from experiments/hard_negatives_results.json` @ fsgeek/arbiter@a4fa30f1771700ae8f21241c78490095cb4c62dd: "Attacks the load-bearing cell of the prior result: the 0.00 FP rate was measured only on EASY controls."
7. Repo experiment (author-run): when a prompt says ALWAYS use TodoWrite and elsewhere NEVER use TodoWrite, the prohibition side won in 315 of 315 trials across 7 Anthropic/Google models and 3 temperatures; one case, no local models.
   - *narrowed*: LLM-authored research note in the repo, one contradiction case, API models only, keyword classifier with a documented retraction history.
   - anchor `docs/cairn/T3_20260223_executor_mode.md Strand 2 @ a4fa30f` @ fsgeek/arbiter@a4fa30f1771700ae8f21241c78490095cb4c62dd: "TodoWrite mandatory-vs-forbidden: B (SKIP_TODO) = 100% for all 7 models, all 3 temperatures. 315/315 trials."

### Verdict

Cite the structural-rule inventory and the v2 corrections; never cite the 152 findings, the 'different models find different things' claim, or the architecture-failure correlation as established. Never cite 'a compiler would catch 95%' without noting it is 20 of 21 hand-labeled patterns in one prompt with no independent labeler. It supports a warn-only delta lint for PromptForge, not a blocking gate and not a contradiction-free certificate.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): AutoPilot PromptForge: add a deterministic, warn-only interference lint (verbatim duplicate lines or blocks, same-scope mandate vs prohibition) over mutated prompt content, reporting only findings the mutation INTRODUCED relative to the original; ship it as part of the HIL-3 import-safe guard extraction.

## intake-2156#record — SEA-Eval: A Benchmark for Evaluating Self-Evolving Agents Beyond Episodic Assessment

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08988
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: harness_optimization, agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2144#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. SEA-Eval proposes success rate (SR) and token consumption (T) as primary metrics, and the sequential convergence of T across a task stream as the criterion that separates genuine from pseudo-evolution.
   - *narrowed*: The convergence-of-T criterion is the authors' proposal; the data do not separate learning from caching or from agent/model differences.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "demonstrating that success rate alone creates a capability illusion and that the sequential convergence of T is the key criterion for distinguishing genuine evolution from pseudo-evolution."
   - anchor `§4.2 Metric Design (intro paragraph)` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "if T fails to converge across sequential tasks, this indicates that the agent has fallen into pseudo-evolution regardless of its SR performance."
1. The benchmark evaluates over fixed length-5 task sequences: correlated (R1,R2,R3 identical repeats then similar variants A2,A3), orthogonal (identical or similar task after 3 unrelated interference tasks), and a noisy condition that preloads 20 irrelevant skills.
   - *unaffected*: Sequence and noise-condition definitions re-found verbatim; design description only.
   - anchor `§4.3.2 Correlated Sequences` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "Correlated Sequences (R1→R2→R3→A2→A3): The leading segment repeats the identical instance A1"
   - anchor `§4.3.2 Orthogonal Sequences` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "Orth-Same (A1→B→C→D→A1) re-executes the identical instance after interference, measuring pure retention robustness; Orth-Similar (A1→B→C→D→A2) executes a different instance after interference"
   - anchor `§4.3.3 Noisy Condition` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "The agent is preloaded with 20 randomly selected skills that have no scenario overlap with the current task sequence"
2. Long-term metrics are defined as adjacent-step differences (Keff, KSR, Kalign) and, in experiments, as ratios of first-vs-later-round tokens (Keff(Evo)=(T_R1-T_R3)/T_R1, Keff(Conv)=(T_R1-T_R2)/T_R1).
   - *narrowed*: Adjacent-difference Keff/KSR/Kalign are defined but the experiments use ratio forms; Kalign is not reported.
   - anchor `§5.4 Metric definitions` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "Keff(Evo)=(TR1−TR3)/TR1, Keff(Conv)=(TR1−TR2)/TR1"
   - anchor `§4.2.3 Keff` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "A Keff(k) sequence that converges monotonically toward zero from the negative side indicates successful experience consolidation"
3. Empirical result: five agents have widely different token consumption; the intro says success rates are 'comparable (85%-96.7%)' while Table 2 shows Hermes at 65.0%; OpenClaw's R3 tokens are 17.6x GenericAgent's.
   - *narrowed*: SR range quoted as comparable (85%-96.7%) excludes Hermes at 65.0% in the same table.
   - anchor `§5.2 Table 2 + text` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "Even after three learning rounds at the R3 stage, OpenClaw (1119K) remains 17.6× that of GA (64K)."
   - anchor `§1 Introduction` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "While the frameworks attain comparable success rates (85%–96.7%), their token consumption differs by an order of magnitude"
   - anchor `§5.2 Finding (3)` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "Hermes represents a notable counterexample: even on tasks not terminated by timeout"
4. Authors concede token numbers for Claude Code and Codex are partly prompt-cache hits and that a 15-minute timeout was applied to only three of the five agents.
   - *unaffected*: Both concessions re-found verbatim.
   - anchor `§5.3 Finding 2` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "ostensibly “low consumption” partially derives from cache hits rather than genuine strategy optimization."
   - anchor `§5.2 footnote 7` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "a 15-minute timeout mechanism was applied when testing Hermes, Claude Code, and Codex."
5. Distillation failure and retrieval failure produce the same token signature; telling them apart needs strategy-reuse rate or retrieval traces, which black-box evaluation may not expose.
   - *unaffected*: Appendix C text re-found; diagnostic stays a best-effort suggestion with no experiment behind it.
   - anchor `§3.3.3` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "both failure modes produce an identical behavioral signature: execution cost fails to decrease across sequential task variants"
   - anchor `App. C.2` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "the strategy reuse rate: the proportion of execution steps in task t that invoke a strategy retrieved from ℳp rather than generated through zero-shot reasoning."
   - anchor `App. C.3` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "Full diagnostic resolution, however, requires access to internal memory update logs and retrieval traces, which are available in controlled experimental settings but may not be accessible in black-box agent evaluations."
6. The five agents are not run on the same foundation model (Claude Opus 4.6 for four, GPT-5.5 for Codex).
   - *unaffected*: Model assignment re-found.
   - anchor `§5.2 setup` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "The foundation model for this sequence is uniformly set to Claude Opus 4.6; Codex adopts GPT-5.5 as an alternative due to architectural constraints."
7. A composite ranking GA > Claude Code > Codex > OpenClaw > Hermes is reported from normalized 8-dimension scores.
   - *narrowed*: Rank depends on the aggregation (median vs mean) and on arbitrary normalization.
   - anchor `§5.4 Table 4 text` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "The rankings are highly consistent across both aggregation methods: GA > Claude Code > Codex > OpenClaw > Hermes."
8. Claude Code is described as the only agent that keeps optimizing on similar tasks 'after orthogonal interference' (Stab-Sim -38.9%).
   - *narrowed*: The cited -38.9% reproduces as (A3-A2)/A2 from Table 2, a correlated-stream increment, not an orthogonal-interference measurement.
   - anchor `§5.4 text on Stab-Sim` @ arXiv:2604.08988v3 HTML (LaTeXML), sha256 862f6808b824: "it is the only agent that continues optimization on similar tasks after orthogonal interference (Stab-Sim raw change rate of −38.9%)"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b read the paper from disk (v3, with v1 diffed). No Stage-1 entry existed; the only prior knowledge was the title and a one-line citation in the 2605.30621 dive. Outcome: the paper does NOT establish a cross-episode metric that HIL-2 must adopt (see DIVE.md). For the wave-1 entries: the question 'does HIL-2 need a cross-episode metric beyond held-out transfer' is answered no for the general checklist, with one conditional leg for memory-accumulating features; this should be recorded in their dive_corrections by main. 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-FLG-05: Operator-flag source: GenericAgent 2604.17091 (recommend monitor under the first re-distil trigger) -> M16. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Cite for: (a) a fixed-order sequence design (repeat, then variant, then interference after unrelated tasks) and a congested-memory condition as ways to expose memory-dependent effects, (b) the observation that distillation failure and retrieval failure share one token signature and need a reuse-rate or retrieval-trace diagnostic. Do not carry: that token convergence is a validated criterion for 'genuine evolution' (no memory-off control, caching confound, single runs, agents on different models), the composite ranking, the Table 3 stability numbers as orthogonal-interference results, or any claim that HIL needs a benchmark-style cross-episode score; the benchmark measures online-memory agents while HIL tunes offline prompt/parameter artifacts.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversational-memory-eval-instrument.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/tool-output-compression.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive] Proposal for operator review: do not add a general cross-episode metric to HIL-2; add one conditional memory-feature leg and a cache-net token column; see DIVE.md rows C1-C4.

## intake-2157#record — Masking Stale Observations Helps Search Agents -- Until It Doesn't: A Regime Map and Its Mechanism

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.00408
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: context_management, agent_architecture, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2151#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The accuracy gain from rule-based observation masking (keep the K=5 newest observations verbatim, replace older ones with a placeholder that keeps tool name and arguments, exempt tool-call errors) is an asymmetric inverted-U against the model's own no-management accuracy: +6.2 to +6.6 plateau under BM25 (recall <=0.55), peak +11.7 for a strong retriever plus a mid-accuracy model (Qwen3.5-35B-A3B + AgentIR, 62.9% -> 74.6%), and <=0 to +3.8 once no-management accuracy passes ~70% (Tongyi-DeepResearch 80.7% -> 79.6%, -1.1).
   - *narrowed*: Inverted-U is real in the table (re-derived every delta from the Acc columns) but the +2.6..+3.8 cells are within plausible noise and the boundary is stated at ~70% without a sweep or CI.
   - anchor `§1 Introduction, findings` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "Across our offline suite, this variance maps into a retriever bottleneck plateau (+6–7 pts), a CM sweet spot (+11.7 pts), and a model-saturated collapse (≤0 pts); this right-hand collapse resonates in live-web environments."
   - anchor `§4 Main Results, Three regimes (iii)` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "Once No-CM accuracy passes 70%, the gain collapses, bottoming at −1.1 for Tongyi-DeepResearch-30B-A3B, which posts the highest No-CM accuracy and recall in the table (80.7%, 0.93) yet is harmed by masking."
1. The regime is set by baseline accuracy ON THE TASK, not by model size or identity: Qwen3.5-35B-A3B and Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (same architecture and size) gain +11.7 vs +3.7; GPT-OSS-120B gains +0.1 on BrowseComp-Plus, +8.0 on xBench (base 70.0%) and loses 4.8 on GAIA-text (72.8 without masking, 68.0 with).
   - *unaffected*: Same-size different-checkpoint contrast (+11.7 vs +3.7) and GPT-OSS-120B across three tasks all reproduce from Table 1.
   - anchor `§4 Main Results, Live web amplifies the collapse` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "The clearest case is GPT-OSS-120B: saturated on BrowseComp-Plus (+0.1 pts), it is harmed by −4.8 pts on GAIA, the largest negative effect we observe."
2. Masking is a token-for-turn trade, never a turn saver: every populated cell adds tool calls (+20.3 to +91.6 per query on BrowseComp-Plus; +68.7 for saturated GPT-OSS-120B for +0.1 accuracy). Fixed (wrong->right) queries use fewer rolling input tokens; broken (right->wrong) queries cost significantly more tokens and turns. Fixes outnumber breaks ~3:1 for the best cell and ~1:1 for GPT-OSS-120B, so a near-zero net delta hides equal help and harm.
   - *unaffected*: Delta calls column and the 3:1 / 1:1 fix-to-break ratio are stated verbatim; Figure 4 itself not numerically readable from HTML.
   - anchor `§4 Main Results, Table 1 caption / (iii)` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "Moreover, tool calls surge when these strong models are run with CM (GPT-OSS-120B, +68.7 per query; DeepSeek-V4-Flash-Max, +57.7 per query)."
   - anchor `App. G.1 Masking buys recall with extra tool calls` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "The “Δ calls/q” column documents a side-effect worth stating plainly: masking lengthens trajectories rather than shortening them."
   - anchor `§5.1 The Trade-Off` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "Second, the two configurations differ in their counts: fixes outnumber breaks roughly 3:1 for Qwen3.5-35B, but only about 1:1 for GPT-OSS-120B, where gains and losses cancel."
   - anchor `§5.1 The Trade-Off` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "First, masking’s benefits are token-efficient, whereas its failures are costly."
3. Mechanism evidence: on three Qwen models (150 BrowseComp-Plus trajectories each) reasoning tokens take 53.7% of per-step attention against 25.6% for tool observations although observations are >85% of context; observation attention is front-loaded (collapses to ~1.7% one turn back, ~0.7% thereafter); agents rarely re-open middle-of-trajectory pages, and masking sharpens a bimodal re-open pattern (latest page or first page).
   - *narrowed*: Attention shares reproduce from text; three Qwen models only; attention mass is a proxy for importance.
   - anchor `§5.3.1 Attention Evidence` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "Summed over all past turns, self-generated reasoning captures 53.7% of the per-step attention budget against only 25.6% for tool observations"
   - anchor `§5.3.2 What staleness means` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "Masking such content reclaims context budget the model had already abandoned, at the cost of optional re-reading behavior that the agent rarely exercises."
4. Masking is only safe because the underlying content stays reachable: a page pool keyed by stable cursor/URL lets the agent re-open masked pages; tool-call errors are exempt from masking (removing the exemption raises the open-error rate 18.6% -> 22.6% on 4B and 20.4% -> 24.6% on 9B, BrowseComp-Plus 100 samples).
   - *unaffected*: Ablation percentages verbatim; sample is 100 queries.
   - anchor `§2.2 Observation Masking` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "The placeholder o~ retains structural information (i.e., which tool was called and with what arguments) but discards the observation content."
   - anchor `§2.3 Scaffold, Page pool` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "Because pages persist in the pool regardless of whether their corresponding observations are masked out in C^t, the agent can seamlessly re-reference any historical page by its cursor without re-issuing an expensive search."
   - anchor `§5.4 Ablation Studies` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "Removing error retention—masking tool-call errors along with stale observations—raises the error rate from 18.6% to 22.6% on 4B and from 20.4% to 24.6% on 9B"
5. DIVE-ADDED scope limits: a single retention window (K=5), a 500-turn cap, temperature 1.0, one run per query, no confidence intervals or seeds, models served with 128K-1M windows and no overflow pressure; the study varies model and retriever, NOT window size, and the authors call the regime map 'descriptive rather than predictive' and masking 'a diagnostic instrument, not a method to be benchmarked'.
   - anchor `§3.2 Baselines` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "In all experiments, we set a 500-turn limit and use an observation retention window of K=5."
   - anchor `App. C Generation configuration` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "all models are evaluated with a maximum generation length of 8192 output tokens per turn and a sampling temperature of 1.0."
   - anchor `Limitations` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "Finally, our regime framework is descriptive rather than predictive."
   - anchor `§2.2 Observation Masking, Why this formulation` @ arXiv:2606.00408v1 (HTML sha256 3d4663c8f3ed1ebcb67527d5db1c43f6db7bfbb590f2465e990ff2f15154d6e7): "This minimality is deliberate: we use masking as a diagnostic instrument for studying when stale-observation content matters to agent performance, not as a method to be benchmarked against more sophisticated context management techniques."
6. DIVE-ADDED: no wall-clock, prefix-cache or prefill measurement is reported; cost is in rolling input tokens and tool calls only.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b combined pass: new entry (no Stage-1 draft). Source revision read: arXiv:2606.00408v1. Narrowings recorded as DIVE-ADDED in key_claims: single window, single run, T=1.0, no CIs, no overflow pressure, no wall-clock. Declines: AgentIR (2603.04384) and BrowseComp-Plus (2508.06600) recorded as retriever/benchmark context only (no EPYC consumer); AgentFold and ReSum already in the index; see DIVE.md surfaced-sources table.

### Verdict

Observation masking is a regime-dependent, turn-increasing, token-saving intervention: large gain for mid-accuracy models fed good evidence, near zero or negative for strong models, with the extra turns paid in every regime. Use it to (a) justify stratifying every EPYC compaction A/B by baseline accuracy and reporting fixed/broken transitions, (b) require turn and rolling-token cost in the report, and (c) add a hard-mask arm. Do not read it as evidence that masking beats summarization or that K=5 is optimal; do not quote its percentages for local EPYC roles or coding tasks.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`

- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [MASK-L2 actionable] A/B requirements text: report extra turns per task and the sum of input tokens over all turns (rolling input tokens), not final context size, plus prefix-cache reuse; cite intake MASK Table 1 delta-calls column and yuj 2.0-3.1x model turns.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [MASK-L3 actionable] Add a 'hard mask, K=5, errors exempt, placeholder keeps tool+args' arm next to the yuj half-life arm (YUJ-L1) and the 1991-L3 staged arm; define the age unit explicitly (tool results vs turns, since one turn can issue several parallel calls).
