## intake-2158#record — Active Context Compression: Autonomous Memory Management in LLM Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07190
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: context_management, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2151#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Focus agent: two added primitives (start_focus, complete_focus). At complete_focus the agent writes a summary (attempted / learned / outcome) that is appended to a persistent Knowledge block at the TOP of the context, and every message since the checkpoint is deleted, giving a sawtooth context.
   - *unaffected*: Mechanism description is consistent between section III-B and the abstract.
   - anchor `§III-B step 4 Withdraw` @ arXiv:2601.07190v1 (HTML sha256 ef45ac3490c88206188456c4e6a7fc28282493dde6dc6b69fbf5aed0795405da): "The system takes this summary, appends it to a persistent “Knowledge” block at the top of the context, and deletes all messages between the checkpoint and the current step."
1. With a prompt that mandates compression every 10-15 tool calls plus injected reminders after 15 calls, Haiku 4.5 on 5 SWE-bench Lite instances used 22.7% fewer total tokens (14,920,555 -> 11,526,418) at identical accuracy (3/5 vs 3/5), averaging 6.0 compressions and 70.2 dropped messages per task.
   - *narrowed*: Arithmetic reproduces (14.92M->11.53M = -22.7%) but N=5, identical accuracy is 3/5 each, and savings are driven by 3 of 5 tasks at 52-57%.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2601.07190v1 (HTML sha256 ef45ac3490c88206188456c4e6a7fc28282493dde6dc6b69fbf5aed0795405da): "Focus achieves 22.7% token reduction (14.9M → 11.5M tokens) while maintaining identical accuracy (3/5 = 60% for both agents)."
2. DIVE-ADDED: the 'autonomous' framing is overstated; the compression cadence is externally prescribed (mandatory workflow, periodic system reminders), while the same paper reports that passive prompting gave 1-2 compressions, ~6% savings and (earlier experiments, no numbers or sample shown) accuracy 60% vs 80%.
   - *narrowed*: Abstract says agent decides autonomously; Section IV-B shows mandatory cadence and system-injected reminders.
   - anchor `§III-B Architecture` @ arXiv:2601.07190v1 (HTML sha256 ef45ac3490c88206188456c4e6a7fc28282493dde6dc6b69fbf5aed0795405da): "Crucially, the agent has full autonomy over when to invoke these tools—there are no external timers or heuristics forcing compression."
   - anchor `§IV-B Aggressive Compression Prompting` @ arXiv:2601.07190v1 (HTML sha256 ef45ac3490c88206188456c4e6a7fc28282493dde6dc6b69fbf5aed0795405da): "ALWAYS call complete_focus after 10-15 tool calls"
   - anchor `§IV-B Aggressive Compression Prompting` @ arXiv:2601.07190v1 (HTML sha256 ef45ac3490c88206188456c4e6a7fc28282493dde6dc6b69fbf5aed0795405da): "Initial experiments showed passive Focus prompting yielded only 1-2 compressions per task with marginal (6%) token savings."
   - anchor `§IV-G Analysis` @ arXiv:2601.07190v1 (HTML sha256 ef45ac3490c88206188456c4e6a7fc28282493dde6dc6b69fbf5aed0795405da): "Without these, Claude Haiku 4.5 compressed only 2.0 times per task with 6% savings."
   - anchor `§IV-G Analysis` @ arXiv:2601.07190v1 (HTML sha256 ef45ac3490c88206188456c4e6a7fc28282493dde6dc6b69fbf5aed0795405da): "Our earlier experiments with passive Focus prompting showed accuracy degradation (60% vs. 80%)."
3. Per-instance results are mixed: savings 18-57% on 4 of 5 instances but +110% tokens on pylint-7080 (136 vs 63 LLM calls; compressions 'occasionally discarded useful context, forcing re-exploration'); accuracy identical per instance (two tasks fail in both arms).
   - anchor `§IV-F Case Study pylint-7080` @ arXiv:2601.07190v1 (HTML sha256 ef45ac3490c88206188456c4e6a7fc28282493dde6dc6b69fbf5aed0795405da): "On pylint-7080, Focus used 110% more tokens than Baseline (4.3M vs. 2.1M), yet both agents passed the test suite."
   - anchor `§IV-F Case Study pylint-7080` @ arXiv:2601.07190v1 (HTML sha256 ef45ac3490c88206188456c4e6a7fc28282493dde6dc6b69fbf5aed0795405da): "The problem required extensive trial-and-error, and Focus’s compressions occasionally discarded useful context, forcing re-exploration."
4. DIVE-ADDED: the evidence is N=5 hand-picked 'context-intensive' instances, one model, one run, no CIs, token count not wall time, and the cost measure is cumulative tokens processed (no prefix-cache or latency accounting).
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2601.07190v1 (HTML sha256 ef45ac3490c88206188456c4e6a7fc28282493dde6dc6b69fbf5aed0795405da): "we evaluated Focus on N=5 context-intensive instances from SWE-bench Lite using Claude Haiku 4.5."
   - anchor `§V-C Limitations` @ arXiv:2601.07190v1 (HTML sha256 ef45ac3490c88206188456c4e6a7fc28282493dde6dc6b69fbf5aed0795405da): "Sample Size: Our evaluation uses N=5 hard instances."
5. DIVE-ADDED: independently reproduced as a training-free baseline inside VISTA (LOCA-Bench, Gemini-3-Flash 128K, n=75, single run): Active Context Compression 36.0% vs ReAct 22.7%, Tool-result Clearing 26.7%, stale-observation masking 28.0%, lossless archive-and-recover 44.0%.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b combined pass: new entry (no Stage-1 draft). Source revision read: arXiv:2601.07190v1. 'Autonomous' narrowed to prompt-mandated cadence (DIVE-ADDED claim 2). No declines.

### Verdict

Active Context Compression (Focus) is a small N=5 feasibility demonstration of agent-initiated, cadence-prompted checkpoint-and-prune; its own text shows the cadence had to be externally mandated. Its value to EPYC is as a named baseline (reproduced by VISTA) and as a design reminder about summary placement and cache cost; its percentages are not evidence for any EPYC role.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/tool-output-compression.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [ACC-L1 knowledge] Cite only as the origin of the agent-initiated checkpoint-and-prune pattern and as a baseline that VISTA reproduced; never quote 22.7% or 3/5.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [ACC-L2 actionable, folded into the ACON-L3 amendment] Any LLM-consolidation arm must declare where the summary is placed (context top vs appended at the tail) and report prefix-cache invalidations; Focus pins the Knowledge block at the top, so every compression changes the first tokens and forces a full re-prefill on a prefill-bound host (EPYC inference from the described design; the paper reports no cache numbers).

## intake-2159#record — TTHE: Test-Time Harness Evolution (companion code repository)

- **URL**: https://github.com/junnie00/TTHE
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2145#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The judge is not code. It is one agentic Claude Code CLI session (Bash, Read, Grep, Edit tools; model calls routed to the same frozen backbone) that reads every candidate's full trace and writes the chosen harness NAME to a file. Selection rules live only in the prompt: re-run and probe, never eyeball, treat consensus as a weak prior, treat proposal cards as untrusted self-reports.
   - anchor `text_to_sql/proposer.py:L543 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "DO NOT EYEBALL. Never judge a candidate right because its SQL or number 'looks right'."
   - anchor `text_to_sql/proposer.py:L547 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "CONSENSUS IS A WEAK PRIOR, NOT PROOF."
   - anchor `text_to_sql/proposer.py:L539 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "treat it as an UNTRUSTED SELF-REPORT: verify every"
1. The LiveCodeBench judge treats public-test pass as a necessary filter, not a ranking signal, and tells the judge to build its own differential tests (same self-generated legal inputs across public-passing candidates; odd one out is suspect), stress runs and a back-translation intent check. The prompt states ~29% of public-passing solutions are hidden-wrong; no data behind that figure ships in the repo.
   - anchor `livecodebench/lcb_proposer.py:L195 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "So DO NOT " f"rank by public-pass count"
   - anchor `livecodebench/lcb_proposer.py:L205 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "DIFFERENTIAL TEST: for a problem, run the public-passing candidates' programs on the SAME self-made"
   - anchor `livecodebench/lcb_proposer.py:L195 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "Measured on this exact task, ~29% of public-passing solutions are hidden-WRONG."
2. Round-trip consistency is a prose back-translation (a frozen-model literal description of the final SQL or code) written into each trace for the judge and proposers to compare against the question. In the live loop it is never a number. A numeric discrepancy score and an AUC harness exist only in text_to_sql/bt.py as an offline diagnostic (gold used only for AUC), and the SQL selector prompt tells the model the back-translation score is one clue, not reliable.
   - anchor `text_to_sql/optimize.py:L256 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "bt_en = recover(schema, sql) if sql else "(no SQL produced)""
   - anchor `text_to_sql/bt.py:L3 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "Signals (all label-free; gold used ONLY for AUC):"
   - anchor `text_to_sql/proposer.py:L256 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "Treat it as ONE clue alongside the executed RESULT and your own DB probing"
3. The 'carried harness cannot regress' gate is pool membership: the incoming incumbent H is among the judge's candidates and a judge failure keeps H. In the SQL judge the `incumbent` argument is assigned but never interpolated into the prompt (dead); the LCB and SWE judges do carry a burden-of-proof-on-the-challenger clause with ties going to the incumbent. There is no mechanical numeric keep/revert gate anywhere in the loop.
   - anchor `text_to_sql/optimize.py:L458 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "H = picked if picked in final_candidates else H # judge failure -> keep the incumbent"
   - anchor `text_to_sql/proposer.py:L521 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "incumbent = tk.get("incumbent") or cands[0]"
   - anchor `livecodebench/lcb_proposer.py:L220 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "The burden of proof is on the challenger: keep `{incumbent}` unless"
4. Control plane: proposers are forbidden to execute any harness; the fixed controller alone runs each child once per batch and writes the official traces. A mechanical `valid_proposal_card` schema check drops a child whose card is malformed, which leaves its branch at the parent. Fixed roles by branch index: conservative repair, independent exploration, adversarial audit.
   - anchor `text_to_sql/proposer.py:L363 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "NEVER execute, import, instantiate, or load any parent, peer, or child harness."
5. An AST/token invariant audit (frozen solver, label-free) is wired into every optimizer's admission check `_loadable` and rejects violating candidates. It fails OPEN: an exception inside the auditor returns True. The claw-eval loop code is not published (slice list only), and optimize.py names helper modules (online.py, online_gold.py, supervised.py, fixability.py, tt.py) that are not in the repository.
   - anchor `README.md:L101 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "The loop code for this domain is **not** included here"
   - anchor `text_to_sql/optimize.py:L502 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "A violating candidate is rejected here, leaving its branch at its parent."
   - anchor `text_to_sql/optimize.py:L511 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "return True # auditor failure must not reject a valid candidate"
   - anchor `text_to_sql/optimize.py:L16 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "Other modules are diagnostics/ablations, NOT this loop: online.py / online_gold.py / supervised.py /"
6. The SWE judge adds a self-consistency forcing function absent from the paper text: before choosing, it must write a candidate x instance verdict matrix (CREDIBLE / NOT-CREDIBLE / UNRESOLVED, no missing cells) and reconcile its named choice against the argmax of its own CREDIBLE counts.
   - anchor `swe/swe_proposer.py:L219 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "BEFORE you choose, write `{choice_path}.matrix`: one ROW per candidate, one COLUMN per instance in"
   - anchor `swe/swe_proposer.py:L222 @ 69a614d` @ 69a614d: "if the harness you are about to name is not the argmax of your own CREDIBLE counts,"

### Verdict

adopt_patterns. The judge protocol is prompt-level and portable to any agentic model with a shell, but selection still rests on LLM judgment, which the paper itself identifies as where accuracy is lost (14-point judge to pool-oracle gap). Patterns adopt; the mechanism does not substitute for a mechanical keep/revert gate.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] For the HIL label-free arm, borrow three judge-protocol elements as design text: incumbent burden-of-proof with ties to the incumbent, a written verdict matrix with an argmax reconcile check, and proposal cards treated as untrusted self-reports. Owner: docs/design/harness-improvement-loop-design-20261006.md HIL-2 (judge protocol rows).
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Do NOT port the fail-open auditor: an exception in an invariant checker must reject, not admit. Check that PromptForge guards fail closed.

## intake-2160#record — Position: Agentic Evolution is the Path to Evolving LLMs

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00359
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2147#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Persistent artifact state is a typed triple: a knowledge registry K (versioned schemas, workflows, exemplars), a tool registry T (executables with signatures and tests) and a validation registry V (unit tests, regression suites, human-review hooks). V is itself editable and grounds the commit decision.
   - anchor `§3 Persistent Artifact State` @ 2602.00359v2: "stores structured or textual artifacts such as schemas, workflows, interface contracts, and exemplars."
   - anchor `§3 Persistent Artifact State` @ 2602.00359v2: "Validation artifacts are themselves editable."
1. The update is a conditional decision: the evolver proposes a delta, a governance gate returns commit or no-op, and the evolve phase is 'triggered asynchronously or when sufficient evidence accumulates'. The paper gives no concrete trigger rule.
   - anchor `§2 Agentic Evolution and Principles` @ 2602.00359v2: "The autonomy principle specifies when to change."
   - anchor `§3 The Solve–Evolve Loop` @ 2602.00359v2: "triggered asynchronously or when sufficient evidence accumulates"
2. On AppWorld (50 train tasks for evolution, 50 test-normal tasks for evaluation, temperature 0.7, no repeats reported) A-Evolve beats APE, AWM and vanilla across five solvers, e.g. Haiku 4.5 TGC 32 -> 64 and Gemini 3 Flash 56 -> 82 (AWM 52), but Sonnet 4.5 86 -> 90 against AWM 88.
   - anchor `Table 1 (§5.1)` @ 2602.00359v2: "Vanilla 32 51.16 86 94.94 42 79.42 80 94.15 56 80.45 APE 30 56.00 80 85.00 44 82.00 82 95.00 52 84.00 AWM 46 65.76 88 97.32 50 86.29 84 94.08 52 87.75 A-Evolve 64 84.31 90 96.47 56 88.21 88 97.02 82 92.05"
   - anchor `§5.1 Datasets` @ 2602.00359v2: "We sample 50 50 tasks from the training split for evolution and 50 50 tasks from the test-normal split for evaluation."
3. Verification is the critical stabilizer: the no-verification ablation degrades most (figure only, no numbers in text). Evolver capacity matters: Table 3 reports Sonnet-4.5 evolver 21 proposals / 4 tools committed / 2 verification failures versus Haiku-4.5 evolver 25 / 1 / 8.
   - anchor `§5.3 Results Analysis` @ 2602.00359v2: "Verification is a critical stabilizer."
   - anchor `App. C.3 Table 3` @ 2602.00359v2: "Sonnet 4.5 21 4 2 (repaired successfully) Haiku 4.5 25 1 8 (3+ repair attempts each)"
4. Evolution-scaling hypothesis: attainable performance rises with evolution-time compute (1 to 12 evolution steps, larger evolver). Evidence is a single curve per setting with no error bars.
   - anchor `§5.4 Scaling Evolution Compute` @ 2602.00359v2: "We vary the evolution step from 1 to 12 steps as a proxy for C evolve"
5. Implementation narrows the governance claim: the Section 3 gate is described as unit tests and regression suites, but the Appendix B.2 verifier validates Python AST syntax, YAML frontmatter schema and isolated runtime safety, not behavioral non-regression on held-out tasks.
   - anchor `App. B.2 Verifier` @ 2602.00359v2: "validates syntactic correctness of generated code (Python AST parsing for tools), schema compliance (YAML frontmatter for skills)"

### Verdict

adopt_patterns, narrowly. The evolver-owned persistent workspace is the transferable pattern (and the repo shows it concretely); the empirical base is thin.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Cite only for the artifact-state decomposition and the commit/no-op framing; do not cite the scaling hypothesis or the AppWorld margins as evidence for EPYC decisions.

## intake-2161#record — A-Evolve: The Universal Infrastructure for Self-Improving Agents (A-EVO-Lab/a-evolve)

- **URL**: https://github.com/A-EVO-Lab/a-evolve
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-2147#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The persistent evolver workspace is a sibling directory of the solver workspace holding task_board.md (four mandatory sections: Failure Patterns, Verified Capabilities, Unresolved, Human Requests; validated, with numeric PRIORITY bullets and a main/branch TARGET), research_log.jsonl (one record per tested approach, 15 required fields incl. tested, works, error), architecture.md, insights.jsonl, strategy_tree.md and tests/.
   - anchor `agent_evolve/algorithms/navigation/templates/_evolution_workspace.py:L4 @ 18ba996` @ 18ba996: "task_board.md — failure patterns + prioritized gaps"
   - anchor `agent_evolve/algorithms/navigation/templates/_evolution_workspace.py:L5 @ 18ba996` @ 18ba996: "research_log.jsonl — one JSON record per tested approach"
1. The pass/fail ledger is thin. `get_failed_approaches` (works == False) is defined with the docstring 'avoid retesting' but is called nowhere in the repository; the research agent is only told in prompt text to read research_log.jsonl. Records are LLM-authored. When all build-verify attempts fail, one generic record (works False, error 'All build-verify attempts exhausted') is appended with no diff, no gate name and no per-suite deltas.
   - anchor `agent_evolve/algorithms/navigation/templates/_evolution_workspace.py:L231 @ 18ba996` @ 18ba996: """"Return records with works=False (avoid retesting).""""
2. The verifier verdict is parsed by substring: passed = 'VERDICT: PASS' in the upper-cased text, or 'PASS' anywhere in its first line. A first line such as 'FAIL: tests that PASS ...' parses as a pass. Rollback is by git tag (rollback_to_tag to the pre-build tag) after each failed attempt, with up to build_verify_retries attempts.
   - anchor `agent_evolve/algorithms/navigation/templates/structured_evolution.py:L851 @ 18ba996` @ 18ba996: ""error": "All build-verify attempts exhausted","
   - anchor `agent_evolve/algorithms/navigation/templates/structured_evolution.py:L882 @ 18ba996` @ 18ba996: "passed = "VERDICT: PASS" in content.upper() or "PASS" in content.upper().split("\n")[0] if content else False"
3. A newer unified engine carries structured verdicts: Verdict(accept, rollback, reason) with rollback instructing the engine to revert to the pre-step snapshot, and a StagnationRollback verifier keyed on pass-rate improvement; the stagnation verifier is documented as not on the Phase-1 loop path.
   - anchor `agent_evolve/algorithms/unified/types.py:L112 @ 18ba996` @ 18ba996: "``rollback=True`` instructs ``UnifiedEngine`` to revert the workspace to"
4. Design contract: 'the workspace IS the interface'. The agent reads from it, the evolver writes to it, and they never talk directly; every cycle is bracketed by pre-evo and evo git tags so any mutation is revertible.
   - anchor `DESIGN.md:L5 @ 18ba996` @ 18ba996: "**The workspace IS the interface.**"

### Verdict

adopt_patterns. Reference layout for an evolver-owned workspace, plus two cautionary findings (dead failed-approach reader, substring verdict).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Map: the A-Evolve research_log ledger is a weaker form of AP-53; AP-53 (harness-written, diff + sha256 + rejecting gate + per-suite deltas, fed back into the mutation prompt) already exceeds it. No port. The 'Verified Capabilities' / 'Unresolved' task-board sections have no AutoPilot equivalent and are recorded as a monitor item.

## intake-2162#record — Adaptive Auto-Harness code release (A-EVO-Lab/a-evolve, branch release/adaptive-auto-harness)

- **URL**: https://github.com/A-EVO-Lab/a-evolve/tree/release/adaptive-auto-harness
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2147#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Located: the arXiv HTML (2606.01770) renders 'Code is available in Link' with an href to github.com/A-EVO-Lab/a-evolve/tree/release/adaptive-auto-harness. The abs page itself carries no code link and the extracted text had dropped the URL.
   - anchor `README.md:L43 @ 17bc9eb` @ 17bc9eb: "git clone -b release/adaptive-auto-harness https://github.com/A-EVO-Lab/a-evolve.git"
1. The EGL the paper calls the 'Expected-Gain-from-Learning trigger that gates whether a cycle runs' is not implemented as such. egl.py defines 'Evolutionary Generality Loss' = new_skills_created / total_tasks_solved x 1000, a convergence metric with `is_converged(threshold=0.05, window=3)`, and no module imports it. The phrase 'Expected-Gain-from-Learning' occurs nowhere in the branch.
   - anchor `agent_evolve/algorithms/aevolve/egl.py:L3 @ 17bc9eb` @ 17bc9eb: "EGL = (new_skills_created / total_tasks_solved) * 1000"
   - anchor `agent_evolve/algorithms/aevolve/egl.py:L21 @ 17bc9eb` @ 17bc9eb: """"Check if EGL has been below threshold for ``window`` consecutive cycles.""""
2. `egl_threshold` is declared in config.py and set to 0.05 in the experiment configs but is never read by any Python file. `egl_window` is read once, as the window of `_is_score_converged` (score unchanged within a hardcoded epsilon 0.01 over the window), a stop check that runs AFTER a cycle completes.
   - anchor `agent_evolve/config.py:L72 @ 17bc9eb` @ 17bc9eb: "egl_threshold: float = 0.05"
   - anchor `agent_evolve/engine/loop.py:L169 @ 17bc9eb` @ 17bc9eb: "if _is_score_converged(score_history, window=self.config.egl_window):"
   - anchor `agent_evolve/engine/loop.py:L36 @ 17bc9eb` @ 17bc9eb: """"Generic convergence: score hasn't improved by more than *epsilon* in *window* cycles.""""
   - anchor `agent_evolve/config.py:L73 @ 17bc9eb` @ 17bc9eb: "egl_window: int = 3"
3. The only pre-evolution gate that exists is `evo_trigger_threshold` on the batch score: evolution is skipped when batch_score >= threshold; default 0.0 disables it, -1 means baseline (never evolve); max_cycles and a late-start cycle also skip. The evolution cells do not set it, so in the paper's runs every batch evolves until max_cycles.
   - anchor `solve_all_with_evolution.py:L768 @ 17bc9eb` @ 17bc9eb: "or (evo_trigger_threshold > 0 and batch_score >= evo_trigger_threshold)"
   - anchor `solve_all_with_evolution.py:L276 @ 17bc9eb` @ 17bc9eb: "evo_trigger_threshold = config.extra.get("evo_trigger_threshold", 0.0)"
4. Temporal-reveal is implemented: the watermark is the earliest creation_date of the next batch, labels reach the routing log only when `observer.is_revealed`, and a cumulative reveal state surfaces newly resolved tasks to the evolver. This is the reference implementation for the A2 row of the AAH dive.
   - anchor `solve_all_with_evolution.py:L709 @ 17bc9eb` @ 17bc9eb: "any past task whose resolution comes"
5. The branch ships the same evolver-workspace helper module as main (task_board.md, research_log.jsonl, architecture.md, insights.jsonl, strategy_tree.md); the new algorithm directory is agent_evolve/algorithms/aevolve with a holdout GatingStrategy that the evolution cells disable (`holdout_ratio: 0.0`).
   - anchor `agent_evolve/algorithms/navigation/templates/_evolution_workspace.py:L5 @ 17bc9eb` @ 17bc9eb: "research_log.jsonl — one JSON record per tested approach"
6. Default evolver trajectory feed is 'index' mode: the evolver prompt carries only a compact trajectory index and 'Evaluation signals (success, score, feedback, judge claims) are NEVER exposed'; the 'inline' legacy mode that includes ground truth is labelled unsafe.
   - anchor `agent_evolve/config.py:L62 @ 17bc9eb` @ 17bc9eb: "# score, feedback, judge claims) are NEVER exposed."

### Verdict

adopt_patterns: temporal-reveal and index-mode feed are the portable pieces; the EGL gate is not there to adopt.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Close AAH dive row A7 as: EGL cycle-trigger NOT FOUND in released code; do not model an AutoPilot gate on it. Keep the simple batch-score skip rule as a monitor item.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Attach the temporal-reveal reference implementation (solve_all_with_evolution.py, observer.is_revealed / update_reveal_state) to the A2 verification task for the AP-54 fence.

## intake-2163#record — EvoTest: Evolutionary Test-Time Learning for Self-Improving Agentic Systems

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.13220
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-2145#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. After each episode an Evolver LLM (o3) reads the full transcript and proposes child configurations (prompt, structured success/failure memory, hyperparameters, tool-use routines incl. a Python state extractor). The next configuration is chosen by UCB over {parent} + children using the environment's episode score; because the parent stays in the pool, a child that does badly falls back to the parent.
   - anchor `§4.3 Configuration Selection via UCB` @ 2510.13220v2: "Because the reliable parent configuration is always in the selection pool, if a new child performs poorly after its initial trial"
1. J-TTL replays the same Jericho game for K=50 episodes from an identical reset initial state (T=110 steps), so improvements are measured on the same instance; the learned artifacts are walkthroughs and state-hash->action tables.
   - anchor `§3 Test-Time Learning Process / App. C.1` @ 2510.13220v2: "The game environment is reset to its identical initial state after each episode, meaning any performance gains are solely attributable to the agent’s learning algorithm."
2. Ablations (Table 3, single numbers, no SD): removing prompt evolution cuts Detective AUC 0.94 -> 0.52, removing UCB 0.94 -> 0.68, memory 0.82, hyperparameters 0.89, tool-use 0.91.
   - anchor `Table 3 (§5.3)` @ 2510.13220v2: "EvoTest 0.94 0.14 0.32 w/o Prompt 0.52 0.05 0.16 w/o UCB 0.68 0.08 0.22"
3. The authors concede the learned strategies are 'strategic overfitting to the current task instance', that the method depends on a powerful evolver, and that the search is a simple (1+m) strategy prone to local optima.
   - anchor `App. A Limitations` @ 2510.13220v2: "The highly specific strategies and “verbal guardrails” that EvoTest learns (e.g., unlock door with key ) are a form of strategic overfitting to the current task instance."
4. Evolver size matters (Table 4): Detective AUC o3 0.94, deepseek-r1 0.90, qwen3-32b 0.82, qwen3-8b 0.68, against Static 0.21; a small evolver still lifts well above static.
   - anchor `Table 4 (§5.3)` @ 2510.13220v2: "qwen3-8b 0.68 0.07 0.20 Static 0.21 0.03 0.11"
5. Headline comparison caveats: online SFT/GRPO baselines run on qwen3-32b while EvoTest uses Gemini 2.5 Flash or Claude 4 Sonnet actors plus an o3 evolver; only a three-game control with a qwen3-32b actor narrows this (Table 6: EvoTest with a qwen3-32b evolver avg 0.35 vs GRPO 0.31; with the o3 evolver 0.40). Table 8 (5 seeds) lists Memory on Detective as 0.56 while Table 1 lists 0.25 and the printed average 0.13 implies about 0.26, a transcription error.
   - anchor `Table 8 (App. F)` @ 2510.13220v2: "Memory 0.56 ± .02"

### Verdict

worth_investigating at low relevance: benchmark is replay of a single deterministic text game; the portable idea (select among parent and children by running score statistics) is one line and already in EPYC practice as champion/challenger.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Pattern only: parent-always-in-the-pool selection (UCB) as the fallback for a bad child is the same safety idea as the incumbent-in-pool gate seen in TTHE; no EPYC task. Monitor if AutoPilot ever evaluates multiple live configurations per window.

## intake-2164#record — A Theory of Reliable Self-Evolution for Agent Harnesses

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08175
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2145#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Reliable-adoption condition: a candidate harness raises expected reward over the user-task distribution if its gain on previously FAILED tasks (L) exceeds (1-Z) times the mean ABSOLUTE change on previously successful tasks (D <= delta); Theorem 1 gives gain >= L - (1-Z)*delta and Corollary 1 requires lambda > (1-Z)*delta.
   - *narrowed*: Condition and bound confirmed in Sec. 2.2 (Theorem 1, Corollary 1); holds under Assumptions 1-2 only, with D defined as the mean ABSOLUTE retained-task change, so run-to-run noise on retained tasks inflates D.
   - anchor `§2.2 A Sufficient Condition for Reliable Adoption` @ arXiv:2609.08175v2: "The condition permits changes on retained tasks while ensuring that failure-task gains outweigh their possible losses."
   - anchor `App. A.1 Assumption 2` @ arXiv:2609.08175v2: "The user-task distribution does not depend on the agent"
1. Two-Gate validation (Rule 1): adopt only if the estimated failure-task contribution is >= tau, the estimated retained-task change is <= delta, and a confidence-corrected gain lower bound Delta = tau - eps_L - (1 - Zhat + eps_Z)(delta + eps_D) is positive; Theorem 2 bounds the probability of adopting a non-improving candidate by the stepwise estimation-failure bound, and Theorem 4 says guaranteed gains accumulate across steps without independence between steps.
   - *narrowed*: Rule 1 and Theorems 2 and 4 confirmed as stated in the main text; proofs in App. B-F not read. Guarantees need fresh reference data after each adoption (Remark 5) and are conditional on the realized suite if a suite is reused (Remark 15).
   - anchor `§3.1 Rule 1 (Two-Gate validation)` @ arXiv:2609.08175v2: "The rule validates a modification only when the estimates support a sufficient failure-task contribution, controlled retained-task change, and a positive lower bound on overall expected-reward gain."
   - anchor `§3.2 Progressive Improvement Guarantees (Theorem 4 remark)` @ arXiv:2609.08175v2: "The bound does not require independence between evolution steps."
   - anchor `App. C.1 Remark 5` @ arXiv:2609.08175v2: "Adoption both changes the reference agent and uses these records in selecting the successor."
   - anchor `App. F.2 Remark 15` @ arXiv:2609.08175v2: "If one suite is reused for adoption across steps, later states and generation may depend on it."
2. Performance ceiling: under Two-Gate the rule cannot validate further improvement once the current agent reaches a ceiling set by the allowed retained-task change plus evaluation error; Measured-margin (Rule 2) uses the candidate's own measured retained-task decrease and can validate beyond that ceiling; Theorem 5 says validating a smaller gain as reward approaches its upper bound needs worst-case evaluation cost that grows (lower bound).
   - *unaffected*: Ceiling, Measured-margin rule and the cost lower bound (Theorem 5) confirmed in Sec. 4.1-4.2; the claim text matches the paper.
   - anchor `§4.1 Performance Ceiling (after Corollary 2)` @ arXiv:2609.08175v2: "verification treats the full allowed retained-task change as a possible loss, and evaluation error further reduces the ceiling."
   - anchor `§4.2 The Cost of Validating Further Improvement` @ arXiv:2609.08175v2: "As the remaining gains shrink, validating further improvement requires more precise evaluation."
3. Stall diagnosis: reachability (probability a generation run yields a qualified modification) can be estimated from extra evaluation of ALL candidates including unadopted ones, separating 'generation rarely produces good edits' from 'validation lacks evidence to accept them'.
   - *unaffected*: Reachability estimation and the generation-vs-validation diagnosis confirmed in Sec. 5.1; the paper itself says the example does not estimate reachability over the full task distribution.
   - anchor `§5 Diagnosing Stalled Harness Self-Evolution (intro, before 5.1)` @ arXiv:2609.08175v2: "These evaluations support reachability estimation, which we combine with validation and adoption records to distinguish the two sources."
4. Experiment (DS-1000, single API model, 4 contexts x 6 pools x 4 outputs per process): 26-39% of outputs meet the audit point target at T0=(0.05,0.50), yet only 2 of 288 outputs pass validation (both from independent generation); a retrospective screen finds one outcome-feedback candidate whose improvement the original 48-task validation could not certify (lower bound 0.0378 < 0.05).
   - *narrowed*: Numbers confirmed against Tables 1-3 and App. G.1. Scope: one benchmark, one API model; the 'only two adopted' is 2 of 288 outputs at 48 validation tasks; the audit and validation sets differ.
   - anchor `§5.2 Experimental Illustration` @ arXiv:2609.08175v2: "Across the three processes, substantial fractions meet the audit point target, yet only two outputs pass validation and are adopted."
   - anchor `App. G.1 Setting` @ arXiv:2609.08175v2: "We use the API model gpt-5.4-mini-2026-03-17."
   - anchor `App. G.1 Candidate-level diagnosis` @ arXiv:2609.08175v2: "Validation and audit use different task sets; the example diagnoses this recorded decision and does not estimate reachability over the full user-task distribution."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 corrections: (1) from the wave-3 systems dive: the surfaced-sources note that arXiv 2608.08466 (HSI) contains a Two-Gate rule is wrong; HSI only cites Wang et al. (2510.04399). (2) Title record (W2T8): arXiv 2609.08175 carried the title 'Safe Harness Self-Evolution: A Theoretical Analysis of Feasibility and Limits' in v1 and 'A Theory of Reliable Self-Evolution for Agent Harnesses' in v2; dedup keys on arxiv_id, so a title-based dedup would miss it.

### Verdict

adopt_patterns, design-input only. The decomposition and the 'validation budget is the bottleneck' finding are directly usable as HIL-2 reporting text; the theorems rest on assumptions (fixed task distribution, binary or bounded reward, fresh reference data after each adoption) that a long-running AutoPilot loop partly violates, and the only experiment is a small one-model illustration whose main empirical content is that a rigorous gate rarely fires.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Cite as the formal statement of 'protect retained tasks while rewarding repairs'; use only as a REPORTING decomposition (L-hat, D-hat, Z-hat) in HIL-2 and as a validation-power statement. Any gate built on it is a new protocol and needs human amendment (MEASUREMENT.md section 5). Not a source of EPYC numbers.

## intake-2165#record — One Recipe, Many Harnesses: What Self-Evolution Encodes Across Languages and Models

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10178
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2145#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. A fixed, typed-routing evolution recipe (TRIAGE: typed failure attribution, a falsifiable per-round change manifest, linear checkpoints with a non-regression selection score) improves held-out solve rate over a minimal seed in most of 24 language x model cells and matches or beats mini-SWE-agent in 14 of 24; two null regions: Python under all three models and GPT-5-mini in every language.
   - *narrowed*: Held-out gains confirmed per Table 4 (Haiku and DeepSeek significant, GPT-5-mini not); 'matches or exceeds mini-SWE-agent in 14 of 24 cells' means 10 of 24 cells are below it. Single run per cell, n=50 held-out.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "The loop improves held-out solve rates over both a minimal seed and the manually designed mini-SWE-agent scaffold in most cells, but with two null regions."
   - anchor `App. H No head-to-head comparison` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "not that harness evolution is the best use of a fixed total budget."
   - anchor `App. H Statistical power and search budget` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "Evolution runs once per cell, so we report rollout variance but not variance over the loop’s own stochasticity"
   - anchor `App. A.4 Selection` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "so a round that raises the aggregate while silently breaking previously-solved instances cannot become the incumbent."
   - anchor `App. A.4 Non-regression gate` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "The reported gain is therefore lower-bounded by zero by construction."
   - anchor `App. A.4 Contract format` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "The prediction is the falsifiable part: it commits to task-level outcomes before they are observed."
1. Gains compensate recoverable execution defects (editing a test file that collides with the hidden gold test patch, shipping non-compiling code, submitting without running the target test) rather than raising the capability ceiling: 88% of the 256 recovered instances carried a detector-covered defect under the seed, and which defect dominates differs by cell (C++ 70% non-compiling, Go under 10%).
   - *narrowed*: 88% / 81% / 7% / 12% confirmed in App. D.1; the paper says the detectors cover only part of the recoverable space and the account is directional, not a bound on headroom; the defect definition depends on the gold-test-patch grader.
   - anchor `§4.3 Scope: compensation, not a raised ceiling` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "Within the failure modes we can detect, the gain is compensation rather than capability extension"
   - anchor `App. H Dependence on the grading protocol` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "Editing a test file is fatal here because the grader applies a hidden gold test patch on top of the agent’s diff"
2. Evolved harnesses share an abstract playbook (concept Jaccard about 0.55) but almost disjoint implementation (ecosystem-marker Jaccard about 0.12); 20-40% of each harness is ecosystem-specific.
   - *unaffected*: Concept and marker Jaccard (0.55 vs 0.12) and the 20-40% ecosystem fraction confirmed in Sec. 4.4; the paper notes the concept tags come from an LLM rubric.
   - anchor `§4.4 Concepts partly converge; instantiations do not` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "Harnesses evolved for different languages overlap substantially in what they aim to enforce and almost not at all in how they enforce it."
3. The shared core transfers: a harness transplanted unchanged onto a foreign language helps in 18 of 20 ordered pairs and a memory-only universal harness recovers 48-68% of native gain on Java, C++ and TypeScript but matches native on Go and JavaScript; a native ceiling remains.
   - *narrowed*: 18/20, 0.63 mean, retention figures confirmed; transfer measured on DeepSeek-V4-Flash only and on five languages; the single strongly negative cell (JS->TS, -2.30) is the one place a transplanted harness did harm.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "The shared core transfers and can be distilled into one universal harness, while an ecosystem margin resists both and requires native re-evolution."
4. A weak 20B open-weights meta-agent (GPT-OSS-20B) driving the loop recovers the held-out gain of a frontier meta-agent in one cell (Go x DeepSeek-V4-Flash: 34.2 vs 35.6 vs seed 18.5); pre-installing a hand-written capability tool was net-negative (-12.9 pp, Haiku x C++).
   - *narrowed*: Table 7 and Table 8 confirmed; each is a single cell with k=3 and a gap smaller than the paper's own rollout noise.
   - anchor `App. E.1 Table 7 caption` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "Even an ultra-weak 20B open Meta-Agent recovers the performance gains delivered by the frontier model"
   - anchor `App. E.2 Table 8 discussion` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "The tool is net-negative"
   - anchor `App. E.1 Meta-Agent ablation discussion` @ arXiv:2608.10178v1: "To be clear, we do not claim that the Meta-Agent’s capability is entirely irrelevant."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-FLG-07: Operator-flag source: HarnessForge 2606.01779 (recommend monitor under the weights-lane trigger) -> M16; HC-FLG-08: Operator-flag source: MUSE-Autoskill 2605.27366 (recommend monitor under the skillbank-gate trigger) -> M16. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

adopt_patterns. The defect-mass pre-screen, the regression-penalized selection score, the instance-token memory filter and the retention metric are portable and cheap; the headline gains rest on small splits, one run per cell and API models, and the paper concedes no compute-matched baseline.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Use as evidence that harness gains track a measurable defect profile of the (model, feature) pair and that typed, falsifiable edit records make edits attributable; use the retention metric rho for any evolve-here-serve-there claim. No EPYC numbers are derived from it.

## intake-2166#record — HarnessX: A Composable, Adaptive, and Evolvable Agent Harness Foundry

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14249
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2146#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. AEGIS evolves a typed harness in four stages (Digester, Planner, Evolver, Critic) driven by one meta-agent, and ships an edit only through a DETERMINISTIC gate (manifest completeness, config normalization, build/smoke test, and a 'seesaw' constraint that rejects any edit regressing a previously solved task); design principle: LLM subagents propose, typed structure and deterministic gates decide what ships.
   - *unaffected*: Pipeline, gate checks and the LLM-proposes / deterministic-gate-decides principle confirmed in Sec. 4.3 and Algorithm 1.
   - anchor `§4.3 AEGIS Architecture, Critic and gating` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "This decouples LLM judgment from acceptance: regardless of the Critic’s recommendation, only deterministic checks govern shipping."
   - anchor `§4.3 Design principle` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "Language-model subagents explore, hypothesize, and propose; typed structure and deterministic gates determine what ships."
   - anchor `App. 10.3 Change-Manifest Schema` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "The manifest makes every harness modification falsifiable"
   - anchor `§7.6 Ethical Considerations` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "the gating layer supports human approval for edits exceeding a configurable risk threshold (not exercised in our automated experiments)."
1. Across 5 benchmarks x 3 task agents (Sonnet 4.6, GPT-5.4, Qwen3.5-9B) and up to 15 rounds, harness evolution improves 14 of 15 configurations, average +14.5 (up to +44.0, Qwen3.5-9B on ALFWorld), with the weakest agent gaining most; ALL numbers are peak pass@2 on the same task set used for evolution, with no held-out split.
   - *narrowed*: Table 4 numbers confirmed, but they are peak accuracy on the evolution set (Sec. 6.1 Scope and Sec. 7.7); 'average +14.5' is an average of peak-minus-initial over 15 configurations, with one of them 0.0.
   - anchor `§6.1 Scope` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "All reported gains are measured on the same task set used for evolution; held-out generalization to unseen tasks is not evaluated in this work."
   - anchor `§7.7 Limitations` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "Since we report peak accuracy and evaluate on the adaptation set itself, the numbers carry both selection bias and potential overfitting."
2. The Global single-harness strategy on GAIA/GPT-5.4 peaks at 73.8 (R4) then degrades to 49.5 (-24.3, above the 95% CI of +-8.5) because sub-threshold regressions are invisible to the binary pass@2 seesaw; ensemble variant isolation (up to K variants, per-variant seesaw scope) ends at 87.4 = peak with fewer tokens (107.8M vs 143.7M).
   - *narrowed*: Table 5 confirmed; single task agent and benchmark (GAIA, GPT-5.4); the ensemble row is also in-sample; the paper says finer strategies were only piloted at 30-40 tasks.
   - anchor `§6.3 Failure mechanism of Global` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "subsequent edits introduce sub-threshold regressions that are individually undetectable under pass@2’s binary signal yet compound into aggregate decline."
   - anchor `§4.5 Variant Isolation via Ensemble Routing` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "in which case the system forks a new variant rather than rejecting the edit outright"
3. Documented failure modes despite the gate: a reward-hack where a shipped tool passed some tasks by exploiting verifier format regularities (GAIA R10, detected R11), and a -14.0 point regression on tau3 Telecom (R7) after five consecutive same-type prompt/processor 'reminder' edits each passed the seesaw individually; the Critic flagged the concentration but still approved.
   - *unaffected*: Both failure cases confirmed in Sec. 6.6 and 7.6, including the statement that the Critic flagged the concentration risk and still approved.
   - anchor `§6.6 Catastrophic forgetting` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "This regression evaded the seesaw constraint because pass@2 registers only per-task binary flips, not sub-threshold coupling."
   - anchor `§6.6 Reward hacking` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "but a subset passed by exploiting format regularities in the verifier rather than performing actual retrieval."
   - anchor `§6.6 Catastrophic forgetting` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "but still approved the edit for shipping because ship-prediction accuracy remained high"
4. A single-agent Claude-Code-SDK evolver with the same model and infrastructure reaches 86.4 vs AEGIS 87.4 on GAIA/GPT-5.4 (within one SE of about 3.3), so the four-stage decomposition buys about 12-14% fewer tokens and auditability, not measurable accuracy; co-evolution with cross-harness GRPO adds +4.7 on two benchmarks with Qwen3.5-9B.
   - *narrowed*: Table 6 and Sec. 6.5 confirmed; 86.4 vs 87.4 is within one SE; co-evolution gain is two benchmarks, one small model, and needs joint control of harness and training.
   - anchor `§6.4 Meta-Agent Effectiveness` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "indicating that the four-stage decomposition does not improve final accuracy at this meta-agent capability level."
   - anchor `§7.7 Limitations` @ arXiv:2606.14249v3: "Co-evolution requires joint control over both harness evolution and model training."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 correction from the wave-3 systems dive: the HarnessX dive's surfaced-sources claim that the HarnessBank family 'credits gains by peak pass rate with no held-out split' came from a search snippet and is wrong for HarnessBank v2 (arXiv 2607.13683), which reports sealed-test paired results.

### Verdict

adopt_patterns, failure-case and gate-design only. The Telecom R7 and GAIA Global-collapse cases are concrete evidence that a per-edit seesaw misses accumulated sub-threshold coupling, which is directly relevant to PromptForge; the accuracy numbers are in-sample peaks and carry no weight.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Cite HarnessX for (a) the deterministic-gate-decides-shipping separation, (b) the documented accumulation failure that a per-edit gate cannot see, and (c) variant isolation as the remedy for heterogeneous task sets. Do not cite its +14.5 / +44.0 as an expected gain: peak round, same set, no held-out.

## intake-2167#record — Workspace Optimization: How to Train Your Agent

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09650
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance low; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2146#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Workspace optimization: with frozen weights, the trainable state is a typed workspace of programs, logs and trace data; each action is a test of a committed prediction, a contradicted prediction is routed as a counterexample to the single artifact that emitted it, and every accepted patch is replayed against a retained regression set before it is kept.
   - *unaffected*: Formulation and the replay mechanism confirmed in Sec. 1-2.
   - anchor `§1 Introduction (related work paragraph)` @ arXiv:2605.09650v1: "Each accepted patch is replayed against earlier transitions retained as a regression set, so a revision must remain consistent with prior evidence as well as the current outcome."
1. DreamTeam instantiates this as six roles (Observer, Simulator, Inductive Explorer, Transductive Explorer, Critic, Team Leader), each owning one surface, with the world model held as executable code.
   - *unaffected*: Six roles, surfaces and the code-as-world-model design confirmed in Sec. 3.2 and 4.1.
   - anchor `§4.1 The Agentic Workspace` @ arXiv:2605.09650v1: "Each role owns exactly one surface: a typed slot holding the artifacts the role reads, edits, and is held responsible for."
2. On the 25-game ARC-AGI-3 public set, averaged over two runs, DreamTeam scores 38.4% RHAE vs 36% for the prior protocol-matched single-run baseline (Symbolica Agentica), and uses 31% fewer environment actions per game (mean 444 vs 643).
   - *narrowed*: 38.4 vs 36 confirmed; the baseline action count is printed inconsistently (643 vs 16,081); the 31% reduction matches 444 vs 643; two runs vs one baseline run; 15 games changed baselines in the April update.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2605.09650v1: "DreamTeam improves the SOTA protocol-matched agent’s score from 36% to 38.4%, while using 31% fewer environment actions per game."
   - anchor `§1 Contributions` @ arXiv:2605.09650v1: "mean of 444 vs the baseline’s 643"
   - anchor `§6 Limitations and Conclusion` @ arXiv:2605.09650v1: "Our reported scores are bounded by time and cost budgets rather than by system intelligence"

### Verdict

adopt_patterns at knowledge level only: no task. The paper adds nothing to the HIL-4 decision or the PromptForge gate beyond a third independent description of replaying accepted edits against retained history.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): [dive-verified] Knowledge only: the regression-set replay and owner-named-slot ideas duplicate patterns already covered by HarnessX, AHE and the Recipe paper at the harness level. Not an EPYC mechanism: the setting is online interactive games with Opus 4.6 and GPT-5.5 role models.

## intake-2168#record — Continual Learning Bench (companion repository of arXiv 2606.05661)

- **URL**: https://github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, memory_augmented, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2149#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Runtime shape: the companion repo is a runnable harness (Python 3.13, uv, a local Docker install for containerized tasks and systems; `clbench` CLI). Custom systems register via @register_system and implement ContinualLearningSystem respond/reset/name, so any agent (including an orchestrator wrapper) can be evaluated as a system. Task scoring in src/tasks contains no LLM judge (only a dev-time discover_quirks script mentions an LLM).
   - anchor `README.md:L13 @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: "and a local Docker installation for tasks and systems that run containerized workspaces."
   - anchor `README.md:L26 @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: "clbench run exploitable_poker --schedule quick_test --system icl"
   - anchor `src/systems/AGENTS.md:L5 @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: "Register packages with `@register_system("<name>")` and implement `ContinualLearningSystem.respond`, `reset`, and `name`."
1. Model access: every LLM call goes through LiteLLM (litellm==1.81.6). No api_base/base_url parameter is plumbed through the ICL / notepad / structured-output path (the kwargs are model, messages, response_format only) and the only environment credentials the code reads are API keys, so a local OpenAI-compatible endpoint is NOT a supported configuration knob; it would need LiteLLM's own env-var convention (unverified, nothing run) or a small patch. Provider routing is by model-name string (names starting with gpt-, o or openai/ go to the OpenAI Responses adapter) and provider_mode 'litellm_chat' forces plain chat completions.
   - *unaffected*: Local-endpoint conclusion rests on grep + file reads; run-time behaviour untested.
   - anchor `pyproject.toml:L9 @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: ""litellm==1.81.6","
   - anchor `src/systems/utils/structured_output.py:L259-L263 @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: "completion_kwargs = dict( model=model, messages=messages, response_format=response_schema, )"
   - anchor `src/systems/utils/provider_adapters.py:L96-L98 @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: "model_lower.startswith("openai/") or model_lower.startswith("gpt-") or model_lower.startswith("o")"
   - anchor `src/systems/icl/system.py:L65-L66 @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: ""litellm_chat" preserves the old chat-completion path."
   - anchor `src/commands/doctor.py:L66-L69 @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: ""OPENAI_API_KEY": "required by gpt-* models (icl default)","
2. The mem0 system defaults to OpenAI text-embedding-3-small embeddings, i.e. a second remote dependency that a local run must also re-point.
   - anchor `src/systems/mem0/README.md:L20 @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: "Embeddings default to OpenAI `text-embedding-3-small`."
3. Run cost shape (from the artifacts the repo ships, ICL GPT-5.4, one stateful rollout, six tasks): 301 instances, 783 model calls, 37.98M input tokens (95.1% cached prefix), 0.235M output tokens, mean 48.5K input tokens per call. The paper averages 5 rollouts per task plus one stateless baseline run. The cheapest end-to-end smoke is `clbench run exploitable_poker --schedule quick_test --system icl` = 5 hands x 3 runs.
   - anchor `src/tasks/exploitable_poker/schedules/quick_test.json:L21 @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: ""num_instances": 5,"
   - anchor `src/tasks/exploitable_poker/schedules/quick_test.json:L11 @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: ""runs": 3,"
   - anchor `final_results/runs/icl-gpt-5.4/tasks/blind_spectrum_monitoring.json.gz :: run_traces[0].trace.execution.usage.respond (subset of keys; gzip JSON) @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: "{"cached_input_tokens": 4295808, "call_count": 90, "input_tokens": 4486921, "output_tokens": 31532}"
   - anchor `final_results/runs/icl-gpt-5.4/tasks/exploitable_poker.json.gz :: run_traces[0].trace.execution.usage.respond (subset of keys; gzip JSON) @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: "{"cached_input_tokens": 15094144, "call_count": 305, "input_tokens": 15241190, "output_tokens": 23989}"
4. The ACE system is the one place that accepts free-form litellm kwargs (generator/reflector/curator_model_kwargs_json), so an api_base could in principle be injected there, but not for ICL, notepad or mem0 (unverified; nothing run).
   - anchor `src/systems/ace/system.py:L125-L127 @ 5f8c50e` @ github.com/pgasawa/continual-learning-bench @ 5f8c50eb1e84b2eda2ef4faff757dfc812a0ea26: "generator_model_kwargs_json: str = "{}","

### Verdict

Use the repo as a SOURCE OF TASKS, SCORERS AND ARTIFACTS, not as a turnkey local benchmark. A local llama-server/orchestrator /v1 endpoint is not a supported configuration: LiteLLM is called without api_base in the ICL path, credentials are API keys only, and routing is by model-name string. Nothing was run here, so LiteLLM's env-var fallback is untested. A full run is ~38M input tokens (95% cached prefix) and ~0.23M output tokens per rollout, 5 rollouts plus a stateless baseline, at ~48K tokens per call: feasible only with KV-prefix reuse and a >=128K context. Cheapest smoke: poker quick_test (5 hands x 3 runs). The shipped final_results are a free offline corpus for building a stateful-vs-stateless gain analysis without any inference.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversational-memory-eval-instrument.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): CR1: Draft (zero inference, no repo execution) a spec for a thin registered ContinualLearningSystem that sends each turn to the orchestrator /v1 chat endpoint with a provider_mode-independent client, keeping the prefix stable for KV reuse; list the exact CL-Bench interface points it must satisfy (Query/Response/TaskStepResult, usage events with pricing None). Follow with ONE bounded smoke ("clbench run exploitable_poker --schedule quick_test --system <ours>", 5 hands x 3 runs) only when a compute window is granted; the smoke needs a compute claim and is out of scope for this dive.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): CR2: Zero-inference analysis: from final_results/runs/icl-gpt-5.4 and icl-claude-sonnet-4.6, recompute per-instance paired gain, the headroom-normalised gain, and the spread of the single stateless run versus the 5 stateful rollouts; report how much of Table 1 interval width the unreplicated baseline hides. Output feeds the wave-1 B1 proposal as a worked example. Read gzip JSON with python3 -I only; do not run repo code.

## intake-2169#record — SkillLearnBench: Benchmarking Continual Learning Methods for Agent Skill Generation on Real-World Tasks

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20087
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, memory_augmented, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2149#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. SkillLearnBench is 20 skill-dependent tasks (15 sub-domains, 100 instances) with deterministic verifiers and a three-level evaluation (LLM-judged skill quality, trajectory alignment/usage, task accuracy and tokens). Four skill-generation methods (One-Shot, Self Feedback, Teacher Feedback, Skill Creator) are run with six closed generator LLMs (Claude Haiku 4.5, Sonnet 4.6, Opus 4.6; Gemini 3.1 Flash Lite, 3 Flash, 3.1 Pro), and scored by one fixed Claude Sonnet 4.6 solver (up to 100 turns, Docker sandbox). Teacher Feedback's teacher has access to the human-authored skills.
   - *unaffected*: Task counts, methods, generators, solver set-up re-read from section 3-4 and App. N.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "comprising 20 verified, skill-dependent tasks across 15 sub-domains derived from a real-world skill taxonomy"
   - anchor `§4.1 Solving agent` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "The agent runs for up to 100 turns in a containerized sandbox with tools for each task."
   - anchor `§3.3 Teacher Feedback` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "this approach introduces an expert teacher with access to human-authored skills."
1. Every method beats No Skill (10.17%) but all stay far below human-authored skills (74.50%); averaged over generators One-Shot 30.44, Self Feedback 31.08, Teacher Feedback 27.47, Skill Creator 27.33. The paper's 'best method covers about 45% of the gap' is the best single (generator, method) cell (Sonnet 4.6 One-Shot 38.83 = 44.6% of the gap); method averages cover about 31-33% of the gap (this dive's computation: (31.08-10.17)/(74.50-10.17)=32.5%), and Self Feedback's 0.64-point lead over One-Shot is small against the paper's own single-trial accuracy SD of 13-17 points.
   - *narrowed*: Paper headline "best method covers about 45% of the gap" is a best single (generator, method) cell; method averages cover 31-33% and the Self-vs-One-Shot gap (0.64) is inside single-trial SD.
   - anchor `§1 Introduction` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "even the best method covers only about 45% of the gap between no-skill and human-authored performance"
   - anchor `§4.2 Main Results` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "On average, Self Feedback performs best and uses the fewest tokens."
   - anchor `App. E Robustness of Single-Trial Evaluation` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "Accuracy fluctuates more (std 13–17% for generated methods)"
2. Method ranking is generator-dependent and stronger generators do not make better skills: no method leads across all tasks and LLMs, mid-tier Sonnet 4.6 is competitive with Opus 4.6, and stronger models write more prescriptive skills that hard-code instance specifics.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "no method leads across all tasks and LLMs, and scaling to stronger LLMs does not reliably help."
   - anchor `App. C Generation LLM Scale` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "stronger models tend to produce skills that are more precise but more prescriptive, hardcoding specific parameters, library choices, or data field names."
3. Self-revision without an external signal drifts: extended to four rounds on Productivity Tools with Claude Sonnet 4.6 only, Self Feedback accuracy rises briefly then falls sharply with alignment declining, while Teacher Feedback (a teacher with access to the human skills) improves steadily through round 4 (shown in Fig. 4, not tabulated in the text).
   - *narrowed*: Drift evidence is one category (Productivity Tools) and one generator (Sonnet 4.6), shown in a figure.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "whereas self-feedback alone induces recursive drift."
   - anchor `§5.4 Skill Evolution` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "we extend Self Feedback and Teacher Feedback to four rounds on Productivity Tools with Claude Sonnet 4.6."
   - anchor `§5.4 Skill Evolution` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "external feedback gives the model something new to learn from, while self-revision without it leads to drift rather than progress."
4. Learned skills can hurt, and adoption is a separate failure axis: in Table 3 the Utilities category is below No Skill (16.67) under all four methods (One-Shot 10.56, Self Feedback 4.17, Teacher Feedback 5.56, Skill Creator 15.28), Skill Creator is below No Skill in Information Retrieval (23.89 vs 28.33), and Teacher Feedback has the lowest mean skill usage rate (60.20, vs One-Shot 70.99 and Skill Creator 84.47).
   - anchor `§5.1 Learning Effect Across Categories` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "which suggests that a rigid skill can hurt when the task does not match the learned template."
   - anchor `§1 Introduction` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "a skill must also be adopted by the agent and correctly guide its execution"
   - anchor `Table 3 caption` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "Table 3: Task accuracy (%) by category, averaged across all six LLMs."
5. More seed instances at generation time made One-Shot skills worse (accuracy 56.67, 23.33, 27.78 for 1, 3, 5 instances); scope is One-Shot, Productivity Tools, Claude Sonnet 4.6 only.
   - anchor `App. A Effect of Instance Count` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "Contrary to expectation, increasing the instance count does not improve performance."
   - anchor `App. A Effect of Instance Count` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "We analyze One-Shot on the Productivity Tools category, using Claude Sonnet 4.6 as the skill-generation LLM"
6. Cost shape: solving cost is over 97% input tokens, and every generated-skill method lowers per-instance solving tokens relative to No Skill (727K) to 390K-528K; Self Feedback is the cheapest (390K) and the most accurate on average.
   - anchor `App. D Token Efficiency` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "Input tokens account for over 97% of the total solving cost across all methods"
7. Evidence limits: the headline is single-trial (a 3-trial robustness check covers one seed instance per task; accuracy SD 13-17 points); judge test-retest was run on only three tasks; Levels 1-2 use a GPT-5-mini judge; generators and solver are closed frontier models (no open-weight or local model), and the Self Feedback and Teacher Feedback round counts are stated inconsistently between section 4.1 and Appendix M.
   - anchor `§4.1 Solving agent` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "We evaluate all generated skills with one fixed solving agent powered by Claude Sonnet 4.6 at temperature 0."
   - anchor `App. E Robustness of Single-Trial Evaluation` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "we run the seed instance per task three times for each method and each of the six skill-generation LLMs."
   - anchor `App. L.3 Judge Reliability` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "running the identical scoring pipeline twice on three tasks"
8. Tension with SkillsBench (existing entry intake-096#record, read here only via the index entry): SkillsBench reports self-generated skills give no benefit on average (-1.3pp) whereas SkillLearnBench One-Shot gives 30.44 vs No Skill 10.17. A candidate reconciliation, NOT tested by this dive: SkillLearnBench keeps only tasks whose no-skill pass rate is below a threshold (10 runs, threshold 0.5 as the text states) and lets the generator analyse the domain and write skills before solving. The sign of 'self-generated skill benefit' is selection- and protocol-dependent.
   - anchor `§3.1 Tasks and Skills` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "Of the 20 tasks, 17 are adapted from SkillsBench"
   - anchor `§2 Related Work` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "SkillsBench (Li et al., 2026) finds that such skills provide no benefit on average"
   - anchor `§3.1 Tasks and Skills` @ arXiv:2604.20087v1: "We run the same fixed agent times on each instance without any skill and require the pass rate to stay below"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-19: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: arXiv 2602.08004 Agent Skills data-driven analysis; Wu and Zhang procedural-memory survey (TechRxiv) -> decline: taxonomy only, no mechanism; HC-UNR-20: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: arXiv 2602.01869 (cited as ProcMEM) -> decline: identifier unverified (the abstract title no longer matches), so it is not persisted; HC-FLG-10: Operator-flag source: Memento-Skills 2603.18743 (recommend decline) -> M16. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Cite SkillLearnBench for PROTOCOL and for three transferable cautions, not for effect sizes: (1) a distilled skill must be both adopted and correct, so report skill-usage rate beside accuracy; (2) learned skills can score below no-skill on open-ended/instance-specific categories, so report per-category deltas including negatives; (3) self-revision without an external signal drifted (one category, one generator). Do NOT cite the 30% vs 10% headline as evidence that self-generated skills help: it conflicts with SkillsBench (intake-096#record) under different task selection, is single-trial with SD 13-17 points, and uses closed frontier generators and a Claude solver. Method ranking differences of ~1 point are noise.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/episodic-memory-integrity.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): SL1: Amend the SkillBank/M-12 readout spec (document change, zero inference): report retrieved-skill adoption rate (share of injected skills actually referenced in the worker trajectory) beside accuracy, and per-category deltas with negative deltas shown; reuse the existing skill_transfer_regression.py negative-delta detector rather than a new one.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): SL2: Add a zero-inference lint to the distill_skillbank output review: flag skills that embed literal parameters, paths, column names or ids copied from the source trajectory; run it on the existing seed_skills output before M-11a is scheduled. Evidence is one task, so treat the lint as advisory.

## intake-2170#record — Harness-Zero: Harness Distillation via Agent-as-Harness

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24974
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: harness_optimization, training_distillation, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2148#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Method: a 'harnessing agent' wraps the student at its response boundary, reviews each proposed response against a private reference harness adapted from an evolved harness, and replaces it with the smallest coherent correction expressed in the student's own action space; it cannot see the sandbox or hidden solutions. SFT is applied to the accepted responses and the student is deployed under the fixed minimal target harness alone.
   - anchor `§1 Introduction` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "It passes sound proposals unchanged and otherwise makes the smallest coherent correction, expressed as a complete response in the student’s action space."
   - anchor `§1 Introduction` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "Harness-Zero applies supervised fine-tuning (SFT) to these reviewed rollouts to internalize the demonstrated behaviors in model parameters."
   - anchor `§1 Introduction (data-collection paragraph)` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "The harnessing agent cannot inspect the student sandbox or consult hidden task solutions"
1. Inference-time result (Table 1): agent-as-harness with the evolved reference harness averages 81.1% vs 78.1% for meta-harness and 68.6% for mini-SWE-agent over six model-benchmark cells. The evidence is single-run pass@1 on 100-task test sets (SpreadsheetBench, USPTO) and 168 AppWorld tasks; agent-as-harness is below meta-harness on AppWorld/DeepSeek-V4-Pro (89.3 vs 91.1); the evolved harness was evolved on Qwen3.5-9B and reused for frontier models; agent-as-harness uses at least two model calls per step so the comparison is not compute-matched.
   - *narrowed*: Headline +3.0 is single-run on 100-task sets, not compute-matched, and the evolved harness was built on a different (9B) model; agent-as-harness is below meta-harness in one cell.
   - anchor `§4.2 Obs.1` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "against 78.1% for meta-harness and 68.6% for mini-SWE-agent."
   - anchor `§4.1 Tasks and splits` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "We report single-run task success (pass@1, %) for SpreadsheetBench and USPTO"
   - anchor `§4.1 Tasks and splits` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "we use 300 for harness evolution and training data collection and hold out 100 for evaluation."
   - anchor `§4.2` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "On the frontier models we reuse the evolved"
2. Distillation result (Table 2): LoRA SFT of Qwen3.5-9B raises macro-average success from 23.3% to 44.3% under the target harness alone, above the 41.7% of the base model with the evolved harness attached; but the margin over harness-attached is carried by AppWorld (58.9 vs 48.2) while USPTO trails (30.0 vs 38.0), on single-run 100-task test sets.
   - *narrowed*: Exceeding the harness-attached base (44.3 vs 41.7) is carried by AppWorld; USPTO trails (30.0 vs 38.0).
   - anchor `§4.3 Obs.2` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "Harness-Zero raises the macro average from 23.3% to 44.3%, a 21.0-point absolute gain and a 90.1% relative improvement."
   - anchor `§4.3 Obs.3` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "On USPTO, Harness-Zero improves over the base model (30.0% vs. 12.0%) but trails"
   - anchor `§4.1 Models and training` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "We perform LoRA SFT on Qwen3.5-9B for two epochs using the Tinker recipe"
3. Ablation (Table 3, USPTO only): supervision value comes from harness-guided review of the student's own trajectory, not from stronger demonstrations or higher collection success: teacher rollouts under the harness succeed on 62.0% of collection tasks yet the student drops to 3%; oracle-answer review reaches 98.6% collection success yet the student reaches 15%; Harness-Zero (59.4% collection) reaches 30%.
   - *unaffected*: Table 3 values re-read; USPTO only.
   - anchor `§4.4 Obs.4` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "Directly fine-tuning on GPT-5.6 Sol trajectories leaves the student at the 12% base result, even though that source succeeds on 52.0% of the collection tasks."
   - anchor `§4.4 Obs.5` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "Collection success therefore does not predict distillation value."
4. Weak-reviewer failure (Appendix F, Table 8, USPTO): the advantage of agent-as-harness over meta-harness is +4.0 on DeepSeek-V4-Pro and +1.0 on GPT-5.6 Sol but -1.0 on DeepSeek-V4-Flash and -12.0 on Qwen3.6-35B-A3B (36.0 vs 48.0); on the weakest model the reviewer replaces 66% of steps and its edits are net harmful, and review without evolved guidance falls below the plain mini-SWE-agent (12.0 vs 16.0).
   - anchor `App. F` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "It remains positive on stronger models but turns negative on weaker ones"
   - anchor `App. F` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "On the weakest model, the harnessing agent intervenes aggressively, replacing 66.0% of reviewed steps, but its edits are net harmful."
5. Cost: review needs at least two model calls per step, and mean USPTO trial latency rises from 100.1 s (mini-SWE-agent) to 237.2 s (about 2.4x, this dive's ratio; the paper's sentence lost its multiplier in extraction) for agent-as-harness with evolved guidance. The overhead applies only at data-collection time.
   - anchor `App. F` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "requiring at least two model calls per step: one to generate the proposal and another to review it."
   - anchor `App. F` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "On USPTO, agent-as-harness with evolved guidance takes 237.2 s per trial on average"
6. Behavioural recovery: the distilled model recovers 82.3% on average of 28 harness-exclusive behaviour patterns, but the patterns are selected conditional on the base model never showing them without the harness (base scores 0% by construction) and each is kept only with at least 10 qualifying tasks, so recovery measures added behaviour, not task benefit.
   - anchor `§4.5 Obs.7` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "so the base model scores 0% on every pattern by construction."
   - anchor `§4.5 Behavioral internalization` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "keep a pattern only when at least 10 tasks meet this criterion."
7. Scope limits stated by the authors: procedural harness behaviour internalizes more easily than deep domain knowledge, the method depends on a capable harnessing model, and training uses a hosted LoRA recipe on a 9B student; the paper text carries no first-party code or artifact link.
   - anchor `§4.3 Obs.3` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "Procedural harness behavior is easier to internalize than deep domain knowledge."
   - anchor `§5 Limitations` @ arXiv:2609.24974v1: "The method depends on a capable harnessing model."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-FLG-09: Operator-flag source: Co-evolution cluster Co-Harness 2607.22688, HASE 2607.03935, WHALE 2609.00196 (recommend monitor under the weights-lane trigger) -> M16. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Read Harness-Zero as evidence about WHERE harness gains can live (weights vs external scaffolding), not as input to the HIL-4 A/B/C loop-architecture decision, which it does not touch. Portable mechanisms: (a) a reviewer wrapping a worker's response boundary and issuing minimal corrections, whose benefit collapses when the reviewer is weak (-12 points with a 35B-A3B model), and (b) the finding that a teacher's task success rate does not predict the training value of its trajectories (62% collection success -> student 3%). Do NOT cite the 81.1 vs 78.1 or the 44.3 vs 41.7 comparisons as established: single run, 100-task sets, uncontrolled compute. No first-party code is linked, and the training path (hosted LoRA recipe) is not part of the EPYC stack.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
