## intake-2171#record — Safety Under Scaffolding: How Evaluation Conditions Shape Measured Safety

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10044
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture, safety)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2154#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Design: six frontier models (February 2026 API versions) x four safety benchmarks (BBQ, TruthfulQA, XSTest/OR-Bench, sycophancy) x four configurations (direct, ReAct, multi-agent critic, map-reduce), pre-registered on OSF; 62,808 records collected and 60,112 analysed (v4; the v3 abstract says 62,808 scored evaluations). Single author; the evaluation pipeline was built mainly with Claude Opus 4.6, which is also one of the tested models; independent replication with a different builder model is requested by the author.
   - *narrowed*: N is 60,112 analysed of 62,808 collected in v4; wave-1 cited 62,808 (v3 wording).
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "We pre-registered our experiment on OSF and conducted 60,112 scored evaluations."
   - anchor `§1 A structural limitation up front` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "Claude Opus 4.6 was the primary model used to build the evaluation pipeline"
   - anchor `§1 A structural limitation up front` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "it is important for our findings to be replicated by an independent team with a pipeline developed using a different model."
1. Pooled scaffold effect: ReAct (-0.8pp) and multi-agent (-0.8pp) are TOST-equivalent within a 2pp margin; map-reduce is -7.3pp (OR 0.64, NNH 14); scaffold architecture explains 0.5% of outcome variance vs 15.1% for benchmark. Scope: 'reasonable default' scaffolds, not optimised; the ReAct arm has only three local tools and 'is more similar to a prompted chain-of-thought'; proxy safety properties (bias, truthfulness, sycophancy, over-refusal) on MC-format items, not capability.
   - *narrowed*: Small pooled scaffold effect holds for proxy safety MC benchmarks under default content-preserving scaffolds; it is not evidence about capability scaffolds.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "Benchmark choice explains 15.1% of the variation in outcomes; scaffold architecture explains 0.5%"
   - anchor `§4.2 H1c` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "map-reduce delegation protocol decreases the odds of a safe response by 36%"
   - anchor `§4.3 Equivalence Testing` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "Only the map-reduce scaffold has a practically significant negative effect on safety."
   - anchor `§7.4 Limitations (Benchmark format scope)` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "the ReAct tested is more similar to a prompted chain-of-thought than a production ReAct with external retrieval."
2. Format effect: multiple-choice vs open-ended administration of the same items changes measured safety by about 5-20pp, but the two formats are scored differently (answer extraction vs LLM judge) so the gap is a measurement result, and the N=4,400 format experiment is explicitly pre-data exploratory on 220 matched items with 10-70 observations per cell; sycophancy shows the largest gap (+19.6pp) while the MMLU capability control has the opposite sign (-9.2pp).
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "The two formats are scored with different methods (answer extraction and an LLM judge), so the gap is due to measurement rather than differences in latent safety."
   - anchor `§1 What we did` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "but this experiment is still pre-data exploratory (rather than ab initio confirmatory)"
   - anchor `§5.1 Table 5` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "Sycophancy: the largest pooled format contrast."
   - anchor `§5.1 AI factual recall / MMLU` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "The factual-recall capability control (MMLU) has the opposite sign in the aggregate data"
3. Map-reduce degradation is partly an option-propagation artifact: answer-option markers reach 0/150, 3/148 and 5/125 worker inputs, and restoring them recovers 40-91% of the map-reduce loss for four of five models and none of Llama 4's 7.5pp.
   - anchor `§5.2` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "Restoring the options recovers 40–91% of the map-reduce loss for Opus, DeepSeek, Mistral, and GPT-5.2, and none of Llama 4’s 7.5 pp"
   - anchor `§4.6 Dose-Response` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "while in the map-worker prompts they appear in 0/150, 3/148, and 5/125 cases"
4. Scoring-rule sensitivity: scoring with a keyword-based refusal classifier instead of the pre-registered LLM judge would have created, reversed or overstated four of five exploratory findings.
   - anchor `§5.3` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "would create, reverse, or overstate four findings (Table 15)."
5. Per-model heterogeneity: on identical sycophancy items under map-reduce Opus 4.6 falls 16.8pp while Llama 4 gains 18.8pp (a 35.6pp opposite-sign spread); composite reliability G=0.251 (95% CI 0.000-0.879).
   - anchor `§4.4` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "Opus 4.6’s score falls by 16.8 pp but Llama 4’s score gains 18.8 pp on the same set of sycophancy items."
6. Reporting practice proposed: TOST equivalence with a pre-registered margin (2pp) distinguishes 'no evidence of harm' from 'evidence of no harm', and paired intention-to-treat (parse failures count as failures) and per-protocol scoring show how much a result depends on parse-failure handling.
   - anchor `§8` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "both intention-to-treat and per-protocol scoring. The former would count all parsing failures as unsafe responses, and the latter would exclude them"
   - anchor `§3.7` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "We conduct TOST equivalence testing for all primary comparisons, using a margin of"
7. Builder-as-subject sensitivity: excluding Opus 4.6 (the pipeline builder) leaves the map-reduce conclusion intact (-5.6pp, Holm p<1e-25) but ReAct and multi-agent lose statistical significance.
   - *unaffected*: Opus-exclusion sensitivity re-read in section 3.8/7.5, Table 17.
   - anchor `§4.1 Protocol Deviations (paragraph after Table 2)` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "The qualitative conclusion about map-reduce was supported in this analysis"
8. Prompt-slot interference: a restrictive benchmark system prompt passed as the custom system prompt suppressed the Recursive Language Model scaffold's behaviour in 10/10 GPT-5.2 test cases, so safety/format instructions and scaffold instructions compete for the single system-prompt slot.
   - anchor `§3.3 System prompt placement` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "passing a restrictive benchmark system prompt as the custom system prompt for RLM suppressed the functionality of the RLM scaffold in 10/10 cases tested for GPT-5.2"
9. Version drift: the same preprint's headline wording moved between versions. v2 describes multi-agent as non-significant and TOST-equivalent while v4 reports it as significant after Holm correction (still TOST-equivalent); v3's abstract says 62,808 scored evaluations while v4 says 60,112 scored of 62,808 collected. Any citation must pin the version.
   - anchor `v2 Abstract` @ arXiv:2603.10044v2: "multi-agent is non-significant and TOST-equivalent"
   - anchor `v3 Abstract` @ arXiv:2603.10044v3: "conducted 62,808 scored evaluations"
   - anchor `v4 §4.2 H1b` @ arXiv:2603.10044v4: "Multi-agent scaffolding has a small, statistically significant effect"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-18: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: arXiv 2604.07709 IatroBench; 2311.05915 Fake Alignment; 2605.04135 Frontier Lag -> decline: different domain, restatement, or already declined. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Use it for MEASUREMENT HYGIENE, not as evidence that scaffold effects are small. Its small pooled scaffold effect (0.5% of variance) is for proxy safety benchmarks under non-optimised, content-preserving scaffolds on six frontier APIs; the one scaffold that destroys content (map-reduce) moves scores by 7.3pp, and the format/scoring choice moves them by 5-20pp. Portable: equivalence testing with a pre-registered margin, paired ITT and per-protocol scoring, a propagation audit of what reaches delegated sub-calls, and distrust of any keyword scorer. Single author, pipeline built by a tested model, no replication. Pin the version when citing (v4 read).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/delegation-context-preassembly.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/episodic-memory-integrity.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/eval-tower-verification.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): SU1: Read MEASUREMENT.md and MEASUREMENT_POLICY.md for an existing equivalence-within-margin claim form; if none, draft a PROPOSAL (not an edit; human amendment) for a no-regression claim that states the margin before the run and passes only if the CI lies inside it.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): SU2: Add to the eval-reporting spec (design-doc edit): every headline delta is reported under both ITT and per-protocol parse handling, and a stored-output re-score with a second independent scorer is run before a scorer-dependent delta is acted on. Zero inference when stored outputs exist.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): SU3: Write a zero-inference propagation audit over recorded orchestrator delegation traces: for each delegated sub-call, check whether the parent task format constraints, option lists, ids and required output schema appear in the worker input, and report the carried share per role. Run on existing journals only; no new inference.

## intake-2172#record — AgentSwing: Adaptive Parallel Context Management Routing for Long-Horizon Web Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27490
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, context_management)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2157#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. kc1: A router that, at each context-budget trigger, builds Keep-Last-N / Summary / Discard-All branches from the same raw context, runs each K=3 lookahead turns against the real environment, then lets the agent model pick, beats every static strategy on BrowseComp for three backbones (60.0 vs 52.5 best static GPT-OSS-120B; 62.5 vs 58.0 DeepSeek-v3.2; 60.5 vs 58.0 Tongyi-DR-30B-A3B).
1. kc2: Success decomposes as P(finish) x P(correct | finish) (eta x rho); the no-management baseline has highest eta but lowest rho, Discard-All the reverse, Keep-Last-N and Summary in between.
2. kc3: Aligned terminal precision falls as the context budget grows (context rot), so the trigger fraction r matters (0.2 or 0.4 of a 128k window).
3. kc4: Context management gives little gain, and some static strategies lose to baseline, at small turn budgets; gains appear only when budget is large.
4. kc5: Random routing (51.0 / 56.5) and routing without lookahead (50.0 / 57.0) are no better than the best static strategies; the lookahead is the value.
5. kc6: Preferred routing transitions are backbone-dependent.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20: second reader flagged anchor c8 (quote truncated mid-sentence before the word underperform); replaced by the continuation sentence verified verbatim in the source. Other 13 anchors passed unchanged. 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-13: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: IterResearch (ICLR 2026, openreview qQ5MZ5Mx7p) -> M7; HC-UNR-14: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: arXiv 2510.24698 ParallelMuse -> M7; HC-UNR-15: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: arXiv 2512.02556 model reports (DeepSeek-V3.2, Kimi K2.5, GLM-5), Chroma Context Rot, RULER -> decline: not regime evidence for the per-role question, or known background. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Use AgentSwing for (a) the eta/rho failure decomposition and (b) the finding that routing without lookahead is no better than a good static strategy. Do NOT treat its BrowseComp numbers as evidence for any EPYC role: web search, 128k windows, H100-class open models, n=200, one run, strategies that reset the trajectory rather than mask tool output. It is not a replication or a refutation of the regime map in intake MASK.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/tool-output-compression.md`

## intake-2173#record — PACE: Anytime-Valid Acceptance Tests for Self-Evolving Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08106
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: harness_optimization, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2164#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The ubiquitous "keep it if the dev score went up" accept rule, applied hundreds of times to one reused noisy dev set, is uncontrolled adaptive multiple testing and accumulates false commits.
   - anchor `§1 Introduction` @ arXiv:2606.08106v1: "Statistically, this is adaptive multiple testing on a reused validation signal."
1. PACE gates each candidate with a paired (McNemar discordant-pair) testing-by-betting e-process, committing only when wealth E >= 1/alpha; this bounds that candidate's false-commit probability at alpha under optional stopping (alpha=0.05, fixed lambda=0.5).
   - anchor `§4 Testing by betting` @ arXiv:2606.08106v1: "which controls the false-commit probability at level α at any stopping time"
2. The guarantee is PER CANDIDATE, explicitly not a run-level familywise bound; loop-level adaptivity is treated as empirical, not theorem.
   - anchor `§4 Guarantee` @ arXiv:2606.08106v1: "This is not a run-level familywise bound: across many tested non-improving candidates the expected number of false commits grows at most as α per candidate"
3. Controlled regime (Qwen2.5 1.5B/3B, GSM8K, n_dev=40, 5 seeds): greedy commits 3-3.4 changes/run, 30-42% false and 10-33% harmful (audit-labelled); PACE commits only the planted real gain (0/5 audit-labelled false commits).
   - anchor `§5.1 Controlled regime` @ arXiv:2606.08106v1: "greedy commits 3 – 3.4 changes per run, 30 – 42 % false and 10 – 33 % harmful"
4. The price is power: a gain too small for the evaluation budget is rejected (milder-handicap run kept about 78% of the gain).
   - anchor `§6 When (not) to use it` @ arXiv:2606.08106v1: "a gain too small for the evaluation budget to resolve is rejected"
5. Evidence is prompt-level only, one model family, own loop; system-level generality is argued from the interface, not shown.
   - anchor `§1 Introduction (scope)` @ arXiv:2606.08106v1: "We demonstrate it only on prompt evolution, however, and so treat system-level generality as argued, not shown."
6. Fixed multiplicity corrections (Bonferroni/alpha-spending) and a decaying-memory online-FDR variant were found too conservative or to lose the genuine gain in this testbed.
   - anchor `§3 Why the obvious fixes fall short` @ arXiv:2606.08106v1: "spends its budget so fast that genuine gains are missed"
   - anchor `§4 Why so simple` @ arXiv:2606.08106v1: "a decaying-memory FDR variant was so cautious that it sometimes missed the genuine improvement entirely"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: ingested and dived in one pass; read FULL from disk; no prior entry to correct.

### Verdict

Cite PACE for the diagnosis (greedy keep/revert = uncontrolled adaptive testing) and the paired-discordant e-process form for HIL A/B arms. Do NOT cite it as a familywise or run-level guarantee (the paper disclaims that), as evidence of power at EPYC sample sizes (fixed lambda=0.5 needs q>0.631), or as a new capability for AutoPilot (seq-v1 already has an e-process gate).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`

## intake-2174#record — SGM: A Statistical Godel Machine for Risk-Controlled Recursive Self-Modification

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10232
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: harness_optimization, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2164#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Trajectory-level false-commit control (Theorem 1): if each opened confirmation attempt has a conditionally valid certificate and the predictable alpha allocation is pathwise summable (sum alpha_k <= delta), then P(any non-improving commitment) <= delta; no independence between attempts is needed.
   - anchor `§3.2 Theorem 1 premise` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "If Equation ( 1 ) holds for every opened confirmation and the predictable allocation is pathwise summable with total at most δ"
   - anchor `§3.2 Theorem 1 proof` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "Independence between different attempts is not required; conditional validity within each selected attempt is required."
1. Protocol discipline: the index k counts every OPENED confirmation attempt including failed ones; alpha_k is durably reserved before observations; a restart must not silently reset the index; budget must not be restarted without a separate argument.
   - anchor `§3.1 State, utility, and confirmation history` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "The index counts opened confirmation attempts, including failed attempts, rather than accepted changes or outer proposal rounds."
   - anchor `§4 Executable protocol, after step 5` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "A restart must not silently reset the test index."
   - anchor `§6 A boundary of repeated fresh-evidence certification` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "restarting the risk budget without a separate argument does not preserve the original lifetime guarantee."
2. Valid schedules: alpha_k = delta/[k(k+1)] for an unbounded sequence, delta/(k*H_K) for a declared maximum K.
   - anchor `§4 Executable protocol` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "For an unbounded sequence, α k = δ / [ k ⁡ ( k + 1 ) ] is one valid choice."
3. Power cost is real: in the synthetic IID +/-1 model, at gain 0.10 power falls from 28.738% at alpha=.05 to 4.639% at alpha_10=.05/110; Proposition 1 shows expected total acceptances are finite under bounded likelihood ratios (alpha-death), which the paper cites to Feng et al. 2021 and does not claim as new.
   - anchor `§5.2 Conditional power diagnostic` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "At gain g = 0.10 , the original test has power 28.738% at α = .05 , but only 4.639% at α 10 = .05 / 110"
   - anchor `§6 A boundary of repeated fresh-evidence certification` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "Proposition 1 shows that the expected total number of acceptances is finite, hence infinitely many acceptances have probability zero."
   - anchor `§2 Earlier statistical approval of model modifications` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "Neither statistical approval of persistent updates nor alpha-death is claimed as new here."
4. Empirically (numerical workload, tolerance 1e-3, 2000 trajectories) screening-only adoption has 76.50% any-false-commit trajectories vs 0.00% for SGM; but per-test confirmation at 0.05 also had 0.00% at slightly higher utility and lower cost.
   - anchor `§5.1 Controlled numerical configuration updates` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "Per-test confirmation also has zero observed errors and slightly higher final utility at lower cost."
5. In the four synthetic settings SGM has lower final and cumulative utility and higher verification cost than per-test confirmation, and per-test FWER stayed below 5%; the paper explicitly claims no utility benefit.
   - anchor `§5.2 Sensitivity to path dependence` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "However, SGM has lower final and cumulative utility and greater verification cost in all four comparisons."
6. The evaluator, confirmation data, certificate and persistent budget accounting form a protected verification layer that candidate changes cannot rewrite.
   - anchor `§4 Protected verification layer` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "The evaluator, confirmation-data access, certificate implementation, and persistent budget accounting form a protected verification layer: candidate changes cannot bypass or rewrite them."
7. Long-horizon error calibration is NOT established; the learning demonstration is two attempts on one ImageNet-100 run.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2510.10232v2: "This short finite-pool demonstration establishes executable adoption, not long-horizon error calibration"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: ingested and dived in one pass; read FULL from disk; no prior entry to correct. Anchor claim_index values 8 and 9 (out of range) re-indexed to 1 and 3 after second-reader review; re-index not re-read by Haiku.

### Verdict

Cite SGM for (a) the trajectory-level union-bound structure with a predictable summable allocation and (b) the ledger discipline (count opened attempts incl. failed, reserve before observing, never reset on restart). Do NOT cite it as showing a better keep/revert gate than per-test confirmation (its own experiments say otherwise), and do NOT adopt the delta/[k(k+1)] schedule for AutoPilot fingerprints without a power check, because it is unreachable at k>=10 for our effect sizes.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autopilot-sequential-allocation.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2175#record — On The Statistical Limits of Self-Improving Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04399
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance low; categories: harness_optimization, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: declined
- **Expanded from**: intake-2164#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Under i.i.d. assumptions, distribution-free PAC learnability is preserved under self-modification iff the policy-reachable family stays uniformly capacity-bounded.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2510.04399v3: "distribution-free PAC learnability is preserved if and only if the policy-reachable family remains uniformly capacity-bounded."
1. Two-Gate guardrail (validation improvement by margin 2*eps_V+tau plus a capacity cap K(m)) gives per-edit true-risk decrease and a VC-rate oracle inequality whose failure probability does not depend on the number of accepted edits, because the bound is uniform over a fixed capped family.
   - anchor `§5 Theorem 2 Probability bookkeeping` @ arXiv:2510.04399v3: "does not depend on the number of accepted edits, since the bound is uniform over the fixed capped family."
2. The same validation set may be reused adaptively across many edits only if the capped family, K(m), eps_V and tau are fixed before seeing V; otherwise a fresh split or reusable holdout is needed.
   - anchor `§5 Theorem 2 Validation reuse` @ arXiv:2510.04399v3: "The same validation set V may be reused adaptively across many edits provided the capped reference family"
3. The theorems are stated for i.i.d. fixed-distribution data; online, adaptive or shifting data need additional concentration tools.
   - anchor `§10 Limitations: Nonstationary, adaptive, and interactive data` @ arXiv:2510.04399v3: "the same invariant must be paired with concentration tools suited to the data-generating process."
4. Two-Gate is only as reliable as the computable capacity proxy it uses, which the paper leaves as an open practical problem.
   - anchor `§10 Limitations: Capacity proxies` @ arXiv:2510.04399v3: "Two-Gate is only as reliable as the capacity proxy used in its capacity gate."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-2b: ingested and dived in one pass; read FULL from disk; no prior entry to correct.

### Verdict

Cite only for the structural point: fix the candidate family and gate thresholds before looking at the validation set. Do NOT cite it as a usable gate for prompt-sized edits (the VC margin is vacuous at EPYC sample sizes, which is a dive inference not stated by the paper), and it does not answer the repeated-adoption error-budget question.

## intake-2176#record — HarnessBank: Semantic Gene-Bank Search with Gated Verification for Agent-Harness Self-Evolution

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13683
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2166#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Gated Harness Screening evaluates each candidate on a sampled training subset through four sequential gates: validity (infrastructure failures such as sandbox crashes or verifier timeouts are repaired and re-run, not charged to the agent), activation (the candidate's patch declares an activation spec and emits a deterministic beacon; a candidate whose beacon never fires is inert), paired significance (task-level paired difference, z >= 1.96, K=3 attempts) and gain over the parent; only survivors get a full training-set evaluation.
   - *unaffected*: Gate definitions confirmed in §3.3 (Eq. 10-15).
   - anchor `§3.3 Validity and activation gates` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "Infrastructure failures, such as sandbox crashes or verifier timeouts, trigger a predefined repair-and-retry procedure rather than being immediately counted as agent failures."
   - anchor `§3.3 Validity and activation gates` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "An evolved but never-triggered harness is treated as inert."
   - anchor `§3.3 Significance gate` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "the paired significance gate removes between-task difficulty variance and makes the decision independently auditable"
1. Reported gains are measured on sealed test splits with the train-selected winner scored once: with a frozen Qwen3.6-27B task agent and Claude Opus 4.8 as evolver, test Pass@1 rises +9.3 TB2, +13.7 LiveCode, +11.7 Omni-MATH, +13.9 BrowseComp+, +9.2 GDPval, +15.4 AppWorld, each credited at paired 2-sigma, and +5.1 on SWE-bench (n=26 test, not credited); test lift retains 37-148% of the training lift.
   - *narrowed*: Table 1 confirmed; six credited cells plus an uncredited SWE-bench cell; retention is 37-148% and the paper itself says retention is a noisy ratio, not a test.
   - anchor `§3.2 Evolved harness selection` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "We note that the test set is never used in the harness self-evolution."
   - anchor `§4.4 Significance and Generalization` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "Each is a single comparison (the train-selected winner scored once on held-out tasks)"
   - anchor `§4.2 Main results` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "at n=26 even a real +5% effect sits below the bar (z=0.78), so we report it as"
2. In the authors' protocol GEPA is credited on none of the five sealed tests and DGM on one (AppWorld +10.7); DGM's ungated selection picked a 15-task K=1 spike (0.733 regressing to 0.533 on re-evaluation) and shipped a harness worse than vanilla on Omni-MATH (-1.1).
   - *narrowed*: Contrast confirmed in §4.3 but confounded: baselines use the 27B as proposer while HarnessBank uses Opus 4.8 as evolver.
   - anchor `§4.3 Comparison with Existing Methods` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "an ungated loop can deploy a regression"
   - anchor `§4.3 Comparison with Existing Methods` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "same frozen Qwen3.6-27B as task agent and proposer"
   - anchor `§4.1 Experimental Setup` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "Claude Opus 4.8 serves as the evolver"
3. Dropping the paired-2-sigma rule on TB2 gives test +-0.0 vs 45.4, two false elites (one inert, its beacon never firing) and a search that never stops (>20-round cap), because phantom progress appears in 62-76% of post-convergence rounds under single-run or K=3-mean crediting; the Harness Gene Bank itself (MAP-Elites cells keyed by where x why) has NO ablation row.
   - *narrowed*: Table 3 confirmed on TB2 only; the gene bank has no ablation row, only qualitative 'signs'.
   - anchor `§4.7 Ablation Study` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "phantom progress appears in 62–76% of rounds under single-run or K=3-mean crediting"
   - anchor `§4.7 Ablation Study` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "Two signs the archive earns its keep."
4. Harnesses are model-specific, not universal: the credited recurring mechanisms are selective recovery from 'thinking-runaway' empty turns (disable thinking only after a runaway) and a verify-finalize self-check; the same lever turned the wrong way costs -15.7 on an evolved 397B harness for Omni-MATH; the LLM-assigned 'why' label is only a search steer, credit comes solely from the gate.
   - *unaffected*: Table 2 and §4.6 text confirmed; 3 models and 2 domains, so 'matching law' is an observation, not a law.
   - anchor `§4.6 Cross-Model Dissociation` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "a credited harness is a correction fitted to the model, not a universally good setting"
   - anchor `§4.5 Diagnosis and Cost` @ arXiv:2607.13683v2 (30 Jul 2026; v1 under the title 'Self-Evolving Agent Harnesses via Gated Semantic Quality-Diversity' not read): "a wrong label costs at most a rejected candidate, never a bad harness"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 id hygiene (wave-3 systems dive): HSI's 'GSME' reference and the wave-2 'earlier title' for HarnessBank are one paper, 2607.13683; ingested once under the v2 title. 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-22: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: arXiv 2607.13104 self-improvements in agentic systems survey; arXiv 2603.26499 AIRA_2 -> decline: a survey and an unrelated system. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Cite for the screening protocol (validity, activation beacon, paired z, single-comparison sealed test) and for the phantom-progress stop-rule evidence. Do not cite 'beats GEPA/DGM' (proposer confound) or the gene bank as a proven contributor (no ablation). Not available as code.

## intake-2177#record — HarnessEvolve: Learning from Reference Trajectories for Reliable Agent Self-Evolution

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00829
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2166#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Reference-guided diagnosis: for each training task the execution agent is re-run WITH the ground-truth answer (up to 5 attempts), an evaluation agent verifies the trajectory is a genuine tool-using path rather than restating the answer, and the optimizer compares a failed run to its verified reference to find the FIRST divergence point as root cause; tasks without a reference fall back to single-trajectory analysis.
   - *unaffected*: Mechanism confirmed in §3.2-§3.4 and Algorithm 1.
   - anchor `§3.3 Reference Verification` @ arXiv:2609.00829v1 (1 Sep 2026; only version): "directly outputting the answer without invoking the necessary tools or following the expected workflow"
   - anchor `§3.4 Trajectory Comparison` @ arXiv:2609.00829v1 (1 Sep 2026; only version): "the first action divergence point ti∗ (the earliest step at which the action in τi− deviates from that in τi+)"
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2609.00829v1 (1 Sep 2026; only version): "generating reference trajectories (execution paths produced when given the ground-truth answers)"
1. Errors are clustered by cause (not by severity, no forced cluster count, singleton clusters preserved) and the optimizer targets clusters with more members and higher severity; the pipeline separates execution, evaluation, optimization and gating into independent agents.
   - *unaffected*: Clustering principles confirmed in §3.4.
   - anchor `§3.4 Error Clustering (Long-Tail Protection)` @ arXiv:2609.00829v1 (1 Sep 2026; only version): "preserves single-member clusters, ensuring rare but critical failure patterns are not absorbed into dominant clusters"
2. Two gates: a quality gate (LLM-judge data-leakage score <= 0.8 per edited file; <= 5 newly injected in-context examples) and a performance gate (candidate must not lose on the current batch, delta=0.0, nor degrade any of the last R=2 batches by more than epsilon=0.025; batch size 40), with epoch-end selection of the best snapshot on a held-out validation set and final accuracy on a test set.
   - *narrowed*: Gate structure confirmed, but delta=0.0 accepts ties and epsilon=0.025 equals one task of 40, so it is a weak noise filter; prose 'improves' vs setting delta=0.0 is inconsistent.
   - anchor `§3.5 Quality Gate` @ arXiv:2609.00829v1 (1 Sep 2026; only version): "if the count exceeds the threshold ηblo, the update is rejected"
   - anchor `§4.1 Implementation Details` @ arXiv:2609.00829v1 (1 Sep 2026; only version): "step margin δ=0.0, degradation tolerance ϵ=0.025"
3. Ablation on one in-house dataset (CloudCoreNetwork-QA, Qwen3.6-27B): full 86.9; without reference trajectories 57.8; without error clustering 68.6; without the quality gate 80.1; the no-reference variant falls below every baseline in Table 1 (SkillOpt 61.9, GEPA 65.3, ACE 59.3).
   - *narrowed*: Ablation numbers confirmed; single dataset, single run, proprietary data; the M1 variant landing below all baselines implies most of the headline margin comes from reference trajectories.
   - anchor `§4.2 Ablation Study` @ arXiv:2609.00829v1 (1 Sep 2026; only version): "M1, which removes reference trajectory comparison, yields the largest degradation from 86.9% to 57.8%"
4. Headline: beats GEPA, ACE and SkillOpt on all five benchmarks (e.g. CloudCoreNetwork-QA Qwen 43.4 base -> 86.9 vs best baseline 65.3; SearchQA 86.5->92.9, OfficeQA 62.8->70.9, SpreadsheetBench 44.3->76.4 with DeepSeek-V4-Flash), single reported number per cell, two benchmarks proprietary, and the Qwen3.6-27B used on them is post-trained with domain-specific fine-tuning.
   - *narrowed*: Numbers confirmed; the 27B on the in-house sets is domain fine-tuned, two of five benchmarks are proprietary, one number per cell, no test sizes or seeds in the text.
   - anchor `§4.1 Implementation Details` @ arXiv:2609.00829v1 (1 Sep 2026; only version): "Qwen3.6-27B, post-trained with domain-specific fine-tuning"
   - anchor `§4.2 Main Results` @ arXiv:2609.00829v1 (1 Sep 2026; only version): "HarnessEvolve consistently achieves the highest accuracy across all evaluated settings."

### Verdict

Cite for the reference-trajectory diagnosis idea and its ablation, as single-run evidence on proprietary data with a domain-fine-tuned model. Do not cite its gate parameters as a noise-robust acceptance rule (HarnessBank's paired-2-sigma screen is the stronger design in this wave).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/tool-output-compression.md`

## intake-2178#record — Evo-Harness: Context-to-Harness Skill Compilation for Self-Evolving Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15071
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research, memory_augmented)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2160#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Online harness learning: a frozen solver is steered by an external harness of skill entries (injection budget b); after each task batch the solver reflects ONLY on failed or negatively-fed-back executions into candidate memories (lesson, trigger, evidence, scope_hint) and an evolver applies Add/Merge/Revise/Skip edits, yielding emergent cross-task 'general' skills and per-topic 'topic' skills (batch 16, at most 5 skills per topic and 5 general).
   - *unaffected*: Mechanism confirmed in §3.2/§3.4/App. E-F; batch size 16 and budget 5 from App. F.
   - anchor `§3.2 Execution reflection` @ arXiv:2608.15071v2 (30 Aug 2026; v1 also exists, not read): "the solver produces a candidate memory only when the execution fails or receives negative feedback"
   - anchor `App. F Experimental Details` @ arXiv:2608.15071v2 (30 Aug 2026; v1 also exists, not read): "We set the maximum number of general skills and the maximum number of skills under each task-type topic to 5."
1. With Claude Opus 4.6 as solver and evolver, Evo-Harness beats No-Evolve and five baselines (AWM, Dynamic Cheatsheet, Evo-Memory, ACE, XSkill) on all five benchmarks: CL-Bench 29.54->34.02, TerminalBench-2 62.92->73.03, SWE-bench Lite 63.67->67.00, tau-bench 72.73->76.97, WebArena-Infinity 72.50->76.25; several baselines sit at or below No-Evolve.
   - *narrowed*: Table 1 numbers confirmed, but gains are 3-10 tasks on three of five benchmarks, three runs averaged with no CI printed, baselines re-run by the authors, and no budget-matched task-level baseline.
   - anchor `§4.2 Overall Effectiveness (RQ1)` @ arXiv:2608.15071v2 (30 Aug 2026; v1 also exists, not read): "Several baselines underperform No-Evolve on some benchmarks"
   - anchor `App. I.3 Descriptive Statistics` @ arXiv:2608.15071v2 (30 Aug 2026; v1 also exists, not read): "The performance is averaged over three runs."
   - anchor `Table 1 caption` @ arXiv:2608.15071v2 (30 Aug 2026; v1 also exists, not read): "Evo-Harness achieves the best results across all five benchmarks."
2. Gain scales with solver capability: on CL-Bench Opus 4.7/4.6/4.5 gain +3.7/+4.5/+3.8 but Kimi-K2.5 and GPT-OSS gain only +1.1 and +0.8; with a Sonnet 4.5 solver on SWE-bench Lite the harness is harmful (same-model evolver 55.3, cross-model 55.7, both below No-Evolve 58.0).
   - *unaffected*: Table 2 and Fig. 6 text confirmed; the Sonnet-solver result rests on a single benchmark.
   - anchor `§4.3 Category and Model Generality` @ arXiv:2608.15071v2 (30 Aug 2026; v1 also exists, not read): "while Kimi-K2.5 and GPT-OSS improve by +1.1 and +0.8"
   - anchor `§4.5 Transfer and Adaptability` @ arXiv:2608.15071v2 (30 Aug 2026; v1 also exists, not read): "both below No-Evolve at 58.0"
3. Feedback grounding matters: LLM self-generated success judgements make the harness WORSE than no evolution (CL-Bench 29.54->27.96, SWE-bench Lite 63.67->61.67); environment pass/fail ('Minimal') already recovers most of the gain on SWE-bench Lite (67.33 vs Standard 67.00), while richer diagnostics help on CL-Bench (34.02 vs 29.86).
   - *narrowed*: Table 4 confirmed on two benchmarks only; the 'Minimal is enough' reading holds on SWE-bench Lite alone (CL-Bench Minimal 29.86 ~ No-Evolve 29.54).
   - anchor `§4.6 Feedback Grounding` @ arXiv:2608.15071v2 (30 Aug 2026; v1 also exists, not read): "Self-Generated feedback underperforms No-Evolve on both benchmarks"
   - anchor `§4.6 Feedback Grounding` @ arXiv:2608.15071v2 (30 Aug 2026; v1 also exists, not read): "On SWE-bench Lite, however, Minimal feedback reaches 67.33, slightly above Standard feedback at 67.00."
4. Skills evolved by Sonnet 4.5 on a train split transfer to an Opus 4.7 solver on the test split (68.8 -> 73.4), and updating online during the test stream adds only +1.6 more (75.0); general-only vs topic-only ablations show the useful level is benchmark dependent (CL-Bench topic-only 33.70 vs general-only 30.28; SWE-bench Lite 64.33 vs 66.67).
   - *narrowed*: Fig. 5 numbers are read from text (figure bars not re-measured); the transfer test uses Sonnet 4.5-evolved skills on an Opus 4.7 solver on one benchmark.
   - anchor `§4.5 Transfer and Adaptability` @ arXiv:2608.15071v2 (30 Aug 2026; v1 also exists, not read): "No-Evolve reaches 68.8, Train-Split Transfer reaches 73.4, and Online-Updated reaches 75.0."
   - anchor `§4.4 Harness Content and Components` @ arXiv:2608.15071v2 (30 Aug 2026; v1 also exists, not read): "On SWE-bench Lite, General Only reaches 66.67, close to the full result of 67.00, while Topic Only reaches 64.33"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-FLG-12: Operator-flag source: Harness Evolution as Learning 2609.36892 (recommend decline) -> M16; HC-FLG-13: Operator-flag source: EvoAgentBench 2607.05202 (recommend decline) -> M16. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Use for exactly one thing: independent-ish evidence that LLM self-judged outcomes poison harness evolution (Table 4) and that small/open solvers gain little from evolved skill libraries. Do not cite its Table 1 as effect sizes (3-10 task margins, no CI) and do not treat its pattern (failure reflection + merge curator) as new relative to ACE/A-Evolve.

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/tool-output-compression.md`

## intake-2179#record — Hierarchical Self-Improvement: A Framework for Task-Specific Evolvable Agent Harnesses

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08466
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: not_applicable (novelty low, relevance low; categories: agent_architecture, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-2166#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. HSI runs one frozen LLM in three nested scopes (task harness H, evolver that rewrites H, meta-evolver that rewrites the evolver strategy Sigma) under a frozen outer anchor: the meta-evolver's own execution logic is loaded from an immutable template, edits outside an authorized directory are rejected, and tasks reach H through a fixed using_harness(agent, task) seam.
   - *unaffected*: Scope/anchor design confirmed in §3.2 and §A.5; it duplicates mechanisms EPYC already enforces (protected surfaces, fixed seams).
   - anchor `§A.5 Scope Isolation and Editable Boundaries` @ arXiv:2608.08466v1 (9 Aug 2026; only version): "Any modification outside the authorized directory is rejected."
   - anchor `§A.5 Scope Isolation and Editable Boundaries` @ arXiv:2608.08466v1 (9 Aug 2026; only version): "The execution logic of the meta-evolver itself is loaded from an immutable initialization template."
1. On BALROG with DeepSeek-V4-Flash (task execution with thinking off, evolution with thinking on), meta-on lifts % progress over the init harness by +39.3 BabyAI, +33.0 Crafter, +25.0 TextWorld, +15.0 MiniHack, and NLE stays ~0; meta-off is lower on every suite (e.g. TextWorld 46.0 vs 65.0).
   - *narrowed*: Table 1 confirmed, but the arms are in-sample, 3 test repeats, and meta-on vs meta-off differences on three suites are 4-19 points with stds of 1-4 (suggestive, not shown with a test).
   - anchor `§4.2.1 Effect of meta-evolution` @ arXiv:2608.08466v1 (9 Aug 2026; only version): "Removing the meta-evolver reduces performance on every evaluated suite"
2. On held-out BabaIsAI sub-suites the evolved harness reaches 0.98 (BreakStop) and 1.00 (GoTo) test progress from a 20% unseen split, while Make stays at 0.36, and meta-on is indistinguishable from meta-off there (0.98 vs 1.00, 1.00 vs 0.96, 0.36 vs 0.34).
   - *narrowed*: Held-out numbers confirmed; the meta-evolver adds nothing measurable on held-out data, so the 'meta' claim is supported only in-distribution.
   - anchor `§4.2.2 Setup B` @ arXiv:2608.08466v1 (9 Aug 2026; only version): "while the meta-off variant achieves comparable results"
3. The Setup-A gains are in-distribution: dev ratio 1.0 and val ratio 0.00 on TextWorld, BabyAI, Crafter, MiniHack and NLE, so the exported best version is selected on the same task set (fresh environment seeds) it is scored on; only BabaIsAI has a sealed test split.
   - *unaffected*: Dev=1.0/val=0.00 for Setup-A suites read from Table 3; selection on dev reward LCB.
   - anchor `§4.1 Evaluation protocols (In-distribution evolution)` @ arXiv:2608.08466v1 (9 Aug 2026; only version): "The same task set is used during evolution and final evaluation, while each evaluation episode is generated from a newly sampled environment seed."
   - anchor `App. B.1 Per-Suite Experimental Configuration` @ arXiv:2608.08466v1 (9 Aug 2026; only version): "are the fractions of the suite assigned to dev (evolution reward signal) and val (best-version selection)"
4. The paper does NOT implement a validation-margin plus capacity-cap 'Two-Gate' rule: that phrase appears only in Appendix B.2.5 as a summary of Wang et al. (arXiv 2510.04399); HSI's own acceptance is LCB-reward ranking (z=0.5) plus a generalization-first best-version selection stage.
   - *overturned*: Overturns the wave-2 Theory-dive hope that this paper 'supplies an implementable gate': it supplies none.
   - anchor `§B.2.5 On The Statistical Limits of Self-Improving Agents` @ arXiv:2608.08466v1 (9 Aug 2026; only version): "Two-Gate guardrail (validation margin + capacity cap) yields finite-sample safety with standard VC-rate oracle inequalities (Theorem 2)."
   - anchor `§3.3.5 Best-Version Selection` @ arXiv:2608.08466v1 (9 Aug 2026; only version): "Candidate versions are evaluated on validation performance, and the selected harness is exported for held-out evaluation."

### Verdict

Do not cite as a source of a two-gate acceptance rule (it contains none; the rule is Wang et al., arXiv 2510.04399). Do not cite its Setup-A gains as generalization. Its scope-isolation design duplicates guards EPYC already has. Value is the lineage survey in App. B.2 only.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`

## intake-2180#record — SkillLearnBench (repository for arXiv 2604.20087)

- **URL**: https://github.com/cxcscmu/SkillLearnBench
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance low; categories: benchmark_methodology, agent_architecture, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2169#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. The repo is a 20-task, 100-instance Docker benchmark (MIT licence) whose solving agent is Claude Code with Sonnet 4.6 and whose LLM judge is GPT-5-mini; every trial runs in a container.
   - anchor `README.md:L15 @ 726e1f7` @ cxcscmu/SkillLearnBench @ 726e1f79b96b (2026-10-02): "20 skill-dependent tasks · 15 sub-domains · 100 verified instances"
   - anchor `README.md:L28 @ 726e1f7` @ cxcscmu/SkillLearnBench @ 726e1f79b96b (2026-10-02): "Docker is a hard requirement, since every agent trial runs inside a container."
   - anchor `README.md:L87 @ 726e1f7` @ cxcscmu/SkillLearnBench @ 726e1f79b96b (2026-10-02): "The solving agent is powered by Claude Sonnet 4.6, and the LLM-as-judge uses GPT-5-mini."
1. evaluate_skills.py hard-requires ANTHROPIC_API_KEY and fails fast by pinging https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models, and the agent registry installs @anthropic-ai/claude-code, so a local llama.cpp endpoint cannot drive the suite without patching both.
   - anchor `evaluate_skills.py:L62 @ 726e1f7` @ cxcscmu/SkillLearnBench @ 726e1f79b96b (2026-10-02): "The evaluation agent calls the Anthropic API — export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY or put it in .env."
   - anchor `evaluate_skills.py:L66 @ 726e1f7` @ cxcscmu/SkillLearnBench @ 726e1f79b96b (2026-10-02): ""https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models?limit=1","
   - anchor `agents/__init__.py:L44 @ 726e1f7` @ cxcscmu/SkillLearnBench @ 726e1f79b96b (2026-10-02): ""install": "npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code","
2. The committed skills/ tree holds pre-generated skills for four baselines x six generator models plus human_authored: 1,277 SKILL.md files (DIVE count with find), usable as a free read-only corpus.
   - anchor `README.md:L39 @ 726e1f7` @ cxcscmu/SkillLearnBench @ 726e1f79b96b (2026-10-02): "The committed `skills/<method>/` tree holds pre-generated skills for all baselines,"
3. The shipped adoption metric skill_invocation_ratio is the fraction of AVAILABLE skills invoked through Skill tool calls in the trajectory (no LLM), i.e. tool-call adoption, not textual reference.
   - anchor `evaluation/trajectory/metrics/metric_skill_invocation.py:L4-L5 @ 726e1f7` @ cxcscmu/SkillLearnBench @ 726e1f79b96b (2026-10-02): "Fraction of available skills (under skill_generation_results/<config>/<task>/) that were actually invoked (via Skill tool calls) in the trajectory."

### Verdict

Use the repo as (a) a corpus: 1,277 generated and human SKILL.md files for read-only calibration of a literal-constant lint (SL2), and (b) a definition check: its adoption metric counts Skill tool calls among available skills, which does not transfer to prompt-injected skills. Do not plan to run the suite: it requires the Anthropic API for the solver and OpenAI for the judge, Docker, and 1.8 GB of task environments, and a weak local model on the Claude Code harness would not be a valid replication.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/episodic-memory-integrity.md`

## intake-2181#record — SkillGenBench: Benchmarking Skill Generation Pipelines for LLM Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18693
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: agent_architecture, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2169#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Under a fixed executor (MiniMax-2.5) five skill-generation pipelines across six generator backbones do not reliably beat no skill: Code 10.8-14.4% and Doc 21.4-25.0% pass@3 against No Skill 13.8% / 23.4%; best average SkillSeekers 14.4 / 25.0; generated skills can underperform no skill.
   - *narrowed*: Differences of 0.6-1.6 points against No Skill are inside the +-5 point CI the paper itself reports; the safe reading is "no method reliably beats no skill", not "skills hurt".
   - anchor `§4.2 Dynamic Execution Results, para 2` @ arXiv:2605.18693v1 (18 May 2026), HTML sha256 a4dfe7148751dfae7b645fd30bc136ced10026eedc0ca80ef6cc095d76a4435f: "under strict execution-based evaluation, generated skills are not universally beneficial and can in some cases perform worse than no-skill baselines."
   - anchor `§4.2 para 3` @ arXiv:2605.18693v1 (18 May 2026), HTML sha256 a4dfe7148751dfae7b645fd30bc136ced10026eedc0ca80ef6cc095d76a4435f: "Code performance remains low (10.8%–14.4%), while Doc performance is significantly higher (21.4%–25.0%)."
1. Task-level bootstrap 95% CIs (B=2000, n=187) are about +-5 points in every cell, so most pairwise method differences are not statistically distinguishable; no method dominates across backbones.
   - anchor `Appendix A.3` @ arXiv:2605.18693v1 (18 May 2026), HTML sha256 a4dfe7148751dfae7b645fd30bc136ced10026eedc0ca80ef6cc095d76a4435f: "The CI half-width is approximately ±5 percentage points across all cells, indicating that most pairwise method differences are not statistically distinguishable at this benchmark scale."
2. Task-agnostic skill libraries (distilled before tasks are known) are weaker than task-conditioned generation and in some cases below the no-skill baseline (negative transfer); only shown for GLM-5 and Qwen3.6-Plus on repository tasks.
   - anchor `§4.2 around Figure 4` @ arXiv:2605.18693v1 (18 May 2026), HTML sha256 a4dfe7148751dfae7b645fd30bc136ced10026eedc0ca80ef6cc095d76a4435f: "task-agnostic skills often fail to capture the precise constraints required for downstream execution, leading not only to weaker performance than task-conditioned generation, but in some cases even underperforming the no-skill baseline."
3. Static rule-based skill quality (eight diagnostics grouped into six axes) does not track execution success: SkillNet has the best static score (overall 59.1) while SkillSeekers has the best pass@3 with the lowest overall static score (44.6).
   - anchor `§4.3 Static Analysis` @ arXiv:2605.18693v1 (18 May 2026), HTML sha256 a4dfe7148751dfae7b645fd30bc136ced10026eedc0ca80ef6cc095d76a4435f: "structural completeness does not guarantee executability, and dynamic success does not necessarily imply sound structure."
4. Failure taxonomy of completed verifier failures is source-specific: repository tasks fail on runtime/dependency issues (53%) then interface/schema (27%); code-documentation tasks on interface/schema (85%); domain-document tasks on state/rule (44%) and numeric/formula (37%) errors.
   - anchor `§4.4 Error Analysis` @ arXiv:2605.18693v1 (18 May 2026), HTML sha256 a4dfe7148751dfae7b645fd30bc136ced10026eedc0ca80ef6cc095d76a4435f: "Code Repo failures are dominated by runtime or dependency issues (1245, 53%), followed by interface or schema errors (626, 27%) and asset or artifact issues (475, 20%)."
5. Benchmark construction filters: tasks passing at >=20% without the corpus or >=50% with the full corpus are returned for refinement; 678 candidates were manually audited and 187 kept (27.6%). Evaluation prompt forces the executor to invoke the Skill tool at least once, so adoption is 100% by construction.
   - anchor `§3.3 Stage 4` @ arXiv:2605.18693v1 (18 May 2026), HTML sha256 a4dfe7148751dfae7b645fd30bc136ced10026eedc0ca80ef6cc095d76a4435f: "Tasks with a pass rate ≥20% on the corpus-free check or ≥50% on the with-corpus check are returned to Stage 3 for refinement."
   - anchor `Appendix F.3 Prompt 9` @ arXiv:2605.18693v1 (18 May 2026), HTML sha256 a4dfe7148751dfae7b645fd30bc136ced10026eedc0ca80ef6cc095d76a4435f: "IMPORTANT: You MUST use the Skill tool at least once before implementing to load and follow the most relevant skill guidance."
   - anchor `Appendix F.1` @ arXiv:2605.18693v1 (18 May 2026), HTML sha256 a4dfe7148751dfae7b645fd30bc136ced10026eedc0ca80ef6cc095d76a4435f: "we manually inspected 678 candidate tasks produced by the generation pipeline and retained 187 that satisfied all five criteria, corresponding to an acceptance rate of 27.6%."
6. Generation budget saturates: pass rate rises up to about 16K-24K generation tokens and flattens by 32K-64K; plateaus differ by backbone (GPT-5 and GLM-5 near 18-20%, Kimi and MiniMax 10-17%).
   - anchor `Appendix A.2` @ arXiv:2605.18693v1 (18 May 2026), HTML sha256 a4dfe7148751dfae7b645fd30bc136ced10026eedc0ca80ef6cc095d76a4435f: "Across backbones, the pass rate rises steeply up to roughly 16K–24K tokens, then flattens between 32K and 64K, with little additional gain at 96K or 128K."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-16: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: arXiv 2606.06741 OpenSkill -> decline: a competitor comparison with nothing new for the SkillBank readout; HC-UNR-17: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: SkillGenBench, AutoSkill, AgentBreeder and safety-under-scaffolding repositories; SkillLearnBench site; openreview ulrZEJrNXA -> decline: no local-endpoint path or nothing to replicate, or cost without a consumer. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Cite SkillGenBench as independent corroboration that generated skills are about neutral against a no-skill arm under deterministic verifiers, and for protocol hygiene (no-skill arm, task-level bootstrap CI, execution pass beats static quality). Do NOT cite any pairwise method ranking (CIs +-5, n=187) and do NOT read it as a test of whether skills help local models (single closed executor; adoption forced by prompt). The three-way sign disagreement (SkillsBench -1.3, SkillGenBench about 0, SkillLearnBench +20.3) is unresolved: any SkillBank claim needs our own matched arms.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/episodic-memory-integrity.md`

## intake-2182#record — AutoSkill: Experience-Driven Lifelong Learning via Skill Self-Evolution

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01145
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance low; categories: agent_architecture, memory_augmented, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2169#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Method: a training-free loop of five prompt-driven modules (query rewrite, response, skill extraction, management judge, merge) plus an embedding model; skills are tuples (name, description, prompt, triggers, tags, examples, version).
   - anchor `§3.1` @ arXiv:2603.01145v2 (05 Mar 2026), HTML sha256 c5967c1825c7765840c8e7ea0af4bbeb469917e37574bf87d6fba1023b169ec2: "Our method is training-free: no model parameters are updated during deployment."
1. Skill extraction uses only user-side queries, never model responses, and a judge compares the new candidate with its single most similar existing skill to choose add, merge or discard; merge bumps the version.
   - anchor `§3.1` @ arXiv:2603.01145v2 (05 Mar 2026), HTML sha256 c5967c1825c7765840c8e7ea0af4bbeb469917e37574bf87d6fba1023b169ec2: "Importantly, the skill extraction stage only uses user queries rather than model responses, i.e., it learns from {q1,…,qt} but does not use rt as extraction evidence."
   - anchor `§3.4.2` @ arXiv:2603.01145v2 (05 Mar 2026), HTML sha256 c5967c1825c7765840c8e7ea0af4bbeb469917e37574bf87d6fba1023b169ec2: "the judge only needs to compare the current candidate with its most relevant memory neighbor, rather than reasoning over the whole skill bank."
2. Retrieval is hybrid (normalised dense + BM25 weighted sum), top-K above a threshold; when no skill clears the threshold the response model runs without skill context.
   - anchor `§3.3.1` @ arXiv:2603.01145v2 (05 Mar 2026), HTML sha256 c5967c1825c7765840c8e7ea0af4bbeb469917e37574bf87d6fba1023b169ec2: "If no skill satisfies the threshold, the model responds without skill augmentation."
3. Evidence is descriptive only: skill banks extracted from WildChat conversations (over 8 turns) in four language/model subsets (1,858 skills; the English GPT-3.5 subset has 10,243 conversations and 631 skills), tag/category counts, and two case-study skill cards (one at version 0.1.34). No downstream accuracy, ablation, baseline or human evaluation is reported.
   - *narrowed*: The Stage-1 style summary "lifelong learning improves agents" is not supported: the paper reports extraction statistics and case studies only.
   - anchor `§5.2` @ arXiv:2603.01145v2 (05 Mar 2026), HTML sha256 c5967c1825c7765840c8e7ea0af4bbeb469917e37574bf87d6fba1023b169ec2: "The English GPT-3.5 subset is the largest, containing 10,243 conversations, 267,681 total messages, and 631 extracted skills"
   - anchor `§5.2` @ arXiv:2603.01145v2 (05 Mar 2026), HTML sha256 c5967c1825c7765840c8e7ea0af4bbeb469917e37574bf87d6fba1023b169ec2: "These observations provide qualitative evidence that the proposed versioned maintenance mechanism supports continual refinement rather than simple skill duplication."

### Verdict

Cite AutoSkill only as a design reference for SkillBank maintenance (query-only extraction, add/merge/discard against the nearest existing skill, versioned merge, abstain-below-threshold retrieval). It carries NO evidence that any of these improves task performance; never cite it for a benefit, and never cite the version counter (0.1.34) as quality.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/episodic-memory-integrity.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`

## intake-2183#record — AI4AI at Test-Time: Strong-to-Weak Capability Transfer via Harnesses

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12307
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: harness_optimization, agent_architecture, training_distillation)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2170#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Setting: a strong builder model, inside an agentic coding harness, writes an executable scaffold for a fixed weaker target model using only a 5% labelled validation split of four Theory-of-Mind benchmarks (3,900-item hidden test); 72 runs (builders x platforms x 3 repeats).
   - anchor `§4 Experiment design` @ arXiv:2608.12307v1 (12 Aug 2026), HTML sha256 37fc7ffecd147e1d4613a2581fb47243b98dba733eee4854f9e988fedef9c0b2: "This yields in total 72 experiment runs."
   - anchor `§3 Method` @ arXiv:2608.12307v1 (12 Aug 2026), HTML sha256 37fc7ffecd147e1d4613a2581fb47243b98dba733eee4854f9e988fedef9c0b2: "containing 5% of the benchmark examples, and reserve the remaining examples as a hidden test split"
1. Headline (the abstract words 0.49 to 0.91 as an average): best scaffold lifts GPT-5.4-mini from 0.488 to 0.912 macro accuracy; DIVE reading: 0.912 is the single best of 57 runs, and the mean over all scaffolded runs is 0.763 (+0.275). The human-designed UserHarness reaches 0.939 on the same target, above every automated run.
   - *narrowed*: Abstract 0.49 to 0.91 is the best run; the mean is 0.763 and the human harness is 0.939.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2608.12307v1 (12 Aug 2026), HTML sha256 37fc7ffecd147e1d4613a2581fb47243b98dba733eee4854f9e988fedef9c0b2: "nearly doubling average target-model performance from 0.49 to 0.91."
   - anchor `§5.1 Results` @ arXiv:2608.12307v1 (12 Aug 2026), HTML sha256 37fc7ffecd147e1d4613a2581fb47243b98dba733eee4854f9e988fedef9c0b2: "Across all 57 scaffolded GPT-5.4-mini runs, the mean macro-average accuracy is 0.763, corresponding to an uplift of +0.275 over the baseline"
   - anchor `§4 Baselines` @ arXiv:2608.12307v1 (12 Aug 2026), HTML sha256 37fc7ffecd147e1d4613a2581fb47243b98dba733eee4854f9e988fedef9c0b2: "This setting yields a macro-average accuracy of 0.939 for GPT-5.4-mini and 0.941 for Gemini-3.5-flash."
2. Gains come mainly from deterministic offloading and format control, not extra reasoning: accuracy correlates with the share of items answered with no target-model call (r=0.72), several scaffolds answer 99-100% of items by code, BigToM is treated as compilable, and technique prevalence is led by format enforcement (57/57), greedy decoding and benchmark routing.
   - *narrowed*: Mechanism is benchmark-specific code (routing and polarity rules), which is why the authors call BigToM "turned into compilable skills and rules"; it is not evidence of general capability transfer.
   - anchor `§5.9 Results` @ arXiv:2608.12307v1 (12 Aug 2026), HTML sha256 37fc7ffecd147e1d4613a2581fb47243b98dba733eee4854f9e988fedef9c0b2: "runs with higher determinism fractions achieve higher final accuracy (Pearson r=0.72)"
   - anchor `§5.9 Setting` @ arXiv:2608.12307v1 (12 Aug 2026), HTML sha256 37fc7ffecd147e1d4613a2581fb47243b98dba733eee4854f9e988fedef9c0b2: "the share of the 3900 evaluation items answered entirely by code or structured rules"
   - anchor `§7 Benchmark selection` @ arXiv:2608.12307v1 (12 Aug 2026), HTML sha256 37fc7ffecd147e1d4613a2581fb47243b98dba733eee4854f9e988fedef9c0b2: "We do not view such exploitable structure as “cheating.”"
3. Uplift follows target headroom (r=0.75 with 1-baseline); on the stronger Gemini-3.5-flash target every builder regresses on at least one benchmark (9/20 cases) while the weaker GPT-5.4-mini target never regresses (0/20).
   - anchor `§5.6 Results (iv)` @ arXiv:2608.12307v1 (12 Aug 2026), HTML sha256 37fc7ffecd147e1d4613a2581fb47243b98dba733eee4854f9e988fedef9c0b2: "For Gemini-3.5-flash, by contrast, every builder regresses on at least one benchmark (9/20 cases)"
   - anchor `§5.6 Results (i)` @ arXiv:2608.12307v1 (12 Aug 2026), HTML sha256 37fc7ffecd147e1d4613a2581fb47243b98dba733eee4854f9e988fedef9c0b2: "realized uplift is strongly predicted by the target’s available headroom on that benchmark, 1−baseline (Pearson r=0.75)."
4. Builder reasoning effort raises scaffold quality monotonically (Opus-4.7 0.711 low to 0.856 x-high, p=0.013 vs high); validation budget does not (iterations vs final accuracy r=0.17); a 5% validation slice tracks the hidden test closely (r=0.96) because both are random splits of the same benchmarks.
   - anchor `§5.7 Results` @ arXiv:2608.12307v1 (12 Aug 2026), HTML sha256 37fc7ffecd147e1d4613a2581fb47243b98dba733eee4854f9e988fedef9c0b2: "Greater builder reasoning effort consistently improves scaffold quality."
   - anchor `§5.3 Results` @ arXiv:2608.12307v1 (12 Aug 2026), HTML sha256 37fc7ffecd147e1d4613a2581fb47243b98dba733eee4854f9e988fedef9c0b2: "the number of validation iterations is essentially uncorrelated with final full-set accuracy (Pearson r=0.17, right panel)."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-21: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: UserHarness (no id); Code as Agent Harness arXiv 2605.18747; SkillOS and SkillRL (no ids); evoharnessrl.github.io -> decline: unresolvable without recalling identifiers, a survey, or tied to a weights lane. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Cite AI4AI at Test-Time for mechanism and caution only: a strong model writing deterministic, routed, format-enforcing scaffold code for a weak model is the weights-free form of consultant/grunt; its gains scale with the weak target's headroom and invert on a strong target. Never quote 0.49 to 0.91 (best of 57; mean 0.763; human harness 0.939), never cite it as general capability transfer (validation and test are the same four benchmarks and offloading runs bypass the target), and never apply its numbers to local roles (closed GPT/Claude/Gemini models, Theory-of-Mind only).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/delegation-context-preassembly.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/episodic-memory-integrity.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2184#record — EvoHarness-RL: Learning Runtime Harness Coordination for Self-Evolving Agents

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05446
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: harness_optimization, agent_architecture, training_distillation, memory_augmented)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2170#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Design: a policy-facing harness interface with four meta-actions (track, commit, recall, note) over a Belief / Progress / Experience workspace; Experience is a per-category store (cap 80 entries) maintained between rollout batches by an evolver using Add, Update, Archive and Skip.
   - anchor `§2.1 Experience` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "reconciles it with the existing store through Add, Update, Archive, and Skip operations."
   - anchor `Appendix K Harness grounding` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "Each Experience category has a maximum capacity of 80 entries"
1. Headline: inference-time EvoHarness-Base adds about 10.0 points to three frontier models and EvoHarness-RL (Qwen3-8B, SFT then GRPO) beats the strongest open baseline by 8.5 points. DIVE recomputation: the 8.5 uses single-run Table 1 where the WebArena score 26.0 is the best of three seeds (20.0 / 22.0 / 26.0, mean 22.7 +-2.5 on 50 tasks); with the 3-seed means the margin over SkillRL is +7.3 (ALFWorld 95.0 vs 85.0, WebShop 80.5 vs 73.2, WebArena 22.7 vs 18.0).
   - *narrowed*: Headline 8.5 depends on a best-seed WebArena result; 3-seed mean margin over SkillRL is +7.3, and WebArena margin is +4.7 on 50 tasks.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "EvoHarness-Base improves the average success rate of frontier models by 10.0 percentage points, while EvoHarness-RL outperforms the strongest open-source baseline by 8.5 percentage points"
   - anchor `Appendix E` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "The three overall scores are 20.0%, 22.0%, and 26.0%, yielding 22.7±2.5%."
   - anchor `Appendix E` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "it contains 372 training tasks and only 50 evaluation tasks"
   - anchor `Appendix E` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "we run three seeds only for EvoHarness-RL; the comparison baselines reproduce the point estimates from Table 1."
2. Frozen prompt-level memory and skill baselines on the same Qwen3-8B give mixed or negative changes against plain ReAct on average (ExpeL -3.8, MemP -1.8, Dynamic Cheatsheet -5.3, ACE -8.2, ReasoningBank +0.1, SkillOS-base +1.2); ALFWorld and WebShop numbers for ReasoningBank, MemP and SkillOS are transcribed from SkillOS rather than re-run; only trained methods (GRPO +30.8, SkillRL +36.3, EvoHarness-RL +43.9) move the policy.
   - *unaffected*: Stated plainly in the main text and table; baselines partly transcribed.
   - anchor `§3.2 Main Results` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "existing frozen memory and harness baselines on Qwen3-8B show mixed or even negative gains across environments"
   - anchor `Table 1 caption` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "marks methods whose ALFWorld and WebShop results are transcribed from SkillOS"
3. Ablations (ALFWorld, single run): removing SFT initialisation drops 95.0 to 50.0; removing Experience costs 10.7; always-on BPE costs 6.4; removing the diversity or efficiency reward costs 2.1 / 8.6. RL reaches high validation success in fewer GRPO steps (2.2x, 1.6x, 2.9x at 85/90/93%) than SFT+GRPO.
   - anchor `§3.3 Ablation Results` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "Without SFT initialization, the policy struggles to explore the new BPE action space from sparse rewards."
   - anchor `Appendix B.2` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "EvoHarness-RL reaches 85%, 90%, and 93% validation success with 2.2×, 1.6×, and 2.9× speedups in GRPO steps, respectively."
4. Self-consolidation: with Qwen3-8B as its own experience consolidator, ALFWorld seen / unseen success is 94.3 / 88.1 versus 95.0 / 94.8 with a Claude Opus 4.6 consolidator and 86.6 for the SFT-init (unevolved) bank.
   - anchor `Appendix K Model roles` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "This variant reaches 94.3% on seen tasks and 88.1% on unseen tasks, still outperforming all baselines while remaining close to the 95.0% and 94.8% obtained with Opus-based consolidation."
5. Cost and scope: teacher trajectories and the reported consolidator are Claude Opus 4.6; RL used 8 x H200 (ALFWorld, WebShop) and 16 x H200 (WebArena); WebArena evaluation is only 50 tasks (five categories of 5-15) so one task moves a category by 6.7-20 points.
   - anchor `Appendix K Model roles` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "We use Claude Opus 4.6 to collect successful SFT trajectories."
   - anchor `Appendix E` @ arXiv:2608.05446v2 (05 Oct 2026), HTML sha256 472d1a1bbcabbdc9f4b53654a6d17f5b0d6449e93cf91c18d030c09cbb97c61d: "A single changed outcome can therefore move a domain score by 6.7–20 percentage points"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-FLG-11: Operator-flag source: EvoHarness-RL 2608.05446 (earlier decline superseded: dived as intake-2184#record) (no decision needed) -> M16. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Cite EvoHarness-RL for (a) the evidence that frozen prompt-level memory/skill stores do not help a small open policy (mixed to negative against ReAct) while trained coordination does, and (b) the experience-store maintenance design (Add/Update/Archive/Skip with a per-category cap) and the observation that a small model can consolidate its own store in-distribution but loses about 6.7 points on unseen tasks. Do NOT quote 8.5 or 10.0 points (best-seed WebArena, 50 tasks, transcribed baselines), do NOT treat it as evidence that harness internalisation works (that is Harness-Zero's different claim), and do not plan a reproduction: it needs Claude Opus 4.6 teacher data and 8-16 H200s.
