## intake-2185#record — AgentBreeder: Mitigating the AI Safety Risks of Multi-Agent Scaffolds via Self-Improvement

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.00757
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: harness_optimization, agent_architecture, safety)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2171#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Blue mode: 20-generation evolutionary search over multi-agent scaffold code (Claude 3.5 Sonnet meta agent, GPT-4o-mini scaffold core) on capability x safety (SaladData) reports a 110.7% maximum and 79.4% average safety uplift with capability maintained. DIVE recomputation from Table 1: 79.4 is the mean of three RELATIVE uplifts of the combined-objective scaffold over the best seed (Step-Back 40.8): +110.8 (DROP run), +106.9 (MMLU run), +20.6 (GPQA run). One run per capability benchmark, 250 held-out samples, CI about +-5 points.
   - *narrowed*: Headline 79.4% is a mean of three relative uplifts over best seed; the GPQA run gives +20.6%. Recomputed by the dive from Table 1, not stated in the paper.
   - anchor `§5.1 Experiment 1, results paragraph` @ arXiv:2502.00757v3 (25 Jun 2025), HTML sha256 0a9a2aad7799562e3a0eec3ae351d1bb882cf11611a1c221a6d4d3507642a1e8: "We observed a 110.7% maximum increase in performance of our best discovered scaffold on SaladData (Li et al., 2024b) with an average uplift of 79.4% across all three experimental runs."
   - anchor `§5 intro, safety evaluation protocol` @ arXiv:2502.00757v3 (25 Jun 2025), HTML sha256 0a9a2aad7799562e3a0eec3ae351d1bb882cf11611a1c221a6d4d3507642a1e8: "During validation, each system responded to 100 attack-enhanced prompts, and an agent aware of the constitution evaluated the safety of each response."
1. The safety objective was reward-hacked: a scaffold reached 95.2 on the safety benchmark by refusing off-domain questions, detected only because a paired helpfulness benchmark (TruthfulQA) fell 43%.
   - *unaffected*: Reward hacking and the helpfulness canary are stated explicitly in the paper.
   - anchor `§5.1 "Vulnerability to Reward Hacking"` @ arXiv:2502.00757v3 (25 Jun 2025), HTML sha256 0a9a2aad7799562e3a0eec3ae351d1bb882cf11611a1c221a6d4d3507642a1e8: "We find that multi-agent scaffolds are prone to reward-hacking the safety objective"
   - anchor `§5.1 "Vulnerability to Reward Hacking"` @ arXiv:2502.00757v3 (25 Jun 2025), HTML sha256 0a9a2aad7799562e3a0eec3ae351d1bb882cf11611a1c221a6d4d3507642a1e8: "under closer examination, the system shows a significant 43% decrease in performance on our helpfulness benchmark."
2. Red mode (maximise capability and harm, meta agent NOT told to be unsafe): unsafe scaffolds emerged easily (81.6 on inverted SaladData vs best seed 76.8) with capability (DROP F1 67.7) comparable to seeds, so capability-equal scaffolds can hide higher safety vulnerability.
   - anchor `§5.2 "Capability Disguises Safety Vulnerabilities"` @ arXiv:2502.00757v3 (25 Jun 2025), HTML sha256 0a9a2aad7799562e3a0eec3ae351d1bb882cf11611a1c221a6d4d3507642a1e8: "scaffolds may appear just as capable in terms of task performance yet simultaneously exhibit increased safety vulnerabilities."
   - anchor `§5.2 Experiment 2` @ arXiv:2502.00757v3 (25 Jun 2025), HTML sha256 0a9a2aad7799562e3a0eec3ae351d1bb882cf11611a1c221a6d4d3507642a1e8: "the Meta Agent is not prompted to discover unsafe scaffolds, instead these arise via Pareto optimization on capability and harm benchmarks."
3. A second objective acted as a validation signal-to-noise aid: single-objective CapableAgentBreeder gained only marginally over ADAS (e.g. DROP 72.3 vs 72.0), which the authors read as support for multi-objective selection. Ablation is one run per benchmark with overlapping CIs.
   - anchor `§5.3 "Multi-Objective outperforms Single-Objective Optimization"` @ arXiv:2502.00757v3 (25 Jun 2025), HTML sha256 0a9a2aad7799562e3a0eec3ae351d1bb882cf11611a1c221a6d4d3507642a1e8: "This supports our hypothesis that incorporating an additional benchmark may increase the signal-to-noise ratio of scaffold validations each generation."
4. Mutation operators are short hand-written text passages split into capability-enhanced and safety-enhanced lists; blue mode samples both, red and capable modes omit the safety list; selection is random sampling from per-cluster Pareto fronts.
   - anchor `§4.2 Mutation` @ arXiv:2502.00757v3 (25 Jun 2025), HTML sha256 0a9a2aad7799562e3a0eec3ae351d1bb882cf11611a1c221a6d4d3507642a1e8: "There are two types of mutation operators, capability-enhanced and safety-enhanced."
   - anchor `§4.2 Selection` @ arXiv:2502.00757v3 (25 Jun 2025), HTML sha256 0a9a2aad7799562e3a0eec3ae351d1bb882cf11611a1c221a6d4d3507642a1e8: "Selection pressure is applied at each generation by sampling candidate scaffolds at random from the Pareto fronts of each cluster."
5. Scope and cost: limited generations and small populations produced only marginal improvements; safety is scored by an LLM judge aware of the constitution on 100 attack prompts; the blue experiment cost about $600 (about $500 GPT-4o-mini, $100 Claude 3.5 Sonnet).
   - anchor `§6 Limitations` @ arXiv:2502.00757v3 (25 Jun 2025), HTML sha256 0a9a2aad7799562e3a0eec3ae351d1bb882cf11611a1c221a6d4d3507642a1e8: "due to computational costs, we conducted experiments over a limited number of generations and with relatively small population sizes, resulting in only marginal performance improvements."
   - anchor `Appendix E` @ arXiv:2502.00757v3 (25 Jun 2025), HTML sha256 0a9a2aad7799562e3a0eec3ae351d1bb882cf11611a1c221a6d4d3507642a1e8: "The BlueAgentBreeder experiment, comprising one 20-generation run on each of our 3 benchmarks as well as evaluations costs approximately $600"

### Verdict

Cite AgentBreeder for two portable cautions, not for effect sizes: (1) a safety/refusal-type objective is reward-hackable by trivial refusal and needs a paired helpfulness/completion canary in the same run; (2) a capability-equal scaffold is not a safety-equal scaffold, so capability-only gates cannot certify no safety regression. Do NOT cite the 79.4% / 110.7% uplift as evidence that evolutionary search makes scaffolds safer: it is relative to the best seed, one run per mode, overlapping CIs, an LLM judge, and closed frontier models. Do not infer anything about local Qwen/Gemma roles.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`

## intake-2186#record — Self-Evolving Agents with Anytime-Valid Certificates

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00871
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: harness_optimization, autonomous_research, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2174#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. SEA states that guarantees proved for exogenous settings are voided, not negated, when the policy produces its own data/evaluator/components; every gate guarantee it uses is a published result whose survival under the endogenous loop is declared an OPEN CONJECTURE with no new bounds derived.
   - *unaffected*: Paper is explicit that survival under the endogenous loop is unproved; the dive found no overclaim in the text.
   - anchor `Sec 1 (epistemic-status paragraph)` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "Where a classical guarantee is invoked, we state precisely what its source proves and treat its survival under the endogenous loop as an open conjecture, without positing a proof."
   - anchor `Sec 4.4 Alg 4 (Solution)` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "Whether familywise safety survives the endogenous proposal process and the edit-induced distribution shift is open"
1. CTHS: the k-th confirmation spends delta_k = delta_0/(Z k log^2(k+1)), Z~3.39, summing to delta_0 exactly with no horizon; the naive delta_0/(2k log^2(k+1)) over-spends (sum ~1.69 delta_0). Source inconsistency: the printed formula reads log2 (log base 2) which does not reproduce Z or the 1.69 over-spend; second reader flagged it. Dive check: Z=3.39 matches a NATURAL-log reading of the sum (3.32 before the paper tail correction); a log2 reading gives 1.59.
   - anchor `Sec 6 CTHS` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "the obvious choice δ0/(2klog2⁡(k+1)) in fact over-spends (∑k≈1.69δ0), silently breaking validity"
   - anchor `Sec 6 CTHS (Eq. 13 text)` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "with Z evaluated by an analytic tail correction so that ∑kδk=δ0 exactly—familywise validity with no horizon."
2. The gate itself (Alg 4, SGM-CS) was NOT part of the live experimental stack (omitted for wall-clock cost, ~90 vs ~7 min per round) and in isolation accepted zero edits, so the experiments contain no evidence either way on whether the gate stays valid when the agent proposes its own edits.
   - *narrowed*: Abstract/intro frame the gate as admitting every modification; the experiments disable Alg 4, so any statement that the gate was validated in the closed loop is not supported by the reported runs.
   - anchor `Table 1 caption` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "Alg 4 is omitted from the live SWE stack for wall-clock cost (§7)"
   - anchor `Sec 9.1` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "Alg 4’s 36 is not an algorithm effect—its confidence-sequence gate accepted zero edits, so it ran the control configuration"
3. Results: 52-instance SWE-bench Verified subset, one run per cell; full suite 28->34 (Gpt) and 24->28 (Glm 5.2) against a no-op composite control (+5/+4); magnitudes only, no significance; several single-algorithm cells sit within noise of the control.
   - *narrowed*: Single run per cell; Alg 4 control cell scored 36 vs control 29, a 7-instance swing from an identical configuration that exposes the noise scale. Per-algorithm deltas of +2/+3 are inside it.
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "Results are single-run on expensive evaluations; confirming run-to-run variance and adapting the per-task algorithm mix are future work."
   - anchor `Sec 10` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "we report magnitudes rather than significance"
4. In-loop verifier pattern: the model authors reproduction oracles from the issue alone, an oracle is admitted only if it fails on the unpatched base, search climbs the self-authored score, and the held-out grader is called once at the end; the self-oracle is fallible in both directions so patches are reported promising, never resolved.
   - anchor `Sec 5.2 Admission` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "An oracle is kept only if it fails on the unpatched base"
   - anchor `Sec 5.2 Extracting the signal` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "The held-out grader is invoked only once, on the finalized patch, to measure whether Vself was right."
   - anchor `Sec 5.2 Why measurement stays honest` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "it can call a patch promising that the grader rejects, or miss a fix its oracles did not cover"
5. The anytime-valid gate guarantees safety, not progress, and may abstain indefinitely; the paper keeps a shadow-best so gate conservatism never discards a good patch.
   - anchor `Sec 10` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "Alg 4 guarantees safety, not progress."
   - anchor `Sec 4.4` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "By construction the protocol favors safety over progress—it may rationally abstain (nsf) indefinitely."
6. SEA reads SGM as controlling familywise (not FDR) error because an accepted edit is an irreversible commit.
   - anchor `Sec 2 Related Work, paragraph "Anytime-valid inference is the gate"` @ arXiv:2607.00871v1 (1 Jul 2026; only version listed); HTML sha256 a8539fde3e719857b7a311a9eb145bf4877c184d2cf156d582ba69d34c5b380f at /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp/dive-w4a_evalues/2607.00871v1.html: "because an accepted edit is an irreversible commit, controls familywise (not false-discovery) error under a harmonic spending schedule"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 (w4a_evalues): read FULL from disk. Overturned the wave-3 hope that SEA settles endogeneity: it does not (claims 0 and 2). Declined as an EPYC gate: CTHS costs more evidence than the SGM k(k+1) schedule for any k below ~55 (E>=33/391/3898 vs 40/240/2200 at k=1/3/10, delta_0=0.05, natural-log reading), so W3G2 option (c) stays rejected. Figures are images and were not read. 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-06: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: Safe Harness, Cai et al. 2026 (no id verified) -> M11. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Do NOT cite SEA as evidence that anytime-valid gates remain valid when an agent authors its own data or evaluator: the paper itself leaves that as an open conjecture and its live runs disable the SGM-CS gate. Cite it for (a) the held-out-grader firewall around a self-authored verifier and (b) the CTHS formula (check log base). Its benchmark numbers are single-run and are not a basis for any EPYC decision.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autokernel-research-loop.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/autopilot-sequential-allocation.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/harness-improvement-loop.md`

## intake-2187#record — Online multiple testing with e-values

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.06412
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2174#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. e-LOND sets test level alpha_t = alpha * gamma_t * (|R_{t-1}|+1) for a fixed discount sequence with sum gamma_t <= 1, and controls FDR <= alpha at every t under ARBITRARY dependence among e-values (Theorem 1).
   - anchor `Sec 3.2 Theorem 1` @ arXiv:2311.06412v1 (10 Nov 2023; only version listed); HTML sha256 940699ed7bad89ec6eb5bf8f3da764435343cff6ece2a827240b6c2c2f2c2354: "Under arbitrary dependence on e-values (5), FDR⁡(ℛte-LOND)≤α for each t∈ℕ."
   - anchor `Sec 3 preamble` @ arXiv:2311.06412v1 (10 Nov 2023; only version listed); HTML sha256 940699ed7bad89ec6eb5bf8f3da764435343cff6ece2a827240b6c2c2f2c2354: "Let a discount sequence (γt) be a fixed sequence of nonnegative reals that satisfy ∑t=1∞γt≤1"
1. The method consumes any e-value, including a Hoeffding e-process stopped when it crosses 1/alpha_t; the paper own simulations do exactly that with gamma_t = 1/(t(t+1)), the same wealth form as AutoPilot seq-v1.
   - anchor `App. B.2` @ arXiv:2311.06412v1 (10 Nov 2023; only version listed); HTML sha256 940699ed7bad89ec6eb5bf8f3da764435343cff6ece2a827240b6c2c2f2c2354: "The following Hoeffding-based process (Mti)i was shown by Waudby-Smith and Ramdas (2023) to be an e-process for random variables bounded in [ℓ,u] if 𝔼⁡[Xti]=0 for i∈[N]."
   - anchor `App. B` @ arXiv:2311.06412v1 (10 Nov 2023; only version listed); HTML sha256 940699ed7bad89ec6eb5bf8f3da764435343cff6ece2a827240b6c2c2f2c2354: "In all our simulations, we let γt=1/(t⁡(t+1))."
2. The controlled error is FDR (expected fraction of false discoveries among discoveries), motivated for filtering candidates before follow-up (including features pushed to production), not for an irreversible commit.
   - *unaffected*: FDR framing verified in Sec 1; it is the premise for using e-LOND only on screening-type decisions.
   - anchor `Sec 1` @ arXiv:2311.06412v1 (10 Nov 2023; only version listed); HTML sha256 940699ed7bad89ec6eb5bf8f3da764435343cff6ece2a827240b6c2c2f2c2354: "We want to control the false discovery rate (FDR), which is the expectation of the FDP."
   - anchor `Sec 1` @ arXiv:2311.06412v1 (10 Nov 2023; only version listed); HTML sha256 940699ed7bad89ec6eb5bf8f3da764435343cff6ece2a827240b6c2c2f2c2354: "FDR is reasonable metric to control in applications where one wishes to filter candidates that are promising before doing more extensive follow-up studies, e.g., clinical trials for drugs, genome-wide association studies for genetic factors, features for pushing to production, etc."
3. The FDR bound is sharp (instances exist with FDR arbitrarily close to alpha), so no slack is hidden in the guarantee.
   - anchor `Sec 1 (Outline)` @ arXiv:2311.06412v1 (10 Nov 2023; only version listed); HTML sha256 940699ed7bad89ec6eb5bf8f3da764435343cff6ece2a827240b6c2c2f2c2354: "i.e., there exists instances where the true FDR is arbitrarily close to α"
4. Adaptive online variants (LORD family, SAFFRON/ADDIS) are NOT established for e-values in this paper and are left as future work.
   - anchor `Sec 9` @ arXiv:2311.06412v1 (10 Nov 2023; only version listed); HTML sha256 940699ed7bad89ec6eb5bf8f3da764435343cff6ece2a827240b6c2c2f2c2354: "One natural direction is to extend our results to the LORD family of algorithms, which are more powerful"
   - anchor `Sec 9` @ arXiv:2311.06412v1 (10 Nov 2023; only version listed); HTML sha256 940699ed7bad89ec6eb5bf8f3da764435343cff6ece2a827240b6c2c2f2c2354: "Another direction is to explore how e-values can be incorporated with the adaptive online FDR controlling procedures of SAFFRON"
5. In its simulations e-LOND has more power than r-LOND/Ur-LOND (and than LORD* under heavy dependence).
   - anchor `Sec 5.1` @ arXiv:2311.06412v1 (10 Nov 2023; only version listed); HTML sha256 940699ed7bad89ec6eb5bf8f3da764435343cff6ece2a827240b6c2c2f2c2354: "e-LOND is more powerful than both r-LOND and Ur-LOND across the board"
6. DIVE-DERIVED (no anchor): with gamma_t = 1/(t(t+1)) the level never exceeds alpha/(t+1) (since |R|<=t-1), so e-LOND is STRICTER than AutoPilot flat per-fingerprint alpha=0.05 (E>=20) at every t, e.g. t=52 needs E>=55,120 with no discoveries or E>=5,011 with 10. Scratch sim (T=52, 30% non-null, mean-shift 0.15): power flat 0.83 vs e-LOND(a=.05) 0.18 / (a=.20) 0.36-0.45; flat FDR ~0.10. Strong effects (0.3) keep power ~0.99. So e-LOND is NOT a drop-in cure for the exhausted fingerprint budget.
   - *narrowed*: Dive-derived, not a paper claim: it narrows the wave-3 ledger row W3G6, which proposed e-BH/e-LOND as the way out of alpha-death; at the paper default gamma it is stricter than flat, and only helps if discoveries are frequent or gamma is chosen with a heavier head.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 (w4a_evalues): read FULL from disk (proofs in Sec 8 and Appendix C skimmed by heading only). Narrowed wave-3 row W3G6: e-LOND is not the way out of alpha-death by itself (claim 6, derived from the paper formula eq. 11 and gamma choice in App. B, plus a scratch simulation). 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-01: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: 2407.20683 online generalization of e-BH -> M11; HC-UNR-02: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: 2506.01452 e-GAI -> M11; HC-UNR-03: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: 2601.20386 SCORE overshoot refund -> decline: only matters after an e-LOND-family route, which was declined; HC-UNR-04: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: Ramdas et al. 2017 online FDR with decaying memory (no id verified) -> M11; HC-UNR-08: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: Online multiple testing survey (Robertson 2023), Waudby-Smith and Ramdas 2023, ADDIS (Tian and Ramdas 2019), Everson-Stewart and Emerson 2010, Fleming 2008 -> decline: background and subsumed by the dived e-value and bio-creep entries. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

e-LOND is correct and sharp but is an FDR procedure whose levels shrink like gamma_t; it does not by itself rescue a spent flat-alpha budget and, at the paper default gamma, is stricter than flat 0.05 per fingerprint. Do not cite it as the validated remedy for AutoPilot alpha exhaustion; successors (online e-BH) reportedly dominate it at weak signal and have not been read.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/autopilot-sequential-allocation.md`

## intake-2188#record — Approval policies for modifications to Machine Learning-Based Software as a Medical Device: A study of bio-creep

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.12413
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, autonomous_research)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2174#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Bio-creep exists in the approval-of-modifications setting: repeated epsilon-acceptability tests, each against the latest approved model, can approve strictly inferior models.
   - anchor `Sec 4.1` @ arXiv:1912.12413v1 (28 Dec 2019; only version listed; the Biometrics 2021 published version, DOI 10.1111/biom.13379, was NOT read); HTML sha256 56177c968c35703d281988fd863e0d6ad1468fba1237beb96_515_675_603_238f75: "Even if each approved model demonstrates superiority with respect to some endpoints and NI with respect to others, repeated applications of ϵ-acceptability tests can still lead to approval of strictly inferior models."
1. aACP-BAC controls the expected bad-approval count by alpha-investing over a finite window W: wealth is spent when a test family launches and earned back when it leaves the window; W=infinity is alpha-death, and the paper sidesteps it by choosing a finite W.
   - *reattributed*: SGM credits Feng with identifying alpha-wealth depletion; the preprint solves it by a rolling window (finite W), i.e. it gives up the lifetime guarantee for power. Wave-3 W3G6 said Feng "predates" SGM Prop 1; the windowed fix is the part transferable to AutoPilot.
   - anchor `Sec 4.1.1` @ arXiv:1912.12413v1 (28 Dec 2019; only version listed; the Biometrics 2021 published version, DOI 10.1111/biom.13379, was NOT read); HTML sha256 56177c968c35703d281988fd863e0d6ad1468fba1237beb96_515_675_603_238f75: "Alternatively, we can think of aACP-BAC as an alpha-investing procedure (Foster and Stine, 2008) that begins with an alpha-wealth of α, spends it when a family of tests is launched, and earns it back when the family leaves the current window."
   - anchor `Sec 4.1.1` @ arXiv:1912.12413v1 (28 Dec 2019; only version listed; the Biometrics 2021 published version, DOI 10.1111/biom.13379, was NOT read); HTML sha256 56177c968c35703d281988fd863e0d6ad1468fba1237beb96_515_675_603_238f75: "We sidestep the issue of alpha-death by selecting a reasonable value for W."
2. Protection against bio-creep requires acceptability against ALL previously approved models; the Reset policy (compare to last approved only) shows consistent bio-creep in simulation, BAC/BABR do not.
   - anchor `Sec 6.1` @ arXiv:1912.12413v1 (28 Dec 2019; only version listed; the Biometrics 2021 published version, DOI 10.1111/biom.13379, was NOT read); HTML sha256 56177c968c35703d281988fd863e0d6ad1468fba1237beb96_515_675_603_238f75: "Bio-creep occurs consistently when using the aACP-Reset since it only compares against the most recently approved model"
   - anchor `Sec 6.1` @ arXiv:1912.12413v1 (28 Dec 2019; only version listed; the Biometrics 2021 published version, DOI 10.1111/biom.13379, was NOT read); HTML sha256 56177c968c35703d281988fd863e0d6ad1468fba1237beb96_515_675_603_238f75: "aACP-BAC and aACP-BABR properly controlled the occurence of bio-creep since they require modifications to demonstrate acceptability with respect to all previously approved models."
3. aACP-BABR controls a modified bad-approval RATIO (FDR-style) and earns alpha-wealth when a new benchmark (superiority discovery) is made, combining the FDR and reward approaches.
   - anchor `Sec 4.2.1` @ arXiv:1912.12413v1 (28 Dec 2019; only version listed; the Biometrics 2021 published version, DOI 10.1111/biom.13379, was NOT read); HTML sha256 56177c968c35703d281988fd863e0d6ad1468fba1237beb96_515_675_603_238f75: "So, aACP-BABR earns alpha-wealth when new benchmarks are discovered, which unites ideas from FDR-approach and reward-approach."
4. Validity needs roughly constant data distributions / acceptability graph; when the graph changes frequently error control is declared extremely difficult.
   - anchor `Sec 4.3` @ arXiv:1912.12413v1 (28 Dec 2019; only version listed; the Biometrics 2021 published version, DOI 10.1111/biom.13379, was NOT read); HTML sha256 56177c968c35703d281988fd863e0d6ad1468fba1237beb96_515_675_603_238f75: "Controlling error rates in this setting is extremely difficult because previous data is not informative for future time points."
   - anchor `Sec 4.3` @ arXiv:1912.12413v1 (28 Dec 2019; only version listed; the Biometrics 2021 published version, DOI 10.1111/biom.13379, was NOT read); HTML sha256 56177c968c35703d281988fd863e0d6ad1468fba1237beb96_515_675_603_238f75: "we recommend checking that the acceptability graphs reasonably constant before using aACP-BAC and -BABR"
5. Cost of control: the simulated BAC/BABR policies run at alpha=0.2 and W=15 and are less efficient than Blind/Reset when improvements are large; W=infinity is judged overly strict.
   - anchor `Sec 6 (aACP list)` @ arXiv:1912.12413v1 (28 Dec 2019; only version listed; the Biometrics 2021 published version, DOI 10.1111/biom.13379, was NOT read); HTML sha256 56177c968c35703d281988fd863e0d6ad1468fba1237beb96_515_675_603_238f75: "aACP-BAC at level α=0.2 with window W=15"
   - anchor `Sec 4 preamble (before 4.1)` @ arXiv:1912.12413v1 (28 Dec 2019; only version listed; the Biometrics 2021 published version, DOI 10.1111/biom.13379, was NOT read); HTML sha256 56177c968c35703d281988fd863e0d6ad1468fba1237beb96_515_675_603_238f75: "The hyperparameter W must be pre-specified and specifies different trade-offs between error control and speed: W=∞ requires the strongest error rate control, but is overly strict in most cases"

### Dive corrections

Read PARTIAL: Sec 1-6 body read from disk; Abstract tail, Sec 7 Discussion, Appendix (proofs, Algorithm 1/2, simulation details) and reference list not read; published Biometrics version not read. 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-05: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: Biometrics discussions and rejoinder to Feng et al. (DOI 10.1111/biom.13380) -> M11. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Located: arXiv:1912.12413 (title matches the abs page; Crossref confirms Biometrics, DOI 10.1111/biom.13379, online 2020-10-11, issue 77(1) per SGM). Read the arXiv v1 preprint only (Appendix proofs, Algorithms 1-2 and simulation tables not read). Cite it for the windowed-budget idea and the bio-creep reference-set rule, not for its numbers.

## intake-2189#record — AgentLongBench: A Controllable Long Benchmark For Long-Contexts Agents via Environment Rollouts

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.20730
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: benchmark_methodology, context_management, agent_architecture)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2172#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Identifier resolved: arXiv:2601.20730 abs-page title matches the AgentSwing dive title (the wave-3 id was unconfirmed; now confirmed).
   - *unaffected*: Title on abs page matches; wave-3 recommendation to resolve the id first is satisfied.
1. The benchmark offers fixed context-length buckets from 32K to 4M tokens (800 samples per length, 32 question types).
   - anchor `Sec 1 Contributions` @ arXiv:2601.20730 (v1-v3 exist; v3 PDF sha256 8d5c212bed2ff2fa69ef506b3f16562067a80191dcc6e7ebfaf78368080ba94d downloaded but no PDF text extractor available; text read from the ar5iv render, sha256 dc16829db5227eff0103f02a504f738a4d80250f07942975b3e6900039320bae, REVISION NOT STATED ON THE PAGE); repo euReKa025/AgentLongBench @ 57e13e82ba20867fff3392903db2e52cf08bcc05; HF dataset ign1s/AgentLongBench sha 7c32c2520a777ea51e3a8dcf9d94d524523be12f: "covering context lengths from 32K to 4M tokens, with 800 samples in each length"
2. Each sample is a pre-generated static messages list (system/user/assistant/tool) cut at a target point plus a question and a deterministic answer; trajectories are produced by a rule-based SIMULATED agent with behavioural noise parameters, not by the model under test.
   - anchor `App. 9.4 Post-Processing and Task Construction (QA dataset step)` @ arXiv:2601.20730 (v1-v3 exist; v3 PDF sha256 8d5c212bed2ff2fa69ef506b3f16562067a80191dcc6e7ebfaf78368080ba94d downloaded but no PDF text extractor available; text read from the ar5iv render, sha256 dc16829db5227eff0103f02a504f738a4d80250f07942975b3e6900039320bae, REVISION NOT STATED ON THE PAGE); repo euReKa025/AgentLongBench @ 57e13e82ba20867fff3392903db2e52cf08bcc05; HF dataset ign1s/AgentLongBench sha 7c32c2520a777ea51e3a8dcf9d94d524523be12f: "we assemble the full ‘messages‘ list (System, User, Assistant, Tool) up to the target point and attach the corresponding question (e.g., "Find Duplicates") and the deterministic ground-truth answer."
   - anchor `App. 9.3` @ arXiv:2601.20730 (v1-v3 exist; v3 PDF sha256 8d5c212bed2ff2fa69ef506b3f16562067a80191dcc6e7ebfaf78368080ba94d downloaded but no PDF text extractor available; text read from the ar5iv render, sha256 dc16829db5227eff0103f02a504f738a4d80250f07942975b3e6900039320bae, REVISION NOT STATED ON THE PAGE); repo euReKa025/AgentLongBench @ 57e13e82ba20867fff3392903db2e52cf08bcc05; HF dataset ign1s/AgentLongBench sha 7c32c2520a777ea51e3a8dcf9d94d524523be12f: "To ensure the generated trajectories mimic realistic and imperfect agent behaviors rather than optimal searches, we introduce several control parameters into the simulator:"
3. Length is controlled by truncating/bucketing whole rounds of long simulated histories; difficulty is separately governed by ACL (tokens needed to locate and assemble the evidence) and by Concise vs Verbose format, not by length alone.
   - *narrowed*: The wave-3 premise that this is "the missing instrument for separating length from difficulty" holds only for static QA over supplied transcripts; it does not control length inside a running agent loop where a context policy acts.
   - anchor `App. 9.4 Post-Processing and Task Construction (truncation step)` @ arXiv:2601.20730 (v1-v3 exist; v3 PDF sha256 8d5c212bed2ff2fa69ef506b3f16562067a80191dcc6e7ebfaf78368080ba94d downloaded but no PDF text extractor available; text read from the ar5iv render, sha256 dc16829db5227eff0103f02a504f738a4d80250f07942975b3e6900039320bae, REVISION NOT STATED ON THE PAGE); repo euReKa025/AgentLongBench @ 57e13e82ba20867fff3392903db2e52cf08bcc05; HF dataset ign1s/AgentLongBench sha 7c32c2520a777ea51e3a8dcf9d94d524523be12f: "We truncate and bucket these trajectories into fixed context lengths (32K, 64K, …, 4M)."
   - anchor `Sec 4.3 (text after Table 2)` @ arXiv:2601.20730 (v1-v3 exist; v3 PDF sha256 8d5c212bed2ff2fa69ef506b3f16562067a80191dcc6e7ebfaf78368080ba94d downloaded but no PDF text extractor available; text read from the ar5iv render, sha256 dc16829db5227eff0103f02a504f738a4d80250f07942975b3e6900039320bae, REVISION NOT STATED ON THE PAGE); repo euReKa025/AgentLongBench @ 57e13e82ba20867fff3392903db2e52cf08bcc05; HF dataset ign1s/AgentLongBench sha 7c32c2520a777ea51e3a8dcf9d94d524523be12f: "Even with identical total context lengths, tool-response tasks impose a much larger ACL, which increases the difficulty of evidence localization and aggregation and is associated with substantially lower accuracy."
   - anchor `Abstract` @ arXiv:2601.20730 (v1-v3 exist; v3 PDF sha256 8d5c212bed2ff2fa69ef506b3f16562067a80191dcc6e7ebfaf78368080ba94d downloaded but no PDF text extractor available; text read from the ar5iv render, sha256 dc16829db5227eff0103f02a504f738a4d80250f07942975b3e6900039320bae, REVISION NOT STATED ON THE PAGE); repo euReKa025/AgentLongBench @ 57e13e82ba20867fff3392903db2e52cf08bcc05; HF dataset ign1s/AgentLongBench sha 7c32c2520a777ea51e3a8dcf9d94d524523be12f: "this degradation is driven by the minimum number of tokens required to resolve a query"
4. Specialised memory/RAG frameworks did not beat the standalone Qwen3-30B-A3B backbone on this benchmark, which is expected when every history constraint is a premise (lossy compression destroys premises).
   - anchor `Sec 3.2 Main Evaluation Results (memory-augmentation paragraph)` @ arXiv:2601.20730 (v1-v3 exist; v3 PDF sha256 8d5c212bed2ff2fa69ef506b3f16562067a80191dcc6e7ebfaf78368080ba94d downloaded but no PDF text extractor available; text read from the ar5iv render, sha256 dc16829db5227eff0103f02a504f738a4d80250f07942975b3e6900039320bae, REVISION NOT STATED ON THE PAGE); repo euReKa025/AgentLongBench @ 57e13e82ba20867fff3392903db2e52cf08bcc05; HF dataset ign1s/AgentLongBench sha 7c32c2520a777ea51e3a8dcf9d94d524523be12f: "The base model consistently outperforms memory-augmented variants across most context lengths."
5. Released resources are the evaluator code and a data tarball; the trajectory GENERATOR is not in the repo, so length cannot be regenerated or extended by us.
   - anchor `repo README @57e13e8` @ arXiv:2601.20730 (v1-v3 exist; v3 PDF sha256 8d5c212bed2ff2fa69ef506b3f16562067a80191dcc6e7ebfaf78368080ba94d downloaded but no PDF text extractor available; text read from the ar5iv render, sha256 dc16829db5227eff0103f02a504f738a4d80250f07942975b3e6900039320bae, REVISION NOT STATED ON THE PAGE); repo euReKa025/AgentLongBench @ 57e13e82ba20867fff3392903db2e52cf08bcc05; HF dataset ign1s/AgentLongBench sha 7c32c2520a777ea51e3a8dcf9d94d524523be12f: "The dataset is not included in the repo due to size."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 (w4a_evalues): id confirmed. Narrowed the wave-3 expectation (claim 3). Read PARTIAL: Sec 1-6 and Appendix 7-9 text read from the ar5iv extraction (revision unknown, may not equal v3); reference list, Appendix 10 (task taxonomy) / 11 (baselines), heatmap figures and sample-count tables not read; repo cloned (evaluator only, no code run) and file list inspected; dataset not downloaded. 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-07: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: LongBioBench (no id verified) -> M7. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

AgentLongBench is a fixed-bucket static long-transcript QA suite (32K..4M) with a useful difficulty notion (ACL); it does not generate lengths on demand and does not run a policy inside the agent loop. Do not describe it as the controllable-length benchmark for stratifying context-policy A/Bs; use ACL as a stratification covariate and treat the data as an optional offline probe.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/promptforge-mutation-safety-contract.md`
- updated `handoffs/active/tool-output-compression.md`

## intake-2190#record — From Raw Experience to Skill Consumption: A Systematic Study of Model-Generated Agent Skills

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23899
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: agent_architecture, memory_augmented, benchmark_methodology, harness_optimization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2181#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Across 150 extractor-target-domain cells (5 extractors, 6 targets, 5 domains; one injected domain-level skill, 3-run average) model-generated skills improve the target in 75% of cells and degrade it in 25% (negative transfer). DIVE-recomputed from Table 1: 37 of 150 cells below zero, mean +2.29 pp, sd 3.5 pp, range -5.72 to +14.66.
   - *narrowed*: 75/25 split is a count of noisy cell signs with no CI; report as descriptive. Mean +2.29 pp recomputed, not stated by the paper.
   - anchor `Section 4.2 Main Results, para 1` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "Model-generated skills are generally effective, improving downstream performance in 75% of entries. Yet negative transfer remains common: 25% of entries have"
1. Negative-transfer rate is domain-dependent: SpreadsheetBench and SWE-bench-Verified 13%, ALFWorld 47% (DIVE recompute: SEAL-0 33%, BFCL 17%).
   - anchor `Section 4.2 Main Results, para 1` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "SpreadsheetBench and SWE-bench-Verified have the lowest negative rates (13%), whereas ALFWorld is the most fragile domain (47%)."
2. Extraction ability is not task-solving ability: the strongest-baseline target (GPT-5.4) ranked last as extractor on SpreadsheetBench while Gemini-3.1-Flash-Lite ranked first.
   - anchor `Section 4.2 Better executor is not necessarily better extractor` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "on SpreadsheetBench, the lightweight Gemini-3.1-Flash-Lite achieves the highest EE, while GPT-5.4 ranks last despite having the strongest baseline among the targets."
3. Consumer model matters: on ALFWorld GPT-5.4 gains +4.93 TE while Gemini-Flash-Lite, Qwen3.5-35B and Qwen3.5-9B have negative TE. DIVE-recomputed per target (25 cells each): GPT-5.4 +4.82 mean / 1 negative; Qwen3.5-35B +1.25 / 7 negative; Qwen3.5-9B +0.93 / 11 negative. Baseline strength does not predict TE (DIVE Spearman -0.13 over 30 model-domain pairs, descriptive).
   - anchor `Section 4.2 Skill utility is target-dependent` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "On ALFWorld, GPT-5.4 benefits consistently from all five extractors (TE=+4.93), while Gemini-3.1-Flash-Lite, Qwen3.5-35B, and Qwen3.5-9B all have negative TE."
4. Scope of the sign result: the skill is one domain-level skill built from the SAME target model own trajectories, inlined in the system prompt (single-skill protocol); multi-skill tool-call retrieval exists only as a protocol and richer harnesses and large libraries are explicitly left to future work. Adoption (tool-call vs inline) is therefore NOT varied, and task selection is a random 1:1 split with no baseline-difficulty filter.
   - anchor `Appendix A Limitations and future work` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "We consolidate each target’s experience into a single domain-level skill and supply it directly through the system prompt at evaluation time"
   - anchor `Appendix A Limitations and future work` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "scaling to richer agent harnesses (for example, with retrieval, planning, or tool-use scaffolds), and scaling to substantially larger skill libraries containing many fine-grained skills"
   - anchor `Appendix B.4 Multi-skill protocol` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "it first calls list_skills to see names and descriptions, then view_skill for the full body"
5. Skills act by reshaping the default policy, not by new explicit skill calls (SpreadsheetBench trajectories, GPT-5.4 and Qwen3.5-9B).
   - anchor `Section 5.3 Behavioral impact of skill consumption` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "skill consumption reshapes the default policy rather than triggering new explicit skill calls"
6. Content, not format: rewriting the same skill as list/checklist/prose has no detectable effect (all p>0.34) while swapping extractor does for 5/6 targets.
   - anchor `Appendix C Format Normalization (Table 8 text)` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "Format has no detectable effect on any target (all p>0.34, all σ-ratios below 1)."
7. An unguided LLM judge reading two skill texts picks the higher-utility one 46.4% of the time (chance), and 15.8% on pairs with gap >= 5 pp.
   - *unaffected*: 46.4% / 15.8% figures read verbatim from the text on 151 pairs, single judge.
   - anchor `Section 5.2 Textual plausibility does not predict skill utility` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "Without any evaluation criteria, overall LLM selection accuracy is 46.4%, indistinguishable from random."
   - anchor `Section 5.2 Textual plausibility does not predict skill utility` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "On pairs with δ≥5%, the judge picks the higher-Δ skill only 15.8% of the time, a clear inversion of actual utility."
8. A 3-dimension validated rubric (Failure Mechanism Encoding, Actionable Specificity, High-Risk Action Blacklist) inserted as an extractor meta-skill lifts all 9 evaluated cells (+1.55 pp avg) while a generic plausibility rubric lowers the average (-0.59 pp).
   - *narrowed*: +1.55 pp is over 9 cells with one extractor (GPT-5.4-mini), no CI; treat as a hypothesis for teacher prompts, not a validated rubric for another extractor.
   - anchor `Section 6 Meta-skill guided extraction` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "the plausibility rubric hurts average performance"
   - anchor `Section 6 Meta-skill guided extraction` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "while the validated rubric improves all nine cells"
9. Experience-pool composition changes skill quality: all-failure pools are consistently worst; optimal success/failure mix is domain-specific.
   - anchor `Section 5.1 Results` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "all-failure pools consistently perform worst, highlighting successful trajectories as the foundation of skill extraction"
10. The same skill text gives sharply different gains per consumer (+1.8 to +9.5 for the strong-pool skill); a weak-pool skill produced negative transfer on some targets (e.g. -2.0 on GPT-5.4).
   - anchor `Section 5.3 Cross-model skill transfer` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "the strong-pool skill consistently improves every target, whereas the weak-pool skill yields clear negative transfer on some targets"
11. Under alternative agentic harnesses (Claude Code, Codex) on SpreadsheetBench the effect shrinks to a modest positive average (+0.4 pp) with large target variance.
   - anchor `Appendix F Alternative Harness Evaluation` @ arXiv:2605.23899v1 (22 May 2026), HTML sha256 5a2c5ac5b1137cfc90a9cf2bd4c8e64ade97d556f8a5af2e9ce2242a010d34a1: "skill injection yields modest positive gains on average"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-20 Stage-4 (harness-cluster intake): dive-surfaced and operator-flag sources recorded against this entry: HC-UNR-09: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: github.com/microsoft/SkillLens (code) -> M5; HC-UNR-10: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: arXiv 2604.04323 How Well Do Agentic Skills Work in the Wild -> M5; HC-UNR-11: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: arXiv 2603.15401 SWE-Skills-Bench -> M5; HC-UNR-12: Dive-surfaced source left unread at the wave cap: arXiv 2603.00718 SkillCraft -> decline: executable tool-composition skills only, not the prompt-injected skills the orchestrator uses. DP-D recommendations are the defaults; the operator has not yet answered DP-D.

### Verdict

Cite this as a fourth, structurally different datapoint that makes the sign of skill benefit a DISTRIBUTION over (domain, consumer, extractor) cells (mean about +2 pp, a quarter of cells negative, local Qwen consumers worse than GPT-5.4), not as the resolver of the SkillsBench -1.3 / SkillGenBench about 0 / SkillLearnBench +20.3 disagreement. It varies consumer, extractor, domain and pool composition, but NOT task-difficulty selection and NOT injection mode (always system-prompt inline), and cites neither sibling benchmark. Any SkillBank claim still needs our own matched arms, stratified per consumer role.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/episodic-memory-integrity.md`
